\begin{align}
 D\{Z_\lambda(s_h(\lambda)+\cdot)\}
 &=\dot Z(s_h+\cdot)
   -\frac{Z_*'(s_h+\cdot)}{r_*'(s_h)}\xi(s_h),\notag\\
 D\{p_\lambda(s_h(\lambda))\}
 &=\dot p(s_h)
   -\frac{p_*'(s_h)}{r_*'(s_h)}\xi(s_h).          \label{eq:fixed-r-handoff-traces}
\end{align}
Thus a fixed-\(r\) handoff has no separate moving-\(S\) column.  For the
reduced terminal branch, the terminal-relative coordinate
\(\varsigma=s-S\) has reference path
\begin{equation}
 r^\#(\varsigma)=\Phi_{q_0}^{\delta\varsigma}(e_1).             \label{eq:terminal-relative-reduced-path}
\end{equation}
For any chosen buffer length \(L_b>0\), choosing
\(e_h=\Phi_{q_0}^{-\delta L_b}(e_1)\) makes its crossing
\(\varsigma=-L_b\).  At this prescribed reduced base, the
terminal-relative component operator is conjugate by simultaneous
translation to the fixed component operator, so its Green kernels and norm
estimates are unchanged.  In this reference linearization
\(\widehat\tau\) occurs only in the scalar phase--event border.  This
statement does not extend the flushing estimates to a moving nonlinear
family, for which the fixed-\(r\) crossing can move in the
\(\varsigma\)-coordinate.

There is a genuine regularity cost if one instead differentiates the raw
moving trace.  With
\begin{equation}
 \|x\|_{d,\delta}
 =\max_{0\le j\le d}\delta^j
       \|\partial_s^jx\|_\infty,                  \label{eq:moving-trace-strong-norm}
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}
 \|\delta^{-1}x'\|_{d,\delta}
 \le\delta^{-2}\|x\|_{d+1,\delta},                \label{eq:moving-trace-one-order-loss}
\end{equation}
and the power \(\delta^{-2}\) is sharp.  Translation is not in general
Fr\'echet differentiable from the continuous piecewise
\(W^{d,\infty}\) space to itself.  Thus the event-aligned formulation
avoids an otherwise unavoidable extra derivative in the component
flushing argument.

This proposition is exact trace calculus.  It does not construct the
nonlinear family, identify the formal collar with the raw-compatible
collar, estimate the affine-residual column, or prove the full bordered
inverse.
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[Terminal-relative event-aligned trace-scale coordinates]
\label[lemma]{lem:terminal-relative-trace-scale-chart}
Fix \(d\ge0\), \(L\ge0\), and \(\ell>0\), translate the reference terminal
time to zero, and put
\[
 K=[-L-\theta_m,0],\qquad
 K^\ell=[-L-\theta_m-\ell,\ell].
\]
Let
\[
 Y_*=(r_*,Z_*,p_*)\in
 C^{d+2}(K^\ell;\R\times E_N\times\R)
\]
satisfy
\begin{equation}
 r_*(0)=e_1,\qquad r_*'(0)=\delta q_e,\qquad
 |q_e|\ge q_->0.                                  \label{eq:trace-scale-reference-event}
\end{equation}
Assume \(r_*\) has a scalar inverse \(\chi_*\) near \(e_1\), normalized by
\(\chi_*(e_1)=0\).  For
\[
 Y=(r,Z,p)\in C^{d+2}(K^\ell;\R\times E_N\times\R),
 \qquad t_{\widehat\tau}=\widehat\tau/\delta,
\]
restrict to the open neighborhood on which
\[
 |t_{\widehat\tau}|<\ell/2,\qquad
 r(t_{\widehat\tau})\in\mathcal D(\chi_*),\qquad
 |\alpha(Y,\widehat\tau)|<\ell/2,
\]
where
\begin{equation}
 \alpha(Y,\widehat\tau)
 =\chi_*\!\left(r(t_{\widehat\tau})\right).       \label{eq:trace-scale-phase-match}
\end{equation}
Define, for \(\varsigma\in K\),
\begin{align}
 E(Y,\widehat\tau)
 &=r(t_{\widehat\tau})-e_1,\notag\\
 r^\#(\varsigma)
 &=r(t_{\widehat\tau}+\varsigma),\notag\\
 N_Z^\#(\varsigma)
 &=Z(t_{\widehat\tau}+\varsigma)
      -Z_*(\alpha(Y,\widehat\tau)+\varsigma),\notag\\
 N_p^\#(\varsigma)
 &=p(t_{\widehat\tau}+\varsigma)
      -p_*(\alpha(Y,\widehat\tau)+\varsigma).
                                                        \label{eq:trace-scale-chart}
\end{align}
Then
\begin{equation}
 (Y,\widehat\tau)\longmapsto
   (\widehat\tau,r^\#,N_Z^\#,N_p^\#)             \label{eq:trace-scale-map}
\end{equation}
is \(C^2\) from the displayed strong neighborhood to
\[
 \R\times C^d(K;\R)
       \times C^d(K;E_N)\times C^d(K;\R).
\]
In these coordinates the fixed physical-section event row is the bounded
functional
\begin{equation}
 E=r^\#(0)-e_1.                                    \label{eq:trace-scale-event-row}
\end{equation}
It is algebraically lossless on the retained moving window: once
\((\widehat\tau,r^\#,N_Z^\#,N_p^\#)\) is known, put
\(\alpha=\chi_*(r^\#(0))\) and reconstruct, for
\(\varsigma\in K\),
\begin{align}
 r(t_{\widehat\tau}+\varsigma)&=r^\#(\varsigma),\notag\\
 Z(t_{\widehat\tau}+\varsigma)
 &=N_Z^\#(\varsigma)+Z_*(\alpha+\varsigma),\notag\\
 p(t_{\widehat\tau}+\varsigma)
 &=N_p^\#(\varsigma)+p_*(\alpha+\varsigma).
                                                        \label{eq:trace-scale-reconstruction}
\end{align}
This is a strong-to-weak moving-window coordinate package, not a
same-space Banach diffeomorphism or a chart of the unretained part of
\(K^\ell\).

At \((Y_*,0)\), let a direction be
\[
 H_i=(\xi_i,\mathsf z_i,\mathsf p_i,\sigma_i),
 \qquad
 B_i=\xi_i(0)+q_e\sigma_i,\qquad
 A_i=\frac{B_i}{\delta q_e}.                      \label{eq:trace-scale-first-data}
\]
The raw translated terminal traces
\[
 Z_{\rm raw}^\#(\varsigma)=Z(t_{\widehat\tau}+\varsigma),
 \qquad
 p_{\rm raw}^\#(\varsigma)=p(t_{\widehat\tau}+\varsigma)
\]
have derivatives
\begin{align}
 DE[H_i]&=B_i,\notag\\
 DZ_{\rm raw}^\#[H_i]
 &=\mathsf z_i+\delta^{-1}Z_*'\sigma_i,\notag\\
 Dp_{\rm raw}^\#[H_i]
 &=\mathsf p_i+\delta^{-1}p_*'\sigma_i.           \label{eq:trace-scale-raw-first}
\end{align}
Thus the nonzero-base terminal columns are retained.  The event-aligned
coordinates instead satisfy the exact triangularization
\begin{align}
 DN_Z^\#[H_i]
 &=\mathsf z_i-\frac{Z_*'}{\delta q_e}\xi_i(0),
 &DZ_{\rm raw}^\#[H_i]
 &=DN_Z^\#[H_i]+\frac{Z_*'}{\delta q_e}DE[H_i],
 \notag\\
 DN_p^\#[H_i]
 &=\mathsf p_i-\frac{p_*'}{\delta q_e}\xi_i(0),
 &Dp_{\rm raw}^\#[H_i]
 &=DN_p^\#[H_i]+\frac{p_*'}{\delta q_e}DE[H_i].
                                                        \label{eq:trace-scale-first-triangularization}
\end{align}
All unmarked reference functions in these formulas are evaluated at
\(\varsigma\).

For the second derivative, put
\begin{align}
 R_{12}
 &=\delta^{-1}\xi_1'(0)\sigma_2
   +\delta^{-1}\xi_2'(0)\sigma_1
   +\delta^{-2}r_*''(0)\sigma_1\sigma_2,\notag\\
 A_{12}
 &=\frac{R_{12}-r_*''(0)A_1A_2}{\delta q_e}.
                                                        \label{eq:trace-scale-second-data}
\end{align}
The second event derivative is
\begin{equation}
 D^2E[H_1,H_2]=R_{12}.                              \label{eq:trace-scale-event-second}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{align}
 D^2N_Z^\#[H_1,H_2]
={}&\delta^{-1}\{
   \mathsf z_1'\sigma_2+\mathsf z_2'\sigma_1\}
 +\delta^{-2}Z_*''\sigma_1\sigma_2\notag\\
 &{}-Z_*'A_{12}-Z_*''A_1A_2,                     \label{eq:trace-scale-Z-second}\\
 D^2N_p^\#[H_1,H_2]
={}&\delta^{-1}\{
   \mathsf p_1'\sigma_2+\mathsf p_2'\sigma_1\}
 +\delta^{-2}p_*''\sigma_1\sigma_2\notag\\
 &{}-p_*'A_{12}-p_*''A_1A_2.                     \label{eq:trace-scale-p-second}
\end{align}
In particular, the pure \(\widehat\tau^2\) reference column cancels
identically, but the mixed path--event columns do not.

With the scaled strong norm in
\cref{eq:moving-trace-strong-norm}, raw translation obeys
\begin{align}
 \|D_{\widehat\tau}\mathcal T x\|_{d,\delta}
 &\le\delta^{-2}\|x\|_{d+1,\delta},\notag\\
 \|D_{\widehat\tau}^2\mathcal T x\|_{d,\delta}
 &\le\delta^{-4}\|x\|_{d+2,\delta}.              \label{eq:trace-scale-translation-losses}
\end{align}
The first power is sharp by
\cref{prop:event-aligned-normal-traces}; the second follows from the same
rescaling.  The alignment cancels pure reference translations but does not
turn these raw strong-to-weak losses into same-space bounds.

This lemma constructs only \(C^2\) moving-window coordinates for an already
available strong terminal buffer.  It does not produce that buffer, a parameterized RFDE
solution map, a fixed-chart nonlinear residual, a moving-event inverse, a
physical endpoint family, or a connection root.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Endpoint-centered fixed-envelope duration selector]
\label[lemma]{lem:endpoint-centered-duration-selector}
Fix an integer \(d\ge0\), a branch, and a supplied classical reference
\(Y_*=(r_*,Z_*,p_*)\) on the fixed forward envelope
\begin{equation*}
 \widehat J_\delta=[-\theta_m,S_*+\ell_{*,\delta}].
\end{equation*}
For fixed \(L\ge0\) and \(c_T>0\), put
\[
 S(\tau)=S_*+\frac{\tau}{\delta},\qquad
 I_\tau=(-c_T\rho_\delta,c_T\rho_\delta).
\]
Assume that
\begin{equation}
 \bigcup_{\tau\in I_\tau}
 \left(\{S(\tau)\}\cup
 [S(\tau)-L-\theta_m,S(\tau)-L]\right)
 \Subset\widehat J_\delta.                       \label{eq:endpoint-selector-envelope-containment}
\end{equation}
Let \(\mathcal X_{\rm env}\) be a Banach space of trial triples on this
fixed envelope which are piecewise \(W^{1,\infty}\) on the rough part and
continuously embedded in \(C^{d+2}\) on a terminal buffer containing the
compact set in \cref{eq:endpoint-selector-envelope-containment}.  Let
\(\mathcal Y_{\rm up}\) be a Banach product range containing the fixed
residual rows and the retained terminal trace slots, and assume that the
displayed terminal evaluations are bounded from
\(\mathcal X_{\rm env}\) to those slots.  The envelope is fixed with
respect to \(\tau\), not uniformly in \(\delta\); in particular its right
margin is at least \(c_T\rho_\delta/\delta\).  Suppose
\begin{equation}
 r_*(0)=e_0,\qquad r_*(S_*)=e_1,\qquad
 q_{0,e}=q_0(e_1)\ne0,\qquad
 q_{*,e}=\delta^{-1}r_*'(S_*)\ne0.              \label{eq:endpoint-selector-reference-speeds}
\end{equation}

Let \(R_\gamma\) be a raw-compatible entry-collar residual in fixed
\(\theta\)-coordinates, let \(R_{\rm RFDE}\) impose the unmodified RFDE
on the fixed envelope, and retain the seam and causal-entry rows.  Only the
terminal selector is evaluated at \(S(\tau)\).  Assume that
\(\mathcal U^{\rm raw}:\mathcal X_{\rm env}\times I_\tau
\to\mathcal Y_{\rm up}\) and
\(E:\mathcal X_{\rm env}\times I_\tau\to\mathbb R\) are \(C^1\) near
\((Y_*,0)\), and write
\begin{align}
 \mathcal U^{\rm raw}(Y,\tau)
 &=\bigl(R_\gamma(Y),R_{\rm RFDE}(Y),
          B^{n,\rm raw}(Y,\tau),r(0)-e_0\bigr),\notag\\
 E(Y,\tau)&=\frac{r(S(\tau))-e_1}{q_{0,e}},\qquad
 \mathcal F^{\rm raw}=(\mathcal U^{\rm raw},E).             \label{eq:endpoint-selector-residual}
\end{align}
Here the attracting finite normal row is fixed at the entry, while a
repelling terminal row has the form
\(p(S(\tau))-b_r\).  Any retained moving terminal voltage history is
written as
\(Z(S(\tau)-L+\cdot)-h_Z\).  Rows absent from a particular branch are
omitted.

At \((Y_*,0)\), a variation \(v\) has displayed components
\((\xi,\mathsf z,\mathsf p)\), and
\begin{equation}
 D\mathcal F^{\rm raw}(Y_*,0)
 =\begin{pmatrix}L^{\rm raw}&C_{\rm ter}^{\rm raw}\\
                  \ell_{\rm ter}&c_{\rm ter}\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 \ell_{\rm ter}v=\frac{\xi(S_*)}{q_{0,e}},\qquad
 c_{\rm ter}=\frac{q_{*,e}}{q_{0,e}}.           \label{eq:endpoint-selector-raw-border}
\end{equation}
The entry collar, bulk, seam, causal-entry, and attracting finite normal
components of \(C_{\rm ter}^{\rm raw}\) vanish exactly.  Its only possible
displayed components are
\begin{equation}
 C_{p,r}^{\rm raw}=\frac{p_*'(S_*)}{\delta},\qquad
 C_{Z,L}^{\rm raw}(\theta)
 =\frac{Z_*'(S_*-L+\theta)}{\delta}.             \label{eq:endpoint-selector-raw-terminal-column}
\end{equation}
Define \(K\in\mathcal L(\mathbb R,\mathcal Y_{\rm up})\) to vanish in
every fixed row and to have
terminal components
\begin{equation}
 K_p=\frac{q_{0,e}p_*'(S_*)}{\delta q_{*,e}},\qquad
 K_{Z,L}(\theta)
 =\frac{q_{0,e}Z_*'(S_*-L+\theta)}
        {\delta q_{*,e}}.                       \label{eq:endpoint-selector-event-shear}
\end{equation}
Then \(C_{\rm ter}^{\rm raw}=Kc_{\rm ter}\).  The bounded row operation
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal U^{\rm ea}=\mathcal U^{\rm raw}-KE     \label{eq:endpoint-selector-nonlinear-row-shear}
\end{equation}
preserves the nonlinear zero set and triangularizes the derivative:
\begin{equation}
 D(\mathcal U^{\rm ea},E)(Y_*,0)
 =\begin{pmatrix}L^{\rm ea}&0\\
                  \ell_{\rm ter}&c_{\rm ter}\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 L^{\rm ea}=L^{\rm raw}-K\ell_{\rm ter}.       \label{eq:endpoint-selector-event-aligned-border}
\end{equation}
The terminal rows in \(L^{\rm ea}\) are exactly the event-aligned traces
of
\cref{eq:event-aligned-normal-quotient,eq:trace-scale-first-triangularization}.
If \(L^{\rm ea}:\mathcal X_{\rm env}\to\mathcal Y_{\rm up}\) is an
isomorphism, then so is the complete bordered derivative.  For raw data
\((f^{\rm raw},b)\), put \(f^{\rm ea}=f^{\rm raw}-Kb\); its inverse is
\begin{equation}
 v=(L^{\rm ea})^{-1}f^{\rm ea},\qquad
 \tau=c_{\rm ter}^{-1}
       \{b-\ell_{\rm ter}(L^{\rm ea})^{-1}f^{\rm ea}\}.
                                                        \label{eq:endpoint-selector-triangular-inverse}
\end{equation}

The upper duration column is not a coordinate invariant.  More generally,
for a block
\begin{equation*}
 \mathscr B(v,\tau)=(Lv+C\tau,\ell v+c\tau)
\end{equation*}
with invertible \(L\), a bounded affine domain lift
\(v=w+A\tau\) changes
\begin{equation}
 C\longmapsto C+LA,\qquad c\longmapsto c+\ell A,              \label{eq:endpoint-selector-domain-shear}
\end{equation}
but preserves the Schur scalar exactly:
\begin{equation}
 (c+\ell A)-\ell L^{-1}(C+LA)
 =c-\ell L^{-1}C.                               \label{eq:endpoint-selector-domain-schur-invariance}
\end{equation}
A range shear \((f,b)\mapsto(f-Kb,b)\) preserves the complete bordered
operator up to an isomorphism but does not in general preserve the
numerical Schur scalar.  If both upper blocks are invertible and
\(\alpha=\ell L^{-1}K\ne1\), then
\begin{equation}
 c-\ell(L-K\ell)^{-1}(C-Kc)
 =\frac{c-\ell L^{-1}C}{1-\alpha}.              \label{eq:endpoint-selector-range-schur-law}
\end{equation}

If the terminal germ of the supplied reference is the physical path in
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-physical-path}, in particular if
\begin{equation}
 \delta^{-1}r_*'(S_*)=q_\sigma^E(e_1),           \label{eq:endpoint-selector-structured-germ}
\end{equation}
then \cref{thm:reference-pulled-causal-endpoint} gives
\begin{equation}
 c_{\rm ter}
 =\frac{q_\sigma^E(e_1)}{q_0(e_1)}\in[1/2,3/2].             \label{eq:endpoint-selector-scalar-margin}
\end{equation}
Without this membership, only the nonvanishing speed ratio in
\cref{eq:endpoint-selector-reference-speeds} is asserted.

This is exact fixed-envelope selector and bordered-operator calculus.  It
does not construct the endpoint reference, prove that \(L^{\rm ea}\) is a
dimension-uniform rough-middle/strong-terminal hybrid isomorphism, control
\(K\) in such a hybrid norm, or produce a nonlinear original-endpoint
tracker, a past orbit, or a physical root.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proposition}[Exact terminal normal shear and raw-collar anchor]
\label[proposition]{prop:endpoint-normal-shear-identity}
Retain
\cref{prop:fold-time-tracker-normal-form,%
lem:raw-compatible-moving-core-collar,%
lem:endpoint-centered-duration-selector}.
Let a supplied classical endpoint reference satisfy the unmodified RFDE on
a terminal buffer, let
\[
 e=r_*(S_*),\qquad q_{*,e}=\delta^{-1}r_*'(S_*)\ne0,
 \qquad Q_e=Q_*(S_*),
\]
and suppose its moving finite normal row is the scalar \(p\)-row in
\cref{eq:endpoint-selector-raw-terminal-column}.  Then the scalar event
shear has the exact representation
\begin{equation}
 K_p
 =R_{q,N}(e)
   +\frac{q_0(e)}{q_{*,e}}w_{0,N}'(e)Q_e.        \label{eq:endpoint-normal-shear-identity}
\end{equation}
Consequently, if
\begin{equation}
 |q_{*,e}|\asymp|q_0(e)|\asymp1,\qquad
 |w_{0,N}'(e)|\asymp R_\delta,\qquad |e|\asymp R_\delta,
                                                        \label{eq:endpoint-normal-shear-scale-hypotheses}
\end{equation}
then
\begin{align}
 |K_p|&\le C\{\varepsilon+R_\delta|Q_e|\},\notag\\
 \kappa_{p,\delta}
 :=\frac{\rho_\delta|K_p|}{\varepsilon R_\delta}
 &\le C\left\{\frac{\rho_\delta}{R_\delta}
              +\frac{\rho_\delta|Q_e|}{\varepsilon}\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:endpoint-normal-shear-scale-bound}
\end{align}
Thus \(|Q_e|\le C\varepsilon/\rho_\delta\) is sufficient for
\(\kappa_{p,\delta}=O(1)\), while
\(|Q_e|\le C\varepsilon/R_\delta\) is sufficient for
\(K_p=O(\varepsilon)\).

For comparison, take the raw-compatible moving-core collar in
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-moving-core} and place its current endpoint at the
repelling outer event, \(|e|\asymp R_\delta\).  Then
\begin{equation}
 q_{*,e}=q_0(e),\qquad Q_e=0,\qquad
 p_*'(0)=\delta R_{q,N}(e),\qquad
 K_p^{\rm rc}=R_{q,N}(e).                       \label{eq:raw-collar-terminal-shear-anchor}
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 |K_p^{\rm rc}|\le C\varepsilon,\qquad
 \kappa_{p,\delta}^{\rm rc}
 \le C\frac{\rho_\delta}{R_\delta}=o(1).       \label{eq:raw-collar-terminal-shear-smallness}
\end{equation}

The last identities concern a local raw-compatible endpoint germ.  They do
not identify that germ with the terminal history reached by a forward branch.
In particular, the ordinary terminal \(\mathsf T^1\)-bound does not imply
either endpoint-speed condition above, and this proposition does not bound
the shear of a still-unconstructed physical endpoint reference.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Exact phase-delay shift and state-norm obstruction]
\label[proposition]{prop:phase-delay-action-shift}
Retain \cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer,%
lem:reduced-flow-phase-defect,prop:event-aligned-normal-traces}.
For \(a\in\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)\), write
\begin{equation}
 P=\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,\qquad
 P_\perp=\mathscr P_{\perp,\sigma,N}a,\qquad
 p^\sharp=p+\varepsilon P,\qquad
 \widehat Q=Q_{\rm n}+P.                          \label{eq:phase-action-shift}
\end{equation}
Then the full formal phase column and the fixed-phase normal block are
equivalent, exactly, to
\begin{align}
 \varepsilon\widehat Q
 &=p^\sharp-\ell_NZ
       +\varepsilon\mathscr D_{c,N}^{\rm lin}[\gamma,Z],\notag\\
 \delta Z_s
 &=\mathcal C_NZ-z_{0,N}'p^\sharp+F
   +\varepsilon\{\mathscr D_{\perp,N}^{\rm lin}[\gamma,Z]
       -z_{0,N}'\mathscr D_{c,N}^{\rm lin}[\gamma,Z]\}
   +F_\phi^\sharp,\notag\\
 \delta p_s^\sharp
 &=\{w_{0,N}'+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N\}
    \{p^\sharp-\ell_NZ
       +\varepsilon\mathscr D_{c,N}^{\rm lin}[\gamma,Z]\}
       +H+H_\phi^\sharp,                          \label{eq:shifted-phase-normal-system}
\end{align}
where
\begin{equation}
 F_\phi^\sharp=-q_0R_{z,N}'a+\varepsilon P_\perp,
 \qquad
 H_\phi^\sharp=\varepsilon q_0R_{q,N}'a
                    +\delta\varepsilon\partial_sP.           \label{eq:shifted-phase-structured-sources}
\end{equation}
The delay columns are continuous and piecewise \(W^{1,\infty}\) on the
finite delay-seam partition, with
\begin{align}
 |P(s)|+\|P_\perp(s)\|_N+|\partial_sP(s)|
 &\le C\frac{\delta}{|\bar r_\sigma(s)|}
          \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)},\notag\\
 \|F_\phi^\sharp(s)\|_N
 &\le C\varepsilon\|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)},
 &|H_\phi^\sharp(s)|
 &\le C\frac{\varepsilon^2}{|\bar r_\sigma(s)|}
          \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}.   \label{eq:shifted-phase-source-profile}
\end{align}
No seam measure occurs in \(\partial_sP\).

For the zero normal-data phase response, the scalar boundary rows become
\begin{align}
 p^\sharp(0)&=0 &&(\sigma=a),\notag\\
 p^\sharp(S_r)&=\varepsilon P(S_r) &&(\sigma=r).  \label{eq:shifted-phase-boundary-rows}
\end{align}
The repelling compatible-history feedback remains indexed by
\(p^\sharp(0)=p(0)\), because the formal entry extension gives \(P(0)=0\).

The shift is necessary, not cosmetic.  If one instead asks for a uniform
bound of the formal phase response in the old normalized state norm, then
in general
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{0<\delta<\delta_0}
 \varepsilon^{-1}
 \|G_\sigma(0,0,\mathscr C_{Z,\sigma}a,
                       \mathscr C_{p,\sigma}a)\|_{
                       \mathsf X_{\sigma,0}}=\infty           \label{eq:normalized-phase-response-obstruction}
\end{equation}
for a bounded family in \(\mathsf A_\phi^1\), whenever a nonzero collective
delayed layer is present.  Thus the coupled inverse must be formulated in a
graph/action norm based on \(\widehat Q\), not by declaring the reverse
off-diagonal bounded in the old normalized state space.  This proposition
does not yet estimate the action of \(\widehat Q\).
\end{proposition}

We next isolate the strong block on which delay generations can actually be
counted.  A zero-delay difference is deleted as one intact zero term.  For a
positive delay, its current evaluation belongs to the generator and only its
shifted evaluation is a generation edge.

\begin{lemma}[Current-absorbed positive-delay splitting]
\label[lemma]{lem:current-absorbed-delay-splitting}
Fix \(d\ge0\), put \(k_d=\max\{2,d+1\}\), and retain
\cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer,prop:physical-backtrack-calculus},
with the backtrack proposition used at order \(k_d\) and the principal
coefficient estimates taken through order \(k_d+2\).  Put
\[
 \mathcal I_+=\{k\in\{0,\ldots,m\}:\theta_k>0\}.
\]
Along either prescribed reduced path, with all undisplayed coefficients
evaluated at \(\bar r_\sigma(s)\), define
\begin{align}
 \mathscr R_{k,N}^{\rm cur}(s)
 &=K\{P_{\perp,N}-z_{0,N}'\pi_N^T\}L_{k,N}(\eta),
 &c_{k,N}(s)&=K\pi_N^TL_{k,N}(\eta),\notag\\
 b_{k,N}(s)
 &=\{\varepsilon w_{0,N}'(s)
       +\varepsilon^2\mathfrak d_N(s)\}c_{k,N}(s),
 &&k\in\mathcal I_+,                              \label{eq:current-absorbed-delay-rows}\\
 \mathbb A_{\rm cur}(s)\binom Zp
 &=
  \binom{\displaystyle
  \mathcal C_NZ-z_{0,N}'p
       +\varepsilon\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}
                    \mathscr R_{k,N}^{\rm cur}Z}
 {\displaystyle
  w_{0,N}'p-w_{0,N}'\ell_NZ
       +\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N(p-\ell_NZ)
       +\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}b_{k,N}Z},\notag\\
 \mathbb B_k(s)u
 &=
 \binom{-\varepsilon\mathscr R_{k,N}^{\rm cur}(s)u}
       {-b_{k,N}(s)u}.
                                                               \label{eq:current-absorbed-delay-blocks}
\end{align}
After eliminating \(Q\), \cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} is exactly
\begin{equation}
 \delta x_s
 =\mathbb A_{\rm cur}(s)x(s)
  +\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}
       \mathbb B_k(s)\widehat Z(s-\theta_k)
  +\binom FH,\qquad x=(Z,p),                       \label{eq:current-absorbed-delay-equation}
\end{equation}
where \(\widehat Z=\gamma\) before \(s=0\) and \(\widehat Z=Z\) afterwards.
No term with \(\theta_k=0\) occurs.

On an attracting outer buffer
\([0,L_b]\subset[0,S_a]\) for which
\begin{equation}
 \frac12r_{\rm out}\le\bar r_a(s)\le r_{\rm out},
 \qquad 0\le s\le L_b,                            \label{eq:attracting-outer-buffer-range}
\end{equation}
let \(\mathbb U_{a,\rm cur}(s,u)\) be the evolution family of
\(\delta x_s=\mathbb A_{\rm cur}(s)x\).  In the norm
\[
 \|(Z,p)\|_{a,\rm out}=\|Z\|_N+\frac{|p|}{r_{\rm out}},
\]
it satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \max_{i+j\le d}\delta^{i+j}
 \|\partial_s^i\partial_u^j
       \mathbb U_{a,\rm cur}(s,u)\|_{\mathcal L(E_N\times\R)}
 \le C_d e^{-c(s-u)/\delta},
 \qquad 0\le u\le s\le L_b.                       \label{eq:current-absorbed-attracting-evolution}
\end{equation}
Here the operator norm is induced by \(\|\cdot\|_{a,\rm out}\).
The constants may depend on \(d\), the fixed \(r_{\rm out}\), and the fixed
delay support, but are uniform in \(N,\delta,\nu,\eta\) on the retained
balls.

There is also a diagonal, order-one version in which the slow rate is not
hidden in the constant.  Fix \(\varkappa>0\), take \(d=1\), and set
\begin{equation}
 R_\delta=\varkappa(\delta S_\delta^3)^{1/2},
 \qquad r_{\rm out}=R_\delta.                    \label{eq:diagonal-outer-buffer-scale}
\end{equation}
For sufficiently small \(\delta\), depending on the fixed
\(\varkappa\), whenever
\[
 \tfrac12R_\delta\le\bar r_a(s)\le R_\delta
 \quad(0\le s\le L_b),
\]
the same evolution family satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \max_{i+j\le1}\delta^{i+j}
 \|\partial_s^i\partial_u^j
       \mathbb U_{a,\rm cur}(s,u)\|_{\mathcal L(E_N\times\R)}
 \le C e^{-cR_\delta(s-u)/\delta},
 \qquad 0\le u\le s\le L_b,                       \label{eq:diagonal-current-absorbed-evolution}
\end{equation}
in the norm
\(\|(Z,p)\|_{a,\delta}=\|Z\|_N+|p|/R_\delta\).
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{0\le s\le L_b}
   \sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}
   \|\mathbb B_k(s)\|_{\mathcal L(E_N,E_N\times\R)}
 \le C\varepsilon,                               \label{eq:diagonal-delayed-block-size}
\end{equation}
where the target has the preceding diagonal norm.  Consequently one strict
positive-delay Duhamel generation has the scale-explicit bound
\begin{align}
 &\sup_{0\le s\le L_b}
 \left\|
  \frac1\delta\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}
   \int_0^s\mathbb U_{a,\rm cur}(s,u)
      \mathbb B_k(u)\zeta_k(u)\,\dd u
 \right\|_{a,\delta}\notag\\
 &\hspace{7em}\le
 C\frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta}
 \max_{k\in\mathcal I_+}
       \|\zeta_k\|_{L^\infty([0,L_b];E_N)}.       \label{eq:diagonal-one-generation}
\end{align}
If \(\mathcal I_+=\varnothing\), the left-hand side and the displayed
right-hand side are both interpreted as zero.
The fixed-annulus exponent in
\cref{eq:current-absorbed-attracting-evolution} is therefore not
diagonal-uniform: on the family
\cref{eq:diagonal-outer-buffer-scale}, its scalar part contains a factor
comparable to \(R_\delta\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proposition}[Attracting finite-generation collar flushing]
\label[proposition]{prop:attracting-finite-generation-flushing}
Retain
\cref{prop:compatible-endpoint-jet-collar,%
prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer,%
lem:current-absorbed-delay-splitting}.  Fix an integer \(d\ge1\), take
\(h\ge\max\{3,d\}\), and choose an integer \(M=M_d\) such that
\begin{equation}
 M(1-2\vartheta)>d.                               \label{eq:attracting-flush-generation-count}
\end{equation}
 Fix \(A_d>0\), put
\begin{equation}
 L_0=A_dS_\delta,\qquad
 L_b=(M+1)\theta_m+L_0+1,                        \label{eq:attracting-flush-buffer-length}
\end{equation}
and decrease \(\delta_0\).  Then \(L_b<S_a\) and
\cref{eq:attracting-outer-buffer-range} hold.

Let \(\gamma\in C^h([-\theta_m,0];\R^N)\) satisfy
\(\gamma(0)\in E_N\), and let \(b_p\in\R\).  Let \((Z,p,Q)\) be the
attracting solution of \cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} with \(F=H=0\),
old voltage history \(\gamma\), and
\[
 Z(0)=\gamma(0),\qquad p(0)=b_p.
\]
On the exit interval
\begin{equation*}
 J_b=\bar r_a([L_b-\theta_m,L_b]),
\end{equation*}
use \(r=\bar r_a(s)\) to write the restrictions as
\((Z_b,p_b,Q_b)\).  Then
\begin{align}
 &\|Z_b\|_{\mathsf Z_\varepsilon^d(J_b)}
 +\|p_b\|_{\mathsf P_\varepsilon^d(J_b)}
 +\|Q_b\|_{\mathsf Q_\varepsilon^{d-1}(J_b)}
 \notag\\
 &\quad\le C_d\left\{
   \delta^{-2d}e^{-cL_0/\delta}
   +\delta^{M(2-2\vartheta)-2d}\right\}
 \varepsilon^{-1}
 \left\{\|\gamma\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,r_{\rm out}}
             +\frac{|b_p|}{r_{\rm out}}\right\}.  \label{eq:attracting-finite-generation-flush}
\end{align}
More precisely, if the input is given the normalized norm
\begin{equation*}
 \|(\gamma,b_p)\|_{a,\mathrm{in}}
 =\varepsilon^{-1}
   \left\{\|\gamma\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,r_{\rm out}}
                  +\frac{|b_p|}{r_{\rm out}}\right\},
\end{equation*}
then the operator norm of this collar-to-exit trace tends to zero with
\(\delta\), uniformly in \(N\).  No decay from the corresponding
\emph{unnormalized} input norm is asserted.  For every fixed \(\mu>0\),
\[
 \delta^{-2d}e^{-cA_dS_\delta/\delta}
 \le C_{d,\mu}\delta^\mu.
\]

The estimate is fixed-parameter.  It neither differentiates the inverse in
\((\nu,\eta)\) nor treats the repelling mixed orientation.  It also supplies
no phase--normal coupling, nonlinear contraction, speed sign, or tracker.
\end{proposition}

The repelling branch cannot be treated by taking powers of its full mixed
Green operator.  Its scalar line is selected from the future, so that full
operator has precursors and no causal support filtration.  The correct
iteration counts only strict delayed evaluations of \(Z\), while resumming
the current \(Z\)--\(p\) loop inside each generation.

\begin{lemma}[Repelling component kernel and decorated delay generations]
\label[lemma]{lem:repelling-component-generations}
Retain \cref{lem:current-absorbed-delay-splitting} at order \(d\ge0\), use
\cref{prop:physical-backtrack-calculus} at order \(d+1\), take the principal
coefficient estimates through order \(d+3\), and work on
\(I_r=[0,S_r]\).  Put
\begin{align}
 \mathbb A_Z
 &=\mathcal C_N+\varepsilon\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}
                 \mathscr R_{k,N}^{\rm cur},
 &a_p&=w_{0,N}'+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N,\notag\\
 C_{Zp}&=z_{0,N}',
 &C_{pZ}&=a_p\left(\ell_N-\varepsilon
                  \sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}c_{k,N}\right),\notag\\
 B_k^Z&=-\varepsilon\mathscr R_{k,N}^{\rm cur},
 &B_k^p&=-b_{k,N}=-\varepsilon a_pc_{k,N}.          \label{eq:repelling-component-coefficients}
\end{align}
Then \cref{eq:current-absorbed-delay-equation}, with \(F=H=0\), is
\begin{align}
 \delta Z_s
 &=\mathbb A_ZZ-C_{Zp}p
       +\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}B_k^Z\widehat Z(s-\theta_k),\notag\\
 \delta p_s
 &=a_pp-C_{pZ}Z
       +\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}B_k^p\widehat Z(s-\theta_k).
                                                               \label{eq:repelling-component-equations}
\end{align}
Write \(r_*=|r|\).  Uniformly on the repelling branch,
\begin{equation}
 c r_*\le a_p(r)\le C r_*,\qquad
 \|C_{Zp}(r)\|\le Cr_*,\qquad
 \|C_{pZ}(r)\|\le Cr_*^2.                         \label{eq:repelling-component-sizes}
\end{equation}
Moreover, for \(0\le j\le d\),
\begin{align}
 r_*^j|\partial_r^j\mathfrak d_N(r)|
 &\le C_d,                                                   \label{eq:repelling-d-weighted-jets}\\
 r_*^j\|\partial_r^j(\mathbb A_Z-A_N)\|
 &\le C_dr_*,\notag\\
 r_*^j\bigl\{|\partial_r^ja_p|
              +\|\partial_r^jC_{Zp}\|\bigr\}
 &\le C_dr_*,\notag\\
 r_*^j\|\partial_r^jC_{pZ}\|
 &\le C_dr_*^2,\notag\\
 r_*^j\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}\|\partial_r^jB_k^Z\|
 &\le C_d\varepsilon,\notag\\
 r_*^j\sum_{k\in\mathcal I_+}\|\partial_r^jB_k^p\|
 &\le C_d\varepsilon r_*,                                  \label{eq:repelling-weighted-block-jets}
\end{align}
and
\begin{equation}
 r_*^{j+1}\left\{
  |\partial_r^j(a_p^{-1})|
  +\left|\partial_r^j\!\left(\frac{q_0}{a_p}\right)\right|
 \right\}\le C_d.                                  \label{eq:repelling-inverse-coefficient-jets}
\end{equation}

Let \(U_Z(s,u)\) be the forward evolution of
\(\delta Z_s=\mathbb A_Z(s)Z\), and, for \(s\le u\), put
\begin{align}
 u_p(s,u)
 &=\exp\left\{-\delta^{-1}\int_s^ua_p(v)\,\dd v\right\},
 &\Psi_p(s,u)&=\delta^{-1}\int_s^ua_p(v)\,\dd v. \label{eq:repelling-component-kernels}
\end{align}
For \(i+j\le d\),
\begin{align}
 \delta^{i+j}\|\partial_s^i\partial_u^jU_Z(s,u)\|
 &\le C_de^{-c(s-u)/\delta},&&u\le s,\notag\\
 \delta^{i+j}|\partial_s^i\partial_u^ju_p(s,u)|
 &\le C_de^{-c\Psi_p(s,u)},&&s\le u.             \label{eq:repelling-component-kernel-bounds}
\end{align}
Define
\begin{align}
 (\mathcal G_Zf)(s)
 &=\delta^{-1}\int_0^sU_Z(s,u)f(u)\,\dd u,\notag\\
 (\mathcal G_p^+g)(s)
 &=\delta^{-1}\int_s^{S_r}u_p(s,u)g(u)\,\dd u,\notag\\
 \mathcal K_{Zp}&=\mathcal G_ZC_{Zp},
 &\mathcal K_{pZ}&=\mathcal G_p^+C_{pZ}.          \label{eq:repelling-component-Green-operators}
\end{align}
Thus the mixed current problem with component sources \((f_Z,f_p)\) and
traces \((b_s,b_u)\) is exactly
\begin{align}
 Z&=U_Z(\,\cdot\,,0)b_s-\mathcal K_{Zp}p
                         +\mathcal G_Zf_Z,\notag\\
 p&=u_p(\,\cdot\,,S_r)b_u+\mathcal K_{pZ}Z
                         -\mathcal G_p^+f_p.       \label{eq:repelling-component-LP}
\end{align}

Let \(\Sigma_r\) be the finite set of all sums of positive delays lying in
\([0,S_r]\), and use the continuous, piecewise \(W^{d,\infty}\) space on
the induced partition, with norm
\begin{equation}
 \|x\|_{\mathfrak S_{r,d}}
 =\max_{0\le j\le d}\delta^j
  \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0\le s\le S_r}
  \left\{\|\partial_s^jZ(s)\|_N
       +\frac{|\partial_s^jp(s)|}{|\bar r_r(s)|}\right\}.     \label{eq:repelling-component-strong-space}
\end{equation}
After the fixed choice of small \(r_{\rm out}\),
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal K_{Zp}\|\le Cr_{\rm out}^2,\qquad
 \|\mathcal K_{pZ}\|\le C,\qquad
 \|\mathcal K_{Zp}\mathcal K_{pZ}\|\le\frac14              \label{eq:repelling-current-loop-bound}
\end{equation}
on this space.  The action statement used below is local and oriented.  Fix
small \(\kappa,\kappa_{\rm out}>0\) and attach to a forward transverse edge
and a future scalar edge, respectively, the two-point weights
\begin{equation}
 W_Z(s,u)=e^{\kappa(s-u)/\delta}\quad(u\le s),\qquad
 W_p(s,u)=e^{\kappa\Psi_p(s,u)}\quad(s\le u).        \label{eq:repelling-oriented-edge-weights}
\end{equation}
If both endpoints and the intervening scalar edge lie in an outer interval
on which \(a_p\ge c_{\rm out}>0\), one may in addition use
\begin{equation}
 W_{p,{\rm out}}(s,u)=e^{\kappa_{\rm out}(u-s)/\delta}.
                                                               \label{eq:repelling-terminal-edge-weight}
\end{equation}
For \(i+j\le d\), the corresponding precise estimates are
\begin{align}
 \delta^{i+j}W_Z(s,u)
       \|\partial_s^i\partial_u^jU_Z(s,u)\|&\le C_d,\notag\\
 \delta^{i+j}W_p(s,u)
       |\partial_s^i\partial_u^ju_p(s,u)|&\le C_d,\notag\\
 \delta^{i+j}W_{p,{\rm out}}(s,u)
       |\partial_s^i\partial_u^ju_p(s,u)|&\le C_d,             \label{eq:repelling-weighted-kernel-bounds}
\end{align}
where the last line is used only under the outer-interval condition just
stated.
On the diagonal family
\cref{eq:diagonal-outer-buffer-scale}, if
\[
 \tfrac12R_\delta\le|\bar r_r(v)|\le R_\delta
 \quad(s\le v\le u),
\]
then the order-one terminal estimate with constants independent of
\(\delta\) is instead
\begin{equation}
 \max_{i+j\le1}\delta^{i+j}
 |\partial_s^i\partial_u^ju_p(s,u)|
 \le C e^{-cR_\delta(u-s)/\delta}.                \label{eq:diagonal-repelling-terminal-kernel}
\end{equation}
Thus the coefficient in a terminal-fast weight must recede with
\(R_\delta\).  On outer intervals containing a fold-time subinterval of
fixed positive length, a fixed positive coefficient cannot be retained
uniformly on this diagonal family.
After expanding the current-loop inverse, the kernel of every decorated
component word, including every boundary-face word obtained by at most
\(d\) differentiations, remains bounded after multiplication by the product
of its applicable edge weights.  Each additional current loop contributes
a factor at most \(q_{\rm loop}<1\), uniformly in \(N\), and the loop
multiplicities sum geometrically.  No global terminal-fast weight for the
scalar component is asserted away from such an outer interval.

Let \(h\ge\max\{3,d\}\), let
\(\gamma\in C^h([-\theta_m,0];\R^N)\) satisfy
\(\gamma(0)\in E_N\), and let \(b_u\in\R\).  Define
\begin{align}
 D_\gamma^\diamond(s)
 &=\sum_{\substack{k\in\mathcal I_+\\0\le s<\theta_k}}
       B_k^\diamond(s)\gamma(s-\theta_k),\notag\\
 (\mathscr S_\diamond y)(s)
 &=\sum_{\substack{k\in\mathcal I_+\\s\ge\theta_k}}
       B_k^\diamond(s)y(s-\theta_k),
 \qquad \diamond\in\{Z,p\}.                     \label{eq:repelling-old-and-strict-sources}
\end{align}
Let \(x^{[0]}=(Z^{[0]},p^{[0]})\) be the unique current-loop solution
\begin{align*}
 Z^{[0]}&=U_Z(\,\cdot\,,0)\gamma(0)
     -\mathcal K_{Zp}p^{[0]}+\mathcal G_ZD_\gamma^Z,\\
 p^{[0]}&=u_p(\,\cdot\,,S_r)b_u
     +\mathcal K_{pZ}Z^{[0]}-\mathcal G_p^+D_\gamma^p,
\end{align*}
and, recursively, let \(x^{[n+1]}\) have zero finite boundary rows and solve
\begin{align}
 Z^{[n+1]}&=-\mathcal K_{Zp}p^{[n+1]}
                  +\mathcal G_Z\mathscr S_ZZ^{[n]},\notag\\
 p^{[n+1]}&=\mathcal K_{pZ}Z^{[n+1]}
                  -\mathcal G_p^+\mathscr S_pZ^{[n]}.
                                                               \label{eq:repelling-component-generation-recursion}
\end{align}
Then
\begin{equation}
 (Z,p)=\sum_{n=0}^\infty(Z^{[n]},p^{[n]})           \label{eq:repelling-component-generation-series}
\end{equation}
converges absolutely in \(\mathfrak S_{r,d}\) to the unique solution of
\cref{eq:repelling-component-equations} with
\(Z(0)=\gamma(0)\) and \(p(S_r)=b_u\).  Moreover,
\begin{align}
 \|x^{[0]}\|_{\mathfrak S_{r,d}}
 &\le C_d\left\{\|\gamma\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
                         +\frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}}\right\},\notag\\
 \|x^{[n+1]}\|_{\mathfrak S_{r,d}}
 &\le\beta_{r,\delta}\|x^{[n]}\|_{\mathfrak S_{r,d}},
 &\beta_{r,\delta}
 &\le C_d\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}
  \le C_d\frac{\delta^{1-2\vartheta}}{S_\delta}=o(1).       \label{eq:repelling-generation-number}
\end{align}
Each recursion index \(n\) counts exactly \(n\) strict delayed evaluations
of the current path after the root insertion.  Endpoint and terminal words
therefore have \(n\) strict positive-delay edges.  An old-history word has,
in addition, its direct collar edge in \(D_\gamma\); equivalently that edge
is absorbed into a current insertion label in \([0,\theta_m]\), after which
the word has \(n\) recursive edges.  The arbitrarily many current
\(Z\)--\(p\) loops have already been summed by
\cref{eq:repelling-current-loop-bound}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proposition}[Diagonal first-order terminal component Green estimates]
\label[proposition]{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green}
Retain
\cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer,%
lem:current-absorbed-delay-splitting,%
lem:repelling-component-generations}
at order one.  Fix \(\varkappa,A>0\) and an integer \(M\ge2\), and put
\begin{align}
 R_\delta&=\varkappa(\delta S_\delta^3)^{1/2},&
 r_{a,T}&=\rho_\delta,& r_{r,T}&=R_\delta,\notag\\
 m_{\sigma,T}&=r_{\sigma,T}
       +\frac{\varepsilon}{r_{\sigma,T}^2},&
 L_0&=AS_\delta,&
 L_b&=(M+2)\theta_m+L_0+1.                       \label{eq:terminal-component-scales}
\end{align}
In this proposition take \(r_{\rm out}=R_\delta\).  Let \(S_\sigma\)
be the reduced fold-time duration in
\cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer}, set
\begin{equation}
 s_{\sigma,T}=S_\sigma-L_b,\qquad
 \bar r_{\sigma,T}(t)=\bar r_\sigma(s_{\sigma,T}+t),\qquad
 0\le t\le L_b,                                  \label{eq:terminal-component-recut}
\end{equation}
and extend this path to \([-\theta_m,L_b]\) by the same reduced flow.
For all sufficiently small \(\delta\), depending on the displayed fixed
choices,
\begin{align}
 \rho_\delta\le \bar r_{a,T}(t)&\le C_A\rho_\delta,
       &&-\theta_m\le t\le L_b,\notag\\
 \frac12R_\delta\le|\bar r_{r,T}(t)|&\le R_\delta,
       &&-\theta_m\le t\le L_b.                 \label{eq:terminal-component-annuli}
\end{align}

On this shifted interval consider the fixed-phase normal problem
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block}, equivalently its current-absorbed form
\cref{eq:current-absorbed-delay-equation}.  Prescribe a continuous
transverse history
\(\gamma\in C([-\theta_m,0];E_N)\), with
\(Z(0)=\gamma(0)\), and continuous distributed sources \((F,H)\).
On the attracting branch prescribe \(p(0)=b_a\); on the repelling branch
prescribe \(p(L_b)=b_r\).  Define
\begin{align}
 \mathcal D_{\sigma,T}(\gamma,b_\sigma,F,H)
 =\varepsilon^{-1}\biggl\{
   \|\gamma\|_{C^0}
   +\frac{|b_\sigma|}{m_{\sigma,T}}
   +\sup_{0\le t\le L_b}
       \left(\|F(t)\|_N
             +\frac{|H(t)|}
                    {r_{\sigma,T}m_{\sigma,T}}\right)
                         \biggr\},               \label{eq:terminal-component-data-norm}\\
 \|(Z,p)\|_{\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1(J)}
 =\varepsilon^{-1}\max_{0\le j\le1}\delta^j
   \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{t\in J}
       \left\{\|\partial_t^jZ(t)\|_N
              +\frac{|\partial_t^jp(t)|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-component-trace-norm}
\end{align}
Then the two mixed problems have unique continuous, piecewise
\(W^{1,\infty}\) solutions and, uniformly in \(N,\delta\), the branch,
and the retained parameters,
\begin{equation}
 \|(Z,p)\|_{\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 \le C_{A,M}\mathcal D_{\sigma,T}(\gamma,b_\sigma,F,H).
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-component-source-bound}
\end{equation}
If the data have no seam on an open neighborhood of
\([L_b-\theta_m,L_b]\), the restriction there is genuinely \(C^1\).

The local strict-positive-delay generation operators have the sharper
branchwise bounds
\begin{equation}
 \beta_{a,T}\le C_{A,M}\frac{\varepsilon}{m_{a,T}},
 \qquad
 \beta_{r,T}\le C_{A,M}\frac{\varepsilon}{m_{r,T}},
 \qquad
 m_{a,T}\asymp S_\delta^{-2},\quad
 m_{r,T}\asymp R_\delta,                         \label{eq:terminal-component-generation-numbers}
\end{equation}
and both tend to zero.  Consequently incoming terminal-interface data are
flushed before the final history window.  More precisely, if \(F=H=0\), then
\begin{align}
 \|(Z,p)\|_{\mathsf T_{a,T}^1([L_b-\theta_m,L_b])}
 &\le C_{A,M}\mathcal E_{a,T}(\delta)\,
   \varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
      \|\gamma\|_{C^0}+\frac{|b_a|}{m_{a,T}}\right\},\notag\\
 \|(Z,p)\|_{\mathsf T_{r,T}^1([L_b-\theta_m,L_b])}
 &\le C_{A,M}\mathcal E_{r,T}(\delta)\,
        \varepsilon^{-1}\|\gamma\|_{C^0}
        \qquad (b_r=0),                          \label{eq:terminal-component-rough-flush}
\end{align}
where
\begin{align}
 \mathcal E_{a,T}(\delta)
 &=\exp\left\{-\frac{c\rho_\delta L_0}{\delta}\right\}
       +\left(C\frac{\varepsilon}{m_{a,T}}\right)^M,\notag\\
 \mathcal E_{r,T}(\delta)
 &=\exp\left\{-\frac{cR_\delta L_0}{\delta}\right\}
       +\left(C\frac{\varepsilon}{m_{r,T}}\right)^M.
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-component-flush-errors}
\end{align}
In particular, both errors tend to zero.  The repelling condition \(b_r=0\)
is necessary in the flushing statement because a nonzero terminal scalar
row is visible in the final window.

Let \(Q\) be reconstructed by the algebraic row in
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block}, and use the reduced coordinate on the
terminal buffer.  The same solution satisfies
\begin{align}
 \sup_{0\le t\le L_b}|Q(t)|
 &\le C_{A,M}m_{\sigma,T}
       \mathcal D_{\sigma,T}(\gamma,b_\sigma,F,H),\notag\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}\sup_{0\le t\le L_b}
 \left|\int_{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(0)}^{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(t)}
          \frac{Q(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u\right|
 &\le C_{A,M}m_{\sigma,T}
       \mathcal D_{\sigma,T}(\gamma,b_\sigma,F,H).          \label{eq:terminal-component-local-action}
\end{align}

This is a reduced-base, fixed-phase, first-order terminal-source theorem.
It permits arbitrary distributed sources at their natural transverse and
scalar terminal scales and proves an incoming-interface flushing estimate;
its range norm is not defined through the solution operator.  It does not
construct an endpoint RFDE reference, control the phase-induced structured
column in this strong norm, construct the rough-middle-to-incoming trace and
prefix map, prove a higher-order or parameter-differentiated terminal
inverse, bound the event shear \(K\), or produce a nonlinear moving-endpoint
family or a physical root.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Structured phase-column action Green estimate]
\label[proposition]{prop:structured-phase-action-green}
Retain \cref{prop:phase-delay-action-shift,%
lem:current-absorbed-delay-splitting,%
lem:repelling-component-generations} at order zero.  Put
\begin{equation}
 m_\delta(r)=|r|+\frac{\varepsilon}{|r|^2}.         \label{eq:phase-action-weight}
\end{equation}
On either branch, let \((Z,p^\sharp)\) solve the current-absorbed form of
\cref{eq:shifted-phase-normal-system}, with zero exogenous normal data,
the structured sources in
\cref{eq:shifted-phase-structured-sources}, and the formal boundary rows
\begin{align*}
 &\gamma=0,\quad Z(0)=0,\quad p^\sharp(0)=0
       &&(\sigma=a),\\
 &Z(0)=0,\quad p^\sharp(S_r)=\varepsilon P(S_r),\quad
   \gamma=\mathfrak C_ep^\sharp(0)
       &&(\sigma=r),
\end{align*}
where the repelling scalar feedback is the closed one from
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-collar-feedback}.  Then the mixed problem has a unique
continuous, piecewise \(W^{1,\infty}\) solution and
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in I_\sigma}\left\{
   \frac{\|Z(s)\|_N}{\varepsilon}
   +\frac{|p^\sharp(s)|}
          {\varepsilon m_\delta(\bar r_\sigma(s))}\right\}
 \le C\|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}.         \label{eq:structured-phase-state-bound}
\end{equation}
The reconstructed true speed response satisfies
\begin{align}
 |\widehat Q(r)|
 &\le C\left\{|r|+\frac{\varepsilon}{|r|^2}\right\}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)},       \label{eq:structured-phase-speed-bound}\\
 \|\widehat Q\|_{\mathsf H_\phi^0(I_\sigma)}
 &\le C\left\{r_{\rm out}^2
             +\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right\}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}
  \le C\left\{r_{\rm out}^2+\frac{\delta}{S_\delta}\right\}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}.       \label{eq:structured-phase-action-bound}
\end{align}
All bounds are dimension uniform.

Equivalently, if \(T_\sigma^{\rm form}a\) denotes the returned normal
response in the original variable
\(p=p^\sharp-\varepsilon P\), then the true action operator
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm form}a
 =Q_{\rm n}[T_\sigma^{\rm form}a]
       +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a                 \label{eq:formal-true-action-operator}
\end{equation}
maps \(\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)\) to
\(\mathsf H_\phi^0(I_\sigma)\) with norm at most the last constant in
\cref{eq:structured-phase-action-bound}.  The proposition does not assert
that \(T_\sigma^{\rm form}\) is uniformly bounded in the old normalized
state norm; \cref{eq:normalized-phase-response-obstruction} shows that it
need not be.
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[Raw-compatible phase boundary-column assembly]
\label[lemma]{lem:raw-phase-boundary-column-assembly}
Retain \cref{lem:raw-compatible-moving-core-collar,%
prop:normal-to-phase-action-coupling,%
prop:structured-phase-action-green}.  Put
\[
 e_a=r_{\rm out},\qquad e_r=-\rho_\delta,\qquad
 E_{0,\sigma}a=a(e_{\sigma,0}),\qquad
 \gamma_\sigma^{\rm ph}=\gamma_{e_\sigma}^{\rm ph}.
\]
For \(c\in\R\), let \(\mathfrak R_{a,\rm col}c\) be the unique zero-source
fixed-phase normal response with
\[
 \gamma=c\gamma_a^{\rm ph},\qquad p(0)=0,
\]
and let \(\mathfrak R_{r,\rm col}c\) be the unique closed repelling response
with
\begin{equation}
 p(S_r)=0,\qquad
 \gamma=c\gamma_r^{\rm ph}+\mathfrak C_ep(0).       \label{eq:raw-phase-repelling-history-column}
\end{equation}
Write \(Q_{\sigma,\rm col}c\) for the algebraically reconstructed
\(Q_{\rm n}\)-component of this response and, on the repelling branch,
write \(\gamma_{r,c}^{\rm tot}\) for the actual history in
\cref{eq:raw-phase-repelling-history-column}.  Then
\begin{align}
 \|(c\gamma_a^{\rm ph},\mathfrak R_{a,\rm col}c)\|_{
       \widetilde{\mathsf X}_{a,0}}
 &\le C|c|,\notag\\
 \|(\gamma_{r,c}^{\rm tot},\mathfrak R_{r,\rm col}c)\|_{
       \widetilde{\mathsf X}_{r,0}}
 &\le C|c|,                                      \label{eq:raw-phase-collar-response-bound}\\
 \|Q_{\sigma,\rm col}c\|_{\mathsf H_\phi^0(I_\sigma)}
 &\le C\{r_{\rm out}^2+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}|c|.
                                                        \label{eq:raw-phase-collar-action-bound}
\end{align}

Define the raw-compatible returned phase column and its true action by
\begin{align}
 T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a
 &=T_\sigma^{\rm form}a
       +\mathfrak R_{\sigma,\rm col}E_{0,\sigma}a,\notag\\
 \mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a
 &=\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm form}a
       +Q_{\sigma,\rm col}E_{0,\sigma}a\notag\\
 &=Q_{\rm n}[T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a]
       +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a.                \label{eq:raw-compatible-true-action}
\end{align}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}\|_{
   \mathcal L(\mathsf A_\phi^1,\mathsf H_\phi^0)}
 \le C\left\{r_{\rm out}^2+\frac{\delta}{S_\delta}
                     +\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:raw-compatible-action-smallness}
\end{equation}
The old-history voltage boundary column is exactly the differential of the
raw-compatible collar.  More explicitly, its phase part is
\begin{equation}
 E_{0,\sigma}a\,\mathcal L_{e_\sigma}\phi_{e_\sigma}^0
 +E_{0,\sigma}a\,\gamma_{e_\sigma}^{\rm ph}
 =E_{0,\sigma}a\,\mathcal L_{e_\sigma}
                         \phi_{e_\sigma}^{\rm rc}.           \label{eq:raw-phase-collar-exact-assembly}
\end{equation}
On the repelling branch the closed feedback
\(\mathfrak C_ep(0)\) is added to this history.  The additive collar phase
column creates no extra scalar boundary row because its voltage and resource
normal traces vanish at the seam.  The total repelling value \(p(0)\) need
not vanish; it is the mixed-boundary output that drives the feedback.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Raw-compatible zeroth-order phase--normal inverse]
\label[theorem]{thm:raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse}
Retain \cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer,%
prop:scaled-phase-event-core,%
prop:normal-to-phase-action-coupling,%
lem:raw-phase-boundary-column-assembly}.  A normal response is a hybrid
triple \(y=(\gamma_y,Z_y,p_y)\), with
\[
 Q_y=\varepsilon^{-1}(p_y-\ell_NZ_y)
          +\mathscr D_{c,N}^{\rm lin}[\gamma_y,Z_y].
\]
Let \(F_y,H_y\) be the residuals obtained by moving every displayed
non-source term in \cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} to the left.  Define
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr N_a y=(\gamma_y,p_y(0),F_y,H_y),\qquad
 \mathscr N_r y=(\gamma_y-\mathfrak C_ep_y(0),
                         p_y(S_r),F_y,H_y).         \label{eq:fixed-phase-normal-boundary-operator}
\end{equation}
Thus on the repelling branch the first component is the prescribed baseline
history, whereas \(\gamma_y\) is the actual total history.  For a normal-data package
\(f=(\gamma_0,b_\sigma,F,H)\), use the scaled norm
\begin{equation}
 \|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}
 =\varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\gamma_0\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^{\sharp}
   +\frac{|b_\sigma|}{r_{\rm out}}
   +\|(F,H)\|_{\mathsf Y_{\sigma,0}}\right\}.     \label{eq:raw-schur-normal-data-norm}
\end{equation}
Here \(b_a=p(0)\), \(b_r=p(S_r)\), and
\(e_a=r_{\rm out}\), \(e_r=-\rho_\delta\).
Let \(\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\) be the continuous finite-seam hybrid
responses with piecewise \(W^{1,\infty}\) current part, seam condition
\(Z_y(0)=\gamma_y(0)\), algebraic row \(Q_y\), and residual in
\(\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}\).  Equip it with
\begin{equation}
 \|y\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)}
 =\|y\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
  +\|\mathscr N_\sigma y\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}.  \label{eq:fixed-phase-normal-domain-graph-norm}
\end{equation}
Then \(\mathscr N_\sigma\) is closed, and
\[
 G_\sigma:\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}
       \longrightarrow\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)
\]
is the solution operator supplied by
\cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer}.  Put
\[
 \mathfrak D_\sigma y=Q_{\rm n}[y]
     \in\mathsf H_\phi^0(I_\sigma),\qquad
 \mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm rc}
 =\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}-
       (\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc},0,0).
\]

First fix \(r_{\rm out}\) sufficiently small and then decrease
\(\delta_0\).  Uniformly in \(N\),
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{-1}\|
       \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}\|\le\frac12,             \label{eq:raw-schur-smallness}
\end{equation}
so \(\mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm rc}\) is an isomorphism from
\(\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)\times\R\) to
\(\mathsf H_\phi^0(I_\sigma)\times\R^2\), with a
dimension-uniform inverse.

More precisely, the coupled raw-compatible linear boundary problem
\begin{align}
 \mathscr N_\sigma\{x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\}&=f,\notag\\
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}(a,\widehat\tau)
  -\bigl(\mathfrak D_\sigma x
       +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,0,0\bigr)&=g             \label{eq:raw-compatible-coupled-block}
\end{align}
has, for every
\(g\in\mathsf H_\phi^0(I_\sigma)\times\R^2\), the unique solution
\begin{align}
 y&=G_\sigma f,\notag\\
 (a,\widehat\tau)
  &=(\mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm rc})^{-1}
       \{(\mathfrak D_\sigma y,0,0)+g\},\notag\\
x&=T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+y.                       \label{eq:raw-compatible-schur-inverse}
\end{align}
Define
\begin{align}
 \mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rc}
 &=\{(x,a,\widehat\tau):
       x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\in\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\},\notag\\
 \|(x,a,\widehat\tau)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rc}}
 &=\|x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)}
       +\|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1}+|\widehat\tau|.    \label{eq:raw-compatible-full-graph-norm}
\end{align}
This is a Banach graph norm.  The solution satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \|(x,a,\widehat\tau)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rc}}
 \le C\{\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}
                 +\|g\|_{\mathsf H_\phi^0\times\R^2}\}.      \label{eq:raw-compatible-schur-bound}
\end{equation}
Thus the correct uniform space is the graph norm on the left, not the old
normalized state norm of \(x\) alone.  For an arbitrary trial \(a\), the
lemma above identifies the raw boundary column; full seam compatibility is
asserted only after the coupled phase equation in
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-coupled-block} is imposed.

This theorem closes the fixed-reduced-base, zeroth-order raw-compatible
phase--normal border.  It does not construct a nonlinear moving tracker,
control parameter or higher slow jets, preserve the sign of the physical
speed, construct a past physical orbit, or prove a preparation-independent
complete-history root.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Diagonal fixed-base zeroth-order phase--normal inverse]
\label[corollary]{cor:diagonal-raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse}
Retain \cref{thm:raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse}, fix
\(\varkappa>0\), and, for each sufficiently small \(\delta\), replace
every occurrence of the fixed outer endpoint by
\[
 r_{\rm out}=R_\delta
 =\varkappa(\delta S_\delta^3)^{1/2}.
\]
Then all conclusions of
\cref{thm:raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse}, including closedness,
bijectivity, and the graph-norm estimate
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-schur-bound}, hold with a constant independent of
\(N\) and \(\delta\).  The threshold in \(\delta\) may depend on the fixed
\(\varkappa\).  In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{-1}\|
 \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}\|
 \le C\left\{
   \varkappa^2\delta S_\delta^3
   +\frac{\delta}{S_\delta}
   +\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\right\}=o(1).           \label{eq:diagonal-raw-schur-smallness}
\end{equation}
This is a fixed-reference, order-zero inverse on a receding spatial
collar.  It does not contain the moving-duration normal column, identify
the full RFDE terminal Schur coefficient, or provide a higher-order
diagonal inverse.
\end{corollary}

The preceding graph inverse is uniform in the order-zero phase norm, but
that norm does not resolve the causal phase displacement on the
\(\rho_\delta\)-scale.  The distinction is not cosmetic.  An arbitrary
normal datum of unit size can generate a speed direction whose accumulated
action is \(R_\delta^2\), whereas the terminal window has size only
\(\rho_\delta\).  We therefore record the action domain on which a
causal-entry inverse is actually uniform.

For the rest of this paragraph set
\begin{equation}
 R_\delta=\varkappa(\delta S_\delta^3)^{1/2},\qquad
 \lambda_\delta=R_\delta+S_\delta^{-2},\qquad
 M_\delta=R_\delta^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta},
 \qquad
 m_\delta(r)=|r|+\frac{\varepsilon}{|r|^2},       \label{eq:prefix-action-diagonal-scales}
\end{equation}
and use the receding intervals and oriented entry endpoints from
\cref{cor:diagonal-raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse}.  Thus
\((e_{a,0},e_{a,1})=(R_\delta,\rho_\delta)\) and
\((e_{r,0},e_{r,1})=(-\rho_\delta,-R_\delta)\).
For \(k\in C(I_\sigma)\), define its causal prefix action by
\begin{equation}
 (\mathcal P_\sigma k)(r)
 =\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r\frac{k(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u.          \label{eq:causal-prefix-action}
\end{equation}
Put
\(\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}=C(I_\sigma)\) and
\(\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}=C^1(I_\sigma)\), with Banach norms
\begin{align}
 \|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 &=\frac1{\lambda_\delta}
     \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|k(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}
   +\frac1{\rho_\delta}
     \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|(\mathcal P_\sigma k)(r)|,
                                                        \label{eq:prefix-action-source-norm}\\
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 &=\frac{\|a\|_{C^0(I_\sigma)}}{\rho_\delta}
   +\frac1{\lambda_\delta}
     \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|a'(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}.
                                                        \label{eq:prefix-action-phase-norm}
\end{align}
Here ``pa'' means prefix action.  For fixed \(\delta\) these are simply
weighted \(C^0\) and \(C^1\) spaces; the content is uniformity as the
collar recedes.
On this global weighted-speed class, the prefix summand is explicit
bookkeeping rather than an independent restriction.  Indeed,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\|\mathcal P_\sigma k\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\frac{\lambda_\delta M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
       \left\{\frac1{\lambda_\delta}
       \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|k|}{m_\delta}\right\}
 \le C_\varkappa\frac1{\lambda_\delta}
       \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|k|}{m_\delta}.       \label{eq:prefix-action-pointwise-domination}
\end{equation}
We retain it to expose the quantity consumed by a terminal row.  A later
source-split space permitting pointwise-large localized columns is not
constructed here.

\begin{lemma}[Causal-entry action-resolved core]
\label[lemma]{lem:causal-entry-prefix-action-core}
The operator
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}a
 =\bigl(q_0^2a',a(e_{\sigma,0})\bigr)             \label{eq:causal-entry-prefix-action-operator}
\end{equation}
is an isomorphism
\[
 \mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}
 \longrightarrow
 \mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\times\mathbb R.
\]
The scalar factor on the right carries the normalized norm
\(|b_0|/\rho_\delta\).
Its inverse is
\begin{equation}
 a(r)=b_0+(\mathcal P_\sigma k)(r),               \label{eq:causal-entry-prefix-action-inverse}
\end{equation}
and, uniformly in \(N,\delta\), and the branch,
\begin{equation}
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 \le C\left\{\|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
                    +\frac{|b_0|}{\rho_\delta}\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:causal-entry-prefix-action-bound}
\end{equation}
Conversely, the norm of the data in
\cref{eq:causal-entry-prefix-action-operator} is bounded by
\(C\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}\).
\end{lemma}

The fixed normal inverse determines which diagonal data families have
uniform weighted-speed/action size.  For each fixed \(\delta\) the sets
below coincide with the old continuous spaces; their graph norms, not their
underlying elements, carry the additional scale.  Put
\begin{align}
 \mathscr Q_\sigma f
 &=\mathfrak D_\sigma G_\sigma f,\notag\\
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}
 &=\left\{f\in\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}:
       \mathscr Q_\sigma f\in\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\right\},
                                                        \label{eq:prefix-action-normal-data-space}\\
 \|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 &=\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}
       +\|\mathscr Q_\sigma f\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}.
                                                        \label{eq:prefix-action-normal-data-norm}
\end{align}
Equivalently, restrict \(\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\) to
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{pa}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)
 =\left\{y\in\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma):
       \mathfrak D_\sigma y\in\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\right\}
                                                        \label{eq:prefix-action-normal-domain}
\end{equation}
and add \(\|\mathfrak D_\sigma y\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}\)
to its graph norm.

\begin{theorem}[Diagonal causal-entry weighted-action graph inverse]
\label[theorem]{thm:diagonal-causal-entry-prefix-action-inverse}
Retain
\cref{cor:diagonal-raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse,%
lem:causal-entry-prefix-action-core} and fix \(\varkappa>0\).  Define
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr L_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}(y,a)
 =\left(
   \mathscr N_\sigma y,
   q_0^2a'-\mathfrak D_\sigma y
                  -\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a,
   a(e_{\sigma,0})
   \right).                                      \label{eq:causal-entry-phase-normal-operator}
\end{equation}
Then, for all sufficiently small \(\delta\), this is an isomorphism
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{pa}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)
       \times\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}
 \longrightarrow
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}
       \times\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\times\mathbb R,
                                                        \label{eq:causal-entry-phase-normal-isomorphism}
\end{equation}
where the last scalar factor again carries the norm
\(|b_0|/\rho_\delta\),
with inverse norm independent of \(N,\delta\), and the branch.  More
precisely, for data \((f,k,b_0)\),
\begin{equation}
 \|y\|_{\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pa}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)}
      +\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 \le C\left\{
   \|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
   +\|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
   +\frac{|b_0|}{\rho_\delta}\right\}.         \label{eq:causal-entry-phase-normal-bound}
\end{equation}
The physical normal response reconstructed in the raw-compatible chart is
\(x=T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+y\).

The small operator in this inverse is estimated in the
weighted-speed/action norm.  If
\begin{equation}
 E_\delta=\varepsilon\delta^{-2\vartheta},\qquad
 \eta_{\mathrm{pa},\delta}
 =M_\delta+\frac{\rho_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
   +\frac{E_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
   +M_\delta\frac{E_\delta}{\rho_\delta},        \label{eq:prefix-action-schur-number}
\end{equation}
then
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 \le C_\varkappa\eta_{\mathrm{pa},\delta}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}},\qquad
 \eta_{\mathrm{pa},\delta}\longrightarrow0.    \label{eq:prefix-action-phase-column-smallness}
\end{equation}
Thus the theorem controls every causal prefix, not merely the total
terminal moment.  It is still a fixed-reference, order-zero theorem.  It
does not define a moving-duration normal column, provide a strong terminal
history, or close the physical endpoint Schur complement.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Terminal first-order trace of the raw-compatible phase column]
\label[proposition]{prop:raw-compatible-terminal-phase-column}
Retain
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green,%
prop:structured-phase-action-green,%
lem:raw-phase-boundary-column-assembly,%
thm:diagonal-causal-entry-prefix-action-inverse},
with the diagonal scales in
\cref{eq:prefix-action-diagonal-scales} and the terminal recuts in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-recut}.  For
\(a\in\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)\), write
\begin{equation}
 T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a
   =(\gamma_\sigma[a],Z_\sigma[a],p_\sigma[a]),\qquad
 P_\sigma[a]=\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,             \label{eq:terminal-phase-column-components}
\end{equation}
and, for \(0\le t\le L_b\), put
\begin{align}
 Z_{\sigma,T}[a](t)
 &=Z_\sigma[a](s_{\sigma,T}+t),\notag\\
 p_{\sigma,T}[a](t)
 &=p_\sigma[a](s_{\sigma,T}+t),\notag\\
 p_{\sigma,T}^{\sharp}[a](t)
 &=p_{\sigma,T}[a](t)
     +\varepsilon P_\sigma[a](s_{\sigma,T}+t).    \label{eq:terminal-phase-column-recut}
\end{align}
Then the shifted, raw-compatible terminal column satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \|(Z_{\sigma,T}[a],p_{\sigma,T}^{\sharp}[a])\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 \le C_{A,M}\|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}.  \label{eq:terminal-shifted-phase-column-bound}
\end{equation}
This is the uniform statement on the full action space; in general one
cannot replace \(p_{\sigma,T}^{\sharp}\) by the physical variable
\(p_{\sigma,T}\) on the attracting branch.

On the causal-entry phase space the physical column does satisfy a stronger
bound.  Uniformly in \(N,\delta\), and the branch,
\begin{equation}
 \|(Z_{\sigma,T}[a],p_{\sigma,T}[a])\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 \le C_{A,M}\rho_\delta
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 \qquad(a\in\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}).      \label{eq:terminal-physical-phase-column-bound}
\end{equation}
If
\(\widehat Q_\sigma^{\rm rc}[a]=\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\)
is the true reconstructed speed column in the reduced coordinate, put
\(\widehat Q_{\sigma,T}^{\rm rc}[a](t)
 =\widehat Q_\sigma^{\rm rc}[a](\bar r_{\sigma,T}(t))\).
Then
\begin{align}
 \sup_{0\le t\le L_b}
   |\widehat Q_{\sigma,T}^{\rm rc}[a](t)|
 &\le C_{A,M}\rho_\delta m_{\sigma,T}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}},\notag\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}\sup_{0\le t\le L_b}
 \left|\int_{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(0)}^{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(t)}
   \frac{\widehat Q_\sigma^{\rm rc}[a](u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u\right|
 &\le C_{A,M}\rho_\delta m_{\sigma,T}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}.  \label{eq:terminal-phase-column-local-action}
\end{align}

The additive entry-collar part is separately flushed.  If
\((Z_{\sigma,\rm col}[a],p_{\sigma,\rm col}[a])\) denotes the terminal
recut of
\(\mathfrak R_{\sigma,\rm col}E_{0,\sigma}a\), then
\begin{equation}
 \|(Z_{\sigma,\rm col}[a],p_{\sigma,\rm col}[a])\|_
  {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([L_b-\theta_m,L_b])}
 \le C_{A,M}\rho_\delta\mathcal E_{\sigma,T}(\delta)
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}.  \label{eq:terminal-phase-collar-flush}
\end{equation}
The proposition uses one phase derivative only.  It closes the structured
phase column in the fixed-reduced-path terminal norm, but it does not
construct the rough-middle-to-incoming trace, an endpoint RFDE reference,
the event shear \(K\), a moving-duration inverse, or a physical root.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Diagonal terminal-free normal data: prefix and terminal trace]
\label[proposition]{prop:diagonal-bulk-terminal-prefix-trace}
Retain
\cref{cor:diagonal-raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse,%
prop:normal-to-phase-action-coupling,%
prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green},
fix \(\varkappa,A>0\) and an integer \(M\ge2\), and use the scales and terminal
recuts in
\cref{eq:prefix-action-diagonal-scales,%
eq:terminal-component-scales,eq:terminal-component-recut}.
Let \(\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}\) be the space of
normal data
\(f=(\gamma_0,b_\sigma,F,H)\in
\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}\) such that
\begin{equation}
 F(s)=H(s)=0\quad(s_{\sigma,T}\le s\le S_\sigma),
 \qquad b_r=0\quad\hbox{if }\sigma=r.            \label{eq:bulk-terminal-free-data}
\end{equation}
Here \(\gamma_0\) denotes the prescribed history on the attracting branch
and the prescribed baseline history in the closed repelling-feedback
problem.  Give this space the direct data norm
\begin{align}
 \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}}
 &=\lambda_\delta^{-1}
       \|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}\notag\\
 &=\frac1{\varepsilon\lambda_\delta}
   \left\{
    \|\gamma_0\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^{\sharp}
    +\frac{|b_\sigma|}{R_\delta}
    +\sup_{0\le s\le S_\sigma}
       \left(\|F(s)\|_N
          +\frac{|H(s)|}{|\bar r_\sigma(s)|^2}\right)
   \right\}.                                    \label{eq:bulk-terminal-free-data-norm}
\end{align}
Thus the norm is specified before applying a solution operator; it contains
neither a reconstructed speed nor a returned trace.

For \(f\in\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}\), write
\begin{equation}
 y_f=(\gamma_f,Z_f,p_f)=G_\sigma f,\qquad
 Q_f=\mathfrak D_\sigma y_f,                     \label{eq:bulk-terminal-returned-normal}
\end{equation}
where \(Q_f\) is pulled back to the reduced coordinate.  Define
\begin{align}
 \Pi_{\sigma,f}(r)
 &=\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r\frac{Q_f(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u,\notag\\
 \Gamma_{\sigma,T}f(\theta)
 &=Z_f(s_{\sigma,T}+\theta),\qquad
                    -\theta_m\le\theta\le0,\notag\\
 b_{a,T}(f)&=p_f(s_{a,T}),\qquad
 b_{r,T}(f)=p_f(S_r)=0,                           \label{eq:bulk-terminal-interface-data}\\
 (Z_{\sigma,T}[f],p_{\sigma,T}[f])(t)
 &=(Z_f,p_f)(s_{\sigma,T}+t),\qquad 0\le t\le L_b,\notag\\
 Q_{\sigma,T}[f](t)
 &=Q_f(\bar r_{\sigma,T}(t)).                    \label{eq:bulk-terminal-recut-data}
\end{align}

For all sufficiently small \(\delta\), with threshold allowed to depend
on the displayed fixed choices, the following estimates are uniform in
\(N\), the branch, and the retained parameters.  First,
\begin{equation}
 \|y_f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 \le C_{\varkappa,A,M}\lambda_\delta
        \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}}.
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-global-normal-bound}
\end{equation}
The speed and every causal prefix satisfy the direct estimate
\begin{equation}
 \frac1{\lambda_\delta}
   \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|Q_f(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}
 +\frac1{\rho_\delta}
   \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|\Pi_{\sigma,f}(r)|
 \le C_{\varkappa,A,M}
       \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}}.
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-direct-prefix-bound}
\end{equation}
Consequently, rather than by hypothesis,
\begin{equation}
 f\in\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}},\qquad
 \|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 \le C_{\varkappa,A,M}
       \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}}.
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-direct-graph-embedding}
\end{equation}

The incoming terminal data have their natural small size,
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\Gamma_{\sigma,T}f\|_{C^0}
    +\frac{|b_{\sigma,T}(f)|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}
 \le C_{\varkappa,A,M}\lambda_\delta
       \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}},
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-terminal-interface-bound}
\end{equation}
and their propagated terminal trace obeys
\begin{align}
 \|(Z_{\sigma,T}[f],p_{\sigma,T}[f])\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 &\le C_{\varkappa,A,M}\lambda_\delta
       \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}},
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-terminal-full-trace-bound}\\
 \|(Z_{\sigma,T}[f],p_{\sigma,T}[f])\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([L_b-\theta_m,L_b])}
 &\le C_{\varkappa,A,M}\lambda_\delta
       \mathcal E_{\sigma,T}(\delta)
       \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}}.
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-terminal-final-flush}
\end{align}
The corresponding local speed and phase increment satisfy
\begin{align}
 \sup_{0\le t\le L_b}|Q_{\sigma,T}[f](t)|
 &\le C_{\varkappa,A,M}\lambda_\delta m_{\sigma,T}
       \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}},\notag\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}\sup_{0\le t\le L_b}
 \left|\int_{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(0)}^{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(t)}
        \frac{Q_f(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u\right|
 &\le C_{\varkappa,A,M}\lambda_\delta m_{\sigma,T}
       \|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}}.
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-terminal-local-action}
\end{align}

The normalized global prefix in
\cref{eq:bulk-direct-prefix-bound} is bounded but is not asserted to tend
to zero: the upper-bound ledger contains the nonvanishing coefficient
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\lambda_\delta R_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}
 =2\alpha\varkappa^2+o(1).                       \label{eq:bulk-prefix-nonsmall-scale}
\end{equation}
This proposition closes the fixed-path, first-order terminal trace of
\(\lambda_\delta\)-small old-normal data whose sources vanish on the
terminal buffer.  A nonzero repelling terminal row is a distinct localized
column and cannot satisfy
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-final-flush}.  No estimate here covers an arbitrary
unit rough datum, a localized terminal endpoint/event column, the event shear \(K\),
a moving-duration or full hybrid inverse, an endpoint RFDE family,
parameter or higher jets, a nonlinear tracker, a past-complete connection,
or a physical root.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Diagonal localized boundary columns: causal action and terminal trace]
\label[proposition]{prop:diagonal-boundary-prefix-terminal-trace}
Retain
\cref{cor:diagonal-raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse,%
prop:normal-to-phase-action-coupling,%
prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green,%
prop:physical-backtrack-calculus}
with the coefficient and backtrack estimates through order two, fix
\(\varkappa,A>0\) and \(M\ge2\), and use the diagonal
scales and terminal recuts in
\cref{eq:prefix-action-diagonal-scales,%
eq:terminal-component-scales,eq:terminal-component-recut}.  For either
branch let
\begin{equation}
 f_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}=(\gamma_0,b_\sigma,0,0),\qquad
 D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}
 =\varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\gamma_0\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^{\sharp}
       +\frac{|b_\sigma|}{R_\delta}\right\}.     \label{eq:boundary-column-direct-norm}
\end{equation}
Here \(b_a=p(0)\), \(b_r=p(S_r)\),
\(e_a=R_\delta\), and \(e_r=-\rho_\delta\).  On the repelling branch
\(\gamma_0\) is the prescribed baseline history before the compatible
\(p(0)\)-feedback is closed.  Thus the norm in
\cref{eq:boundary-column-direct-norm} is specified entirely by the input
data and not by the returned history or speed.

Write
\begin{equation}
 y_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}
   =(\gamma_\sigma,Z_\sigma,p_\sigma)
   =G_\sigma f_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},\qquad
 Q_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}=\mathfrak D_\sigma y_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},
 \qquad
 \Pi_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}(r)
   =\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r
       \frac{Q_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u .      \label{eq:boundary-column-returned-data}
\end{equation}
On the repelling branch split the solution linearly as
\begin{equation}
 y_r^{\rm bd}=y_r^{\rm in}+y_r^{\rm ter},\qquad
 \mathscr N_ry_r^{\rm in}=(\gamma_0,0,0,0),\qquad
 \mathscr N_ry_r^{\rm ter}=(0,b_r,0,0),          \label{eq:boundary-column-repelling-split}
\end{equation}
where both columns include the compatible collar feedback.  Put
\begin{equation}
 \eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}
 =\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}
    \left\{1+\log\frac{R_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\right\},
 \qquad
 M_\delta=R_\delta^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}.
                                                        \label{eq:boundary-column-small-numbers}
\end{equation}
Then, for all sufficiently small \(\delta\), uniformly in \(N\), the
branch, and the retained parameters,
\begin{align}
 \|y_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 &\le C D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},\notag\\
 \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}
     \frac{|Q_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}
 &\le C D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},\notag\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}
   \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|\Pi_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}(r)|
 &\le C\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}.          \label{eq:boundary-column-global-action}
\end{align}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
 \eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}=O(\delta S_\delta)=o(1).             \label{eq:boundary-column-action-smallness}
\end{equation}
In particular, the causal phase lift
\begin{equation}
 a_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}=\Pi_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},\qquad
 q_0^2(a_{\sigma}^{\rm bd})'=Q_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},\qquad
 a_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}(e_{\sigma,0})=0              \label{eq:boundary-column-causal-phase-lift}
\end{equation}
satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \|a_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}
 \le C M_\delta D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},\qquad
 \frac{\|a_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}\|_{C^0}}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}.          \label{eq:boundary-column-phase-lift-bound}
\end{equation}

Let \(\Gamma_{\sigma,T}^{\rm bd}\),
\(b_{\sigma,T}^{\rm bd}\), and the terminal recut of
\((Z_\sigma,p_\sigma,Q_\sigma^{\rm bd})\) be defined as in
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-interface-data,eq:bulk-terminal-recut-data}, except
that \(b_{r,T}^{\rm bd}=b_r\).  Then
\begin{align}
 \varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\Gamma_{\sigma,T}^{\rm bd}\|_{C^0}
      +\frac{|b_{\sigma,T}^{\rm bd}|}{m_{\sigma,T}}
                    \right\}
 &\le C D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},\notag\\
 \|(Z_{\sigma,T}^{\rm bd},p_{\sigma,T}^{\rm bd})\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 &\le C D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd},\notag\\
 \sup_{0\le t\le L_b}|Q_{\sigma,T}^{\rm bd}(t)|
 +\frac1{\rho_\delta}\sup_{0\le t\le L_b}
  \left|\int_{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(0)}^{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(t)}
       \frac{Q_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u\right|
 &\le C m_{\sigma,T}D_{\sigma}^{\rm bd}.        \label{eq:boundary-column-terminal-trace}
\end{align}
The boundary columns which do not carry a repelling terminal scalar row
are flushed from the final history window:
\begin{align}
 \|(Z_{a,T}^{\rm bd},p_{a,T}^{\rm bd})\|_
       {\mathsf T_{a,T}^1([L_b-\theta_m,L_b])}
 &\le C\mathcal E_{a,T}(\delta)D_a^{\rm bd},\notag\\
 \|(Z_{r,T}^{\rm in},p_{r,T}^{\rm in})\|_
       {\mathsf T_{r,T}^1([L_b-\theta_m,L_b])}
 &\le C\mathcal E_{r,T}(\delta)
       \varepsilon^{-1}
       \|\gamma_0\|_{N,-\rho_\delta,\delta}^{\sharp}.       \label{eq:boundary-column-final-flush}
\end{align}
The terminal scalar column does not flush.  Indeed,
\begin{equation}
 \|(Z_{r,T}^{\rm ter},p_{r,T}^{\rm ter})\|_
       {\mathsf T_{r,T}^1([L_b-\theta_m,L_b])}
 \ge \frac{|b_r|}{\varepsilon m_{r,T}}
 \ge c\frac{|b_r|}{\varepsilon R_\delta}.       \label{eq:boundary-terminal-column-nonflush}
\end{equation}

The small action in \cref{eq:boundary-column-global-action} is special to
the zero distributed scalar residual \(H=0\).  It does not contradict
\cref{prop:rough-normal-prefix-action-obstruction}, whose witness has a
nonzero distributed \(H\)-row.  This proposition supplies the global
localized boundary lifts, including the repelling terminal scalar column.
It does not turn a retained terminal voltage history into a freely
prescribed boundary row, construct a source-split phase--normal inverse,
bound the event shear of a nonzero endpoint reference, or produce a
moving-duration family, a complete-history connection, or a physical root.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Terminal-supported distributed Green extension]
\label[proposition]{prop:diagonal-terminal-source-extension}
Retain
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green,%
prop:diagonal-boundary-prefix-terminal-trace}
and the terminal scales and recuts in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-scales,eq:terminal-component-recut}.  For a
Banach space \(X\), write
\[
 L^\infty_{\rm pw}(I_\sigma;s_{\sigma,T};X)
 =L^\infty((0,s_{\sigma,T});X)
   \oplus L^\infty((s_{\sigma,T},S_\sigma);X).
\]
Put
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}
 =\left\{f_T=(0,0,F_T,H_T):
 \begin{array}{l}
   F_T\in L^\infty_{\rm pw}(I_\sigma;s_{\sigma,T};E_N),\\
   H_T\in L^\infty_{\rm pw}(I_\sigma;s_{\sigma,T};\mathbb R),\\
   (F_T,H_T)=0\ \text{a.e. on }[0,s_{\sigma,T})
 \end{array}\right\}.                                      \label{eq:terminal-source-space}
\end{equation}
and give this space the direct input norm
\begin{equation}
 \|f_T\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}}
 =\frac1\varepsilon
   \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{s_{\sigma,T}<s<S_\sigma}
   \left\{\|F_T(s)\|_N
       +\frac{|H_T(s)|}{r_{\sigma,T}m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}.    \label{eq:terminal-source-direct-norm}
\end{equation}
This norm is specified before solving the normal problem.

For a response \(y=(\gamma_y,Z_y,p_y)\), define the branchwise state norm
\begin{align}
 \|y\|_{\mathsf X_{\sigma,T}^{\rm pw}}
 =\varepsilon^{-1}\bigg\{&
   \|\gamma_y\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^{\sharp}
 +\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0<s<s_{\sigma,T}}
     \left(\|Z_y(s)\|_N+
       \frac{|p_y(s)|}{|\bar r_\sigma(s)|}\right)\notag\\
 &+\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{s_{\sigma,T}<s<S_\sigma}
     \left(\|Z_y(s)\|_N+
       \frac{|p_y(s)|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right)\bigg\}.          \label{eq:terminal-source-state-norm}
\end{align}
On the repelling branch \(\gamma_y\) is the actual history after closing
the compatible collar feedback; its prescribed baseline history is zero.

Let \(\mathscr N_\sigma^\infty\) denote the boundary-residual operator in
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-boundary-operator}, with its differential rows
interpreted a.e. for piecewise-\(L^\infty\) sources.  The component Green
formula defines a solution operator
\begin{equation}
 G_{\sigma,T}^{\infty}:
 \mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}
 \longrightarrow\mathsf X_{\sigma,T}^{\rm pw},              \label{eq:terminal-source-green-extension}
\end{equation}
