whose response is continuous, piecewise \(W^{1,\infty}\), and agrees with
\(G_\sigma\) for continuous sources.  It is the unique continuous,
piecewise-\(W^{1,\infty}\) response with zero prescribed baseline history,
zero attracting initial scalar row or repelling future scalar row, and
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr N_\sigma^\infty y_T=f_T.                         \label{eq:terminal-source-ae-normal-equation}
\end{equation}
If
\(
y_T=G_{\sigma,T}^{\infty}f_T
\),
\(
Q_T=\mathfrak D_\sigma y_T
\), and
\begin{equation}
 \Pi_{\sigma,T}(r)
 =\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r\frac{Q_T(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u,       \label{eq:terminal-source-causal-primitive}
\end{equation}
then, uniformly in the dimension, the branch, and the diagonal parameters,
\begin{align}
 \|y_T\|_{\mathsf X_{\sigma,T}^{\rm pw}}
 &\le C\|f_T\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}},
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-source-state-bound}\\
 \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|Q_T(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}
 &\le C\|f_T\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}},
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-source-speed-bound}\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|\Pi_{\sigma,T}(r)|
 &\le C m_{\sigma,T}
       \|f_T\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}}.     \label{eq:terminal-source-action-bound}
\end{align}
Moreover, its incoming data and full terminal recut satisfy
\begin{align}
 &\varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\Gamma_{\sigma,T}y_T\|_{C^0}
   +\frac{|b_{\sigma,T}(y_T)|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}
 \le C\|f_T\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}},
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-source-interface-bound}\\
 &\|(Z_{\sigma,T}[y_T],p_{\sigma,T}[y_T])\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 \le C\|f_T\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}}.     \label{eq:terminal-source-trace-bound}
\end{align}
Here
\begin{equation}
 m_{a,T}=\frac{4\alpha^2}{S_\delta^2}(1+o(1)),
 \qquad
 m_{r,T}=R_\delta(1+o(1)),                       \label{eq:terminal-source-small-factors}
\end{equation}
so the action factor in \cref{eq:terminal-source-action-bound} vanishes on
both branches.

The factor \(m_{\sigma,T}\) belongs only to the causal action.  In
general neither the whole state norm nor the terminal \(\mathsf T^1\)
trace is \(O(m_{\sigma,T})\).  On the attracting branch an arbitrary
terminal scalar source also need not embed uniformly into the old global
state norm, which is why \cref{eq:terminal-source-state-norm} uses the
terminal scale after the recut.  No final-window flushing is asserted for
a source which remains active up to the endpoint.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Terminal-free piecewise-\(L^\infty\) bulk extension]
\label[proposition]{prop:diagonal-bulk-pw-extension}
Retain
\cref{prop:diagonal-bulk-terminal-prefix-trace,%
prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green}
and the interface time \(s_{\sigma,T}\) in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-recut}.  Let
\(\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,\mathrm{tf},\infty}\)
consist of the pure distributed data
\begin{equation}
 f_{\rm B}=(0,0,F_{\rm B},H_{\rm B})              \label{eq:bulk-pw-data}
\end{equation}
for which
\begin{align*}
 F_{\rm B}&\in
 L^\infty_{\rm pw}(I_\sigma;s_{\sigma,T};E_N),&
 H_{\rm B}&\in
 L^\infty_{\rm pw}(I_\sigma;s_{\sigma,T};\mathbb R),
\end{align*}
and
\begin{equation}
 (F_{\rm B},H_{\rm B})=0
 \quad\text{a.e. on }(s_{\sigma,T},S_\sigma).     \label{eq:bulk-pw-terminal-free}
\end{equation}
Give this space the direct norm
\begin{equation}
 \|f_{\rm B}\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,
             \mathrm{tf},\infty}}
 =\frac1{\varepsilon\lambda_\delta}
  \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0<s<s_{\sigma,T}}
  \left\{\|F_{\rm B}(s)\|_N
       +\frac{|H_{\rm B}(s)|}{|\bar r_\sigma(s)|^2}\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-pw-direct-norm}
\end{equation}
The component Green formula defines a unique continuous,
piecewise-\(W^{1,\infty}\) response
\begin{equation}
 G_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\infty}f_{\rm B}
 =y_{\rm B}=(\gamma_{\rm B},Z_{\rm B},p_{\rm B}) \label{eq:bulk-pw-green}
\end{equation}
with zero finite normal row and
\(\mathscr N_\sigma^\infty y_{\rm B}=f_{\rm B}\) a.e.  On the
repelling branch the first zero denotes the zero prescribed baseline
history after closing the compatible feedback; the actual history is the
one generated by that feedback.  The operator agrees with \(G_\sigma\)
whenever the source is continuous.

All conclusions of
\cref{prop:diagonal-bulk-terminal-prefix-trace} for pure distributed data
remain valid with essential suprema.  In particular, if
\(Q_{\rm B}=\mathfrak D_\sigma y_{\rm B}\) and
\(\Pi_{\sigma,\rm B}=\mathcal P_\sigma Q_{\rm B}\), then
\begin{align}
 \|y_{\rm B}\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 &\le C\lambda_\delta\|f_{\rm B}\|,
                                                        \label{eq:bulk-pw-global-bound}\\
 \frac1{\lambda_\delta}
   \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{I_\sigma}
      \frac{|Q_{\rm B}|}{m_\delta}
 +\frac1{\rho_\delta}
   \sup_{I_\sigma}|\Pi_{\sigma,\rm B}|
 &\le C\|f_{\rm B}\|,                           \label{eq:bulk-pw-prefix-bound}\\
 \varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\Gamma_{\sigma,T}y_{\rm B}\|_{C^0}
   +\frac{|b_{\sigma,T}(y_{\rm B})|}{m_{\sigma,T}}
                  \right\}
 +\|(Z_{\sigma,T},p_{\sigma,T})[y_{\rm B}]\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 &\le C\lambda_\delta\|f_{\rm B}\|.           \label{eq:bulk-pw-terminal-bound}
\end{align}
Moreover the final-window flushing and local speed/action estimates in
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-final-flush,eq:bulk-terminal-local-action}
hold with the same right-hand side and with essential suprema where
appropriate.

This is a direct a.e. mixed-problem extension.  It is not inferred from
density of continuous sources in \(L^\infty\).  Its purpose is to permit an
exact hard split of a smooth nonlinear source at
\(s_{\sigma,T}\); it does not yet prove that a particular nonlinear
source has the required normalized size.
\end{proposition}

\paragraph{The source-split causal core.}
The preceding two propositions have deliberately different scales.  To keep
them distinct, fix \(\varkappa,A>0\) and an integer \(M\ge2\).  All constants
and smallness thresholds in this paragraph may depend on these fixed choices.
Define the pure distributed bulk space
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}
 =\{(0,0,F,H)\in
       \mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}\}     \label{eq:source-split-pure-bulk}
\end{equation}
with the inherited norm, and define
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{\mathrm{bd}}
 =\{(\gamma_0,b_\sigma,0,0)
       \in\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}\},\qquad
 \|(\gamma_0,b_\sigma,0,0)\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{\mathrm{bd}}}
 =\varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\gamma_0\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^{\sharp}
       +\frac{|b_\sigma|}{R_\delta}\right\}.       \label{eq:source-split-boundary-space}
\end{equation}
The normal range is the algebraic direct sum
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}}
 =\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}
       \oplus\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{\mathrm{bd}},
 \qquad
 \|f_{\rm B}+f_{\rm bd}\|_{\mathrm{split}}
 =\|f_{\rm B}\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}}
   +\|f_{\rm bd}\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{\mathrm{bd}}}. \label{eq:source-split-normal-range}
\end{equation}
This splitting is unique because the first summand has zero history and zero
finite normal rows, while the second has zero distributed rows.  In
particular, its norm is specified before applying \(G_\sigma\).

Define the unscaled hybrid phase spaces
\(
 \mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}=C(I_\sigma)
\)
and
\(
 \mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}=C^1(I_\sigma)
\)
by
\begin{align}
 \|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 &=\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|k(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}
   +\frac1{\rho_\delta}
       \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|(\mathcal P_\sigma k)(r)|,
                                                        \label{eq:source-split-hybrid-H}\\
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 &=\frac{\|a\|_{C^0(I_\sigma)}}{\rho_\delta}
   +\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}
       \frac{|q_0(r)^2a'(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}.        \label{eq:source-split-hybrid-A}
\end{align}
The prefix term in \(\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}\) is not controlled by
its pointwise term uniformly; it retains the rough-source obstruction below.
The final scalar factor \(\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}\) is given the norm
\(|b|/\rho_\delta\).

\begin{lemma}[Hybrid causal-entry phase core]
\label[lemma]{lem:source-split-hybrid-phase-core}
Retain
\cref{prop:structured-phase-action-green,%
lem:raw-phase-boundary-column-assembly,%
eq:prefix-action-diagonal-scales} and put
\begin{equation}
 E_\delta=\varepsilon\delta^{-2\vartheta},\qquad
 \eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}
 =M_\delta+\frac{M_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}
       +E_\delta+\frac{M_\delta E_\delta}{\rho_\delta}.
                                                        \label{eq:source-split-hybrid-small-number}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}a
 =\bigl(q_0^2a',a(e_{\sigma,0})\bigr)             \label{eq:source-split-hybrid-phase-operator}
\end{equation}
is a dimension- and diagonal-uniform isomorphism
\[
 \mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
 \longrightarrow
 \mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}\times\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta},
 \qquad
 a(r)=b_0+(\mathcal P_\sigma k)(r).
\]
Moreover,
\begin{align}
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}
 &\le C\{\rho_\delta+M_\delta\}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}},    \label{eq:source-split-hybrid-A1-embedding}\\
 \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\|_
       {\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 &\le C\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}},
 \qquad
 \eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}=o(1).                \label{eq:source-split-hybrid-phase-smallness}
\end{align}
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Diagonal source-split causal-entry square core]
\label[theorem]{thm:diagonal-source-split-hybrid-core}
Retain
\cref{cor:diagonal-raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse,%
prop:diagonal-bulk-terminal-prefix-trace,%
prop:diagonal-boundary-prefix-terminal-trace,%
prop:raw-compatible-terminal-phase-column,%
lem:source-split-hybrid-phase-core}.
Put
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)
 =G_\sigma\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}},
 \qquad
 \|y\|_{\mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}}}
 =\|\mathscr N_\sigma y\|_{\mathrm{split}}.       \label{eq:source-split-normal-domain}
\end{equation}
Then, for all sufficiently small \(\delta\),
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr L_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}^{\mathrm{split}}(y,a)
 =\left(
   \mathscr N_\sigma y,\,
   q_0^2a'-\mathfrak D_\sigma y
             -\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a,\,
   a(e_{\sigma,0})\right)                        \label{eq:source-split-square-operator}
\end{equation}
is an isomorphism
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)
       \times\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
 \longrightarrow
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}}
       \times\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
       \times\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}              \label{eq:source-split-square-isomorphism}
\end{equation}
with inverse norm independent of \(N,\delta\), and the branch.  For data
\((f,k,b_0)\), its inverse is
\begin{align}
 y&=G_\sigma f,\notag\\
 a&=\left\{\mathscr B_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
       -(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc},0)\right\}^{-1}
       (k+\mathfrak D_\sigma y,b_0),              \label{eq:source-split-square-inverse}\\
 x&=T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+y. \notag
\end{align}

The terminal history is a bounded observation, not an additional onto row.
Indeed, if \(f=f_{\rm B}+f_{\rm bd}\), the terminal recuts below,
interpreted by linear extension of the definitions in
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-interface-data,eq:bulk-terminal-recut-data}, satisfy
\begin{align}
 &\varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\Gamma_{\sigma,T}y\|_{C^0}
      +\frac{|b_{\sigma,T}(y)|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}
 +\|(Z_{\sigma,T}[y],p_{\sigma,T}[y])\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}\notag\\
 &\quad
 +\frac{\|Q_{\sigma,T}[y]\|_{C^0}}{m_{\sigma,T}}
 +\frac1{\rho_\delta m_{\sigma,T}}
   \sup_{0\le t\le L_b}
   \left|\int_{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(0)}^{\bar r_{\sigma,T}(t)}
       \frac{\mathfrak D_\sigma y(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u\right|\notag\\
 &\le C\left\{
     \lambda_\delta\|f_{\rm B}\|_
       {\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}}
       +\|f_{\rm bd}\|_{\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{\mathrm{bd}}}
       \right\}.                                 \label{eq:source-split-terminal-observation}
\end{align}
For the reconstructed response, put
\(
 p_x^\sharp=p_x+\varepsilon\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a
\).
Then
\begin{equation}
 \|(Z_{x,T},p_{x,T}^\sharp)\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 +\|(Z_{x,T},p_{x,T})\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 \le C\left\{
   \lambda_\delta\|f_{\rm B}\|
   +\|f_{\rm bd}\|
   +(\rho_\delta+M_\delta)
        \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}\right\}.     \label{eq:source-split-reconstructed-terminal-observation}
\end{equation}
Finally,
\begin{equation}
 \ell_{\sigma,\mathrm{ter}}(y,a)=a(e_{\sigma,1})             \label{eq:source-split-terminal-event-row}
\end{equation}
is a bounded scalar observation into \(\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}\).

This theorem closes the square causal-entry core for terminal-free
distributed bulk plus zero-source boundary columns.  The distinct
terminal-source extension is stated next.  Neither result makes an arbitrary
final voltage history a boundary condition, verifies the endpoint shear of a
nonzero physical reference, constructs a moving-duration family, or proves a
physical root.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Three-summand causal core with terminal sources]
\label[corollary]{cor:terminal-source-split-hybrid-core}
Retain
\cref{prop:diagonal-bulk-pw-extension,%
prop:diagonal-terminal-source-extension,%
thm:diagonal-source-split-hybrid-core}.
Because the bulk sources vanish on the terminal buffer and terminal sources
vanish before it, the a.e. support decomposition
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}+T}
 =\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,\mathrm{tf},\infty}
   \mathbin{\widehat\oplus_1}
     \mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{\mathrm{bd}}
   \mathbin{\widehat\oplus_1}
     \mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}                 \label{eq:terminal-source-three-summand-range}
\end{equation}
is unique.  Extend the Green operator linearly by
\begin{equation}
 G_\sigma^\infty(f_{\rm B}+f_{\rm bd}+f_T)
 =G_{\sigma,\rm B}^\infty f_{\rm B}
   +G_\sigma f_{\rm bd}+G_{\sigma,T}^\infty f_T,          \label{eq:terminal-source-extended-green}
\end{equation}
and let
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}+T}(\mathscr N_\sigma)
 =G_\sigma^\infty
    \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}+T}        \label{eq:terminal-source-extended-domain}
\end{equation}
carry the transported three-summand norm.  Then
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr L_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}^{\mathrm{split}+T}(y,a)
 =\left(
   \mathscr N_\sigma^\infty y,
   q_0^2a'-\mathfrak D_\sigma y
      -\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a,
   a(e_{\sigma,0})\right)                                  \label{eq:terminal-source-extended-square-operator}
\end{equation}
is a dimension- and diagonal-uniform isomorphism from
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}+T}(\mathscr N_\sigma)
       \times\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
 \quad\hbox{onto}\quad
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}+T}
       \times\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
       \times\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}.                     \label{eq:terminal-source-extended-isomorphism}
\end{equation}
Its inverse is still
\begin{equation}
 y=G_\sigma^\infty f,\qquad
 a=\left\{\mathscr B_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
       -(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc},0)\right\}^{-1}
       (k+\mathfrak D_\sigma y,b_0).                        \label{eq:terminal-source-extended-inverse}
\end{equation}

More precisely, writing
\(
f=f_{\rm B}+f_{\rm bd}+f_T
\), one has
\begin{align}
 \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|\mathfrak D_\sigma y|}{m_\delta}
 &\le C\{\lambda_\delta\|f_{\rm B}\|
       +\|f_{\rm bd}\|+\|f_T\|\},\notag\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}\sup_{I_\sigma}
       |\mathcal P_\sigma\mathfrak D_\sigma y|
 &\le C\{\|f_{\rm B}\|
       +\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}\|f_{\rm bd}\|
       +m_{\sigma,T}\|f_T\|\}.                           \label{eq:terminal-source-returned-action-ledger}
\end{align}
The left-hand side of the terminal observation in
\cref{eq:source-split-terminal-observation} is bounded by
\begin{equation}
 C\{\lambda_\delta\|f_{\rm B}\|
       +\|f_{\rm bd}\|+\|f_T\|\}.                         \label{eq:terminal-source-extended-observation}
\end{equation}
For a pure terminal source and zero phase and entry data, the returned event
observation has the sharper bound
\begin{equation}
 \frac{|\ell_{\sigma,\mathrm{ter}}
   (\mathscr L_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}^{\mathrm{split}+T})^{-1}
       (f_T,0,0)|}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\{m_{\sigma,T}+\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}\}
       \|f_T\|
 \le C m_{\sigma,T}\|f_T\|.                              \label{eq:terminal-source-event-smallness}
\end{equation}
All unlabeled norms in these three displays are the corresponding summand
norms in \cref{eq:terminal-source-three-summand-range}.

This is an \(L^\infty\)-source extension of the fixed-envelope linear core.
It does not by itself prove that the nonlinear affine residual belongs to
this range, close a nonlinear two-point estimate, move the terminal event,
or construct a physical endpoint reference or root.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[Speed-aligned repelling terminal row]
\label[proposition]{prop:repelling-speed-aligned-terminal-row}
Retain
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green,%
lem:repelling-component-generations,%
prop:diagonal-boundary-prefix-terminal-trace,%
prop:endpoint-normal-shear-identity,%
prop:fold-time-tracker-normal-form,%
lem:endpoint-centered-duration-selector}
and put \(e_r=-R_\delta\).  If
\(y=(\gamma_y,Z_y,p_y)\) belongs to the repelling fixed-phase normal
domain, extend \(Z_y\) by its actual old history and define
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_{r}^{\rm sp}y
 =p_y(S_r)-\ell_N(e_r)Z_y(S_r)
   +\varepsilon\mathscr D_{c,N}^{\rm lin}
       [\gamma_y,Z_y](S_r).
                                                        \label{eq:speed-aligned-linear-row}
\end{equation}
Thus, exactly,
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}y
 =\varepsilon Q_y(e_r).                          \label{eq:speed-aligned-linear-speed}
\end{equation}
Give its scalar range the same normalization as the old future row,
\(\|b_{\rm sp}\|_{\mathbb R_{\varepsilon R_\delta}}
=|b_{\rm sp}|/(\varepsilon R_\delta)\).  Then
\begin{equation}
 \frac{|\mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}y|}{\varepsilon R_\delta}
 \le C\|y\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_r)}.          \label{eq:speed-aligned-row-bound}
\end{equation}

Let
\(\mathfrak u_r^{\rm ter}b=G_r(0,b,0,0)\) be the closed repelling
terminal scalar column, including its compatible entry feedback, and set
\begin{equation}
 \chi_{r,\delta}
 =\mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}\mathfrak u_r^{\rm ter}1,
 \qquad
 \omega_{r,\delta}^{\rm sp}
 =R_\delta^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta}
       +\mathcal E_{r,T}(\delta).                \label{eq:speed-aligned-row-coefficient}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 |\chi_{r,\delta}-1|
 \le C\omega_{r,\delta}^{\rm sp}=o(1).          \label{eq:speed-aligned-row-margin}
\end{equation}
Consequently, after replacing the future row \(p_y(S_r)\) by
\(\mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}y\), the repelling normal operator remains a
dimension- and diagonal-uniform isomorphism.  More explicitly, if
\(y_0=G_r(\gamma_0,0,F,H)\), the solution with speed-row datum
\(b_{\rm sp}\) is
\begin{equation}
 y=y_0+\mathfrak u_r^{\rm ter}
   \frac{b_{\rm sp}-\mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}y_0}
        {\chi_{r,\delta}}.                       \label{eq:speed-aligned-row-inverse}
\end{equation}
The same row replacement is valid on every source-split restriction of the
normal range for which the old inverse and the terminal observation are
bounded.

For the exact nonlinear tracker, let a supplied classical selector envelope
satisfy
\begin{equation}
 r_*(S_*)=e_r,\qquad
 q_{*,e}=\delta^{-1}r_*'(S_*)\ne0,\qquad
 c_{\rm ter}=\frac{q_{*,e}}{q_0(e_r)},                      \label{eq:speed-aligned-selector-normalization}
\end{equation}
and use the original-endpoint row and normalization of
\cref{lem:endpoint-centered-duration-selector}.  Define
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_{r}^{\rm sp,nl}(r,Z,p)
 =p_e-\ell_N(e_r)Z_e-h_{c,N}(e_r,Z_e)
       +\varepsilon\Delta\mathcal D_{c,N}^{\rm ft}(e_r).
                                                        \label{eq:speed-aligned-nonlinear-row}
\end{equation}
By \cref{eq:fold-time-speed-reconstruction}, this is the exact identity
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_r^{\rm sp,nl}=\varepsilon Q_e.       \label{eq:speed-aligned-nonlinear-speed}
\end{equation}
Hence any classical moving-endpoint RFDE zero satisfying
\(\mathscr B_r^{\rm sp,nl}=0\) has
\begin{equation}
 Q_e=0,
 \qquad q_{*,e}=q_0(e_r),
 \qquad c_{\rm ter}=1,
 \qquad K_p=R_{q,N}(e_r),                        \label{eq:speed-aligned-endpoint-consequences}
\end{equation}
and therefore
\begin{equation}
 |K_p|\le C\varepsilon,
 \qquad
 \kappa_{p,\delta}\le C\frac{\rho_\delta}{R_\delta}=o(1).
                                                        \label{eq:speed-aligned-endpoint-shear}
\end{equation}
This is a uniformly transverse choice of the one future scalar row, not the
construction of its nonlinear zero.  It removes the scalar endpoint-shear
hypothesis once such a zero has been obtained, but it neither supplies the
terminal \(Z\)-jet in
\cref{eq:terminal-history-slow-jet-gate} nor constructs a past orbit or a
physical root.
\end{proposition}


\begin{theorem}[Speed-bordered phase--normal inverse and affine corrector]
\label[theorem]{thm:speed-bordered-affine-endpoint-core}
Retain
\cref{cor:terminal-source-split-hybrid-core,%
prop:repelling-speed-aligned-terminal-row}
on the receding diagonal.  On the attracting branch put
\(\mathscr N_a^{\rm sp,\infty}=\mathscr N_a^\infty\).  On the repelling
branch replace the future scalar component of
\(\mathscr N_r^\infty\) by the speed row in
\cref{eq:speed-aligned-linear-row} and write
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr N_r^{\rm sp,\infty}y
 =\bigl(\gamma_y-\mathfrak C_ep_y(0),
          \mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}y,F_y,H_y\bigr).             \label{eq:speed-bordered-normal-operator}
\end{equation}
The scalar datum in the second slot retains the norm
\(|b|/(\varepsilon R_\delta)\).  Let
\(G_\sigma^{\rm sp,\infty}\) be the corresponding Green operator; on the
repelling branch it is given by the rank-one formula
\cref{eq:speed-aligned-row-inverse}, applied separately to the three source
summands.
Define
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}+T}
       (\mathscr N_\sigma^{\rm sp})
 =G_\sigma^{\rm sp,\infty}
       \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}+T}       \label{eq:speed-bordered-normal-domain}
\end{equation}
with the transported three-summand norm.

The old phase column must also be projected into the kernel of the new
speed row.  On the attracting branch set
\begin{equation}
 T_a^{\rm sp}=T_a^{\rm rc},\qquad
 \mathfrak A_a^{\rm sp}=\mathfrak A_a^{\rm rc}.             \label{eq:speed-projected-phase-attracting}
\end{equation}
On the repelling branch define the normalized speed-row right inverse
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak v_r^{\rm sp}b
 =\frac{\mathfrak u_r^{\rm ter}b}{\chi_{r,\delta}},
 \qquad
 \mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}\mathfrak v_r^{\rm sp}b=b,            \label{eq:speed-row-right-inverse}
\end{equation}
and put
\begin{align}
 c_r^{\rm sp}[a]
 &=\varepsilon(\mathfrak A_r^{\rm rc}a)(e_{r,1}),\notag\\
 T_r^{\rm sp}a
 &=T_r^{\rm rc}a-\mathfrak v_r^{\rm sp}c_r^{\rm sp}[a],
                                                               \label{eq:speed-projected-phase-column}\\
 \mathfrak A_r^{\rm sp}a
 &=\mathfrak D_rT_r^{\rm sp}a+\mathscr P_{c,r,N}a
  =\mathfrak A_r^{\rm rc}a
       -\mathfrak D_r\mathfrak v_r^{\rm sp}c_r^{\rm sp}[a].
                                                               \label{eq:speed-projected-phase-action}
\end{align}
Thus
\begin{equation}
 (\mathfrak A_r^{\rm sp}a)(e_{r,1})=0.           \label{eq:speed-projected-phase-kernel}
\end{equation}
Moreover, with
\begin{equation}
 \eta_{\mathrm{sp},\delta}
 =\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}
      +\frac{\rho_\delta}{\lambda_\delta},                 \label{eq:speed-projected-phase-small-number}
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{align}
 \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm sp}a\|_
       {\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 &\le C\eta_{\mathrm{sp},\delta}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}},
 \qquad \eta_{\mathrm{sp},\delta}=o(1),\notag\\
 \|(T_\sigma^{\rm sp}-T_\sigma^{\rm rc})a\|_
       {\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 +\|(Z_{\sigma,T},p_{\sigma,T})
       [(T_\sigma^{\rm sp}-T_\sigma^{\rm rc})a]\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 &\le C\frac{\rho_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}.             \label{eq:speed-projected-phase-bounds}
\end{align}

Put
\begin{align}
 \mathscr L_{\sigma,\rm sp}^{\mathrm{split}+T}(y,a)
 =\bigl(&\mathscr N_\sigma^{\rm sp,\infty}y,
 q_0^2a'-\mathfrak D_\sigma y
              -\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm sp}a,
 a(e_{\sigma,0})\bigr),                          \label{eq:speed-bordered-phase-normal-operator}\\
 \mathbb L_{\sigma}^{\rm aff}(y,a,\tau)
 =\bigl(&\mathscr L_{\sigma,\rm sp}^{\mathrm{split}+T}(y,a),
          a(e_{\sigma,1})+\tau\bigr).            \label{eq:affine-complete-bordered-operator}
\end{align}
The triangular event-slack coordinate \(\tau\), reserved for a prospective
duration correction, and the last range row are both
\(\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}\).  Then
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb L_{\sigma}^{\rm aff}:
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}+T}
       (\mathscr N_\sigma^{\rm sp})
 \times\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
 \times\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}
 \longrightarrow
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}+T}
 \times\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
 \times\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}\times\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}
                                                        \label{eq:affine-complete-bordered-isomorphism}
\end{equation}
is a dimension- and diagonal-uniform isomorphism.  If the first three data
slots are denoted by \((f,k,b_0)\) and the terminal event datum by \(b_E\),
its inverse is
\begin{align}
 y&=G_\sigma^{\rm sp,\infty}f,\notag\\
 a&=\left\{\mathscr B_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
       -(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm sp},0)\right\}^{-1}
       (k+\mathfrak D_\sigma y,b_0),\notag\\
 \tau&=b_E-a(e_{\sigma,1}).                     \label{eq:affine-complete-bordered-inverse}
\end{align}

This inverse accepts the exact affine residual of the reduced reference.
Let
\begin{equation}
 \mathbf1_{\rm B}=\mathbf1_{(0,s_{\sigma,T})},
 \qquad
 \mathbf1_T=\mathbf1_{(s_{\sigma,T},S_\sigma)},             \label{eq:affine-hard-source-split}
\end{equation}
where endpoint values are immaterial in the a.e. equations, and define
\begin{align}
 f_{\sigma,\rm bd}^{\rm aff}
 &=\bigl(\Delta\phi_{e_{\sigma,0}},0,0,0\bigr),\notag\\
 f_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\rm aff}
 &=\bigl(0,0,-\mathbf1_{\rm B}R_{z,N}(\bar r_\sigma),
            \mathbf1_{\rm B}\varepsilon R_{q,N}(\bar r_\sigma)\bigr),\notag\\
 f_{\sigma,T}^{\rm aff}
 &=\bigl(0,0,-\mathbf1_TR_{z,N}(\bar r_\sigma),
            \mathbf1_T\varepsilon R_{q,N}(\bar r_\sigma)\bigr),\notag\\
 f_\sigma^{\rm aff}
 &=f_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\rm aff}
      +f_{\sigma,\rm bd}^{\rm aff}
      +f_{\sigma,T}^{\rm aff}.                  \label{eq:affine-normal-source-decomposition}
\end{align}
Thus \(f_\sigma^{\rm aff}\) is the normal source whose negative is the
normal component of the exact nonlinear residual at the reduced reference;
compare \cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-base-residual}.  The hard split is
an identity in the a.e. normal equations, not a cutoff approximation.  One
has
\begin{align}
 \|f_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\rm aff}\|_
  {\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,
      \mathrm{tf},\infty}}
 &\le C,\notag\\
 \|f_{\sigma,\rm bd}^{\rm aff}\|_
  {\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm bd}}
 +\|f_{\sigma,T}^{\rm aff}\|_
  {\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}}
 &\le C R_\delta.                               \label{eq:affine-source-summand-bounds}
\end{align}
In fact the sharper terminal estimate
\begin{equation}
 \|f_{\sigma,T}^{\rm aff}\|_
   {\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}}
 \le C r_{\sigma,T}                             \label{eq:affine-terminal-source-sharp-bound}
\end{equation}
will be used below.

Define the affine predictor by
\begin{equation}
 (y_\sigma^{\rm aff},a_\sigma^{\rm aff},
       \tau_\sigma^{\rm aff})
 =\bigl(\mathbb L_\sigma^{\rm aff}\bigr)^{-1}
       (f_\sigma^{\rm aff},0,0,0).              \label{eq:affine-endpoint-predictor}
\end{equation}
With
\begin{equation}
 \Omega_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm aff}
 =\lambda_\delta
   +\frac{\lambda_\delta M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
   +R_\delta\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}
   +R_\delta m_{\sigma,T},                       \label{eq:affine-predictor-small-number}
\end{equation}
the predictor satisfies
\begin{align}
 \|y_\sigma^{\rm aff}\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 &\le C\lambda_\delta,\notag\\
 \|a_\sigma^{\rm aff}\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 +\frac{|\tau_\sigma^{\rm aff}|}{\rho_\delta}
 &\le C\Omega_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm aff},       \label{eq:affine-predictor-phase-slack-bound}\\
 \|(Z_{x,T}^{\rm aff},p_{x,T}^{\rm aff})\|_
       {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 &\le C\left\{\lambda_\delta
       +(\rho_\delta+M_\delta)
             \Omega_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm aff}\right\},   \label{eq:affine-predictor-terminal-bound}
\end{align}
where \(x^{\rm aff}=T_\sigma^{\rm sp}a_\sigma^{\rm aff}
+y_\sigma^{\rm aff}\).  For fixed \(\varkappa>0\),
\begin{equation}
 \limsup_{\delta\downarrow0}
   \Omega_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm aff}
 \le C\varkappa^2.                              \label{eq:affine-explicit-budget-limit}
\end{equation}
This limit concerns the explicit source budget.  Since the constants in
the present source-split paragraph are allowed to depend on the fixed
\(\varkappa\), it does not by itself prove a
\(\varkappa\)-uniform two-parameter smallness theorem for the predictor.
That uniformity belongs to the nonlinear contraction gate.  On the
repelling branch the predictor nevertheless satisfies the exact linear
endpoint identity
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak D_ry_r^{\rm aff}(e_{r,1})=0,\qquad
 \{\mathfrak D_ry_r^{\rm aff}
       +\mathfrak A_r^{\rm sp}a_r^{\rm aff}\}(e_{r,1})=0.
                                                        \label{eq:affine-predictor-speed-row}
\end{equation}

The theorem closes the full fixed-reference linear border and the affine
source corrector.  The predictor is not a zero of the nonlinear RFDE
residual.  In particular, no nonlinear split-range membership, moving
endpoint, terminal second jet, past orbit, connection, or physical root is
asserted here.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional event-aligned scalar core and history interface]
\label[corollary]{cor:conditional-event-aligned-source-split-inverse}
Retain \cref{lem:endpoint-centered-duration-selector}, including its fixed
envelope and selector length \(L\), and assume
\begin{equation}
 0\le L,\qquad L+\theta_m\le L_b,\qquad
 J_L=[L_b-L-\theta_m,L_b-L]\subset[0,L_b].       \label{eq:terminal-selector-recut-window}
\end{equation}
Let \(L_\sigma=\mathscr L_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}^{\mathrm{split}}\) and
\(\ell_\sigma=\ell_{\sigma,\mathrm{ter}}\).  On the attracting branch put
\(\mathcal K_{p,a}=0\).  On the repelling branch, for a supplied endpoint
reference, define the scalar normal-row column
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal K_{p,r}\beta
 =\bigl((0,K_p\beta,0,0),0,0\bigr),\qquad
 \kappa_{p,\delta}
 =\frac{\rho_\delta|K_p|}{\varepsilon R_\delta}. \label{eq:source-split-event-normal-column}
\end{equation}
If \(\kappa_{p,\delta}\le C_K\), then
\begin{equation}
 \alpha_{p,\sigma}
 =\ell_\sigma L_\sigma^{-1}\mathcal K_{p,\sigma},
 \qquad
 |\alpha_{p,\sigma}|
 \le C C_K\{
   \eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}+\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}\}
 =o(1).                                         \label{eq:source-split-event-alpha}
\end{equation}
Hence the event-aligned scalar core
\begin{equation}
 L_\sigma^{\rm ea}
 =L_\sigma-\mathcal K_{p,\sigma}\ell_\sigma       \label{eq:source-split-event-aligned-core}
\end{equation}
is a uniform isomorphism, with
\begin{equation}
 (L_\sigma^{\rm ea})^{-1}
 =L_\sigma^{-1}
  +\frac{L_\sigma^{-1}\mathcal K_{p,\sigma}
              \ell_\sigma L_\sigma^{-1}}
         {1-\alpha_{p,\sigma}}.                  \label{eq:source-split-Sherman-Morrison}
\end{equation}

Let
\(
 \mathcal H_{Z,T}^1=W^{1,\infty}([-\theta_m,0];E_N)
\), with
\begin{equation}
 \|h\|_{\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1}
 =\varepsilon^{-1}
   \max\{\|h\|_{C^0},\delta\|h'\|_{L^\infty}\}.  \label{eq:terminal-voltage-interface-norm}
\end{equation}
For \(v=(y,a)\), put \(x=T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+y\), and define the full physical
observation on the same selector window by
\begin{equation}
 (\mathcal T_{Z,L}v)(\theta)
 =Z_{x,T}(L_b-L+\theta),\qquad -\theta_m\le\theta\le0.
                                                               \label{eq:terminal-voltage-full-observation}
\end{equation}
The estimates in
\cref{eq:source-split-terminal-observation,eq:source-split-reconstructed-terminal-observation},
and the definition of the terminal \(\mathsf T^1\)-norm, make
\(\mathcal T_{Z,L}\) a uniform map into \(\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1\).  Let
\(K_{Z,L}:\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}\to\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1\) be bounded.  Introduce
a free interface history \(h\in\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1\).  Then
\begin{align*}
 \mathcal X_\sigma^{\rm split}
 &=\mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)
       \times\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}},\\
 \mathcal Y_\sigma^{\rm split}
 &=\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}}
       \times\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
       \times\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}.
\end{align*}
The operator
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde L_\sigma^{\rm ea}(v,h)
 =\left(
   L_\sigma v-\mathcal K_{p,\sigma}\ell_\sigma v,\,
   \mathcal T_{Z,L}v-K_{Z,L}\ell_\sigma v-h\right)          \label{eq:terminal-history-augmented-event-core}
\end{equation}
from
\(
 \mathcal X_\sigma^{\rm split}\times\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1
\)
to
\(
 \mathcal Y_\sigma^{\rm split}\times\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1
\)
is an isomorphism if and only if \(L_\sigma^{\rm ea}\) is, and
\begin{equation}
 v=(L_\sigma^{\rm ea})^{-1}f,\qquad
 h=(\mathcal T_{Z,L}-K_{Z,L}\ell_\sigma)v-g.     \label{eq:terminal-history-augmented-inverse}
\end{equation}
Its inverse is uniform whenever
\(L_\sigma^{\rm ea}\), \(\mathcal T_{Z,L}\), and \(K_{Z,L}\) have uniform norms.
In particular, \(K_{Z,L}\) does not enter the Sherman--Morrison denominator.

For the selector column in
\cref{eq:endpoint-selector-event-shear}, assume
\(
 Z_*\in C^2(I_{*,L};E_N)
\), where
\(
 I_{*,L}=[S_*-L-\theta_m,S_*-L]
\), and take every norm below on this interval.  If
\(
 |q_0(e)/q_{*,e}|\le C
\), then
\begin{equation}
 \|K_{Z,L}\|_{\mathcal L(\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta},
                \mathcal H_{Z,T}^1)}
 \le C\frac{\rho_\delta}{\varepsilon}
   \left\{\frac{\|Z_*'\|_{C^0}}{\delta}
                  +\|Z_*''\|_{C^0}\right\}.      \label{eq:terminal-history-shear-jet-bound}
\end{equation}
Thus the sufficient slow-jet conditions
\begin{equation}
 \|Z_*'\|_{C^0}\le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta},
 \qquad
 \|Z_*''\|_{C^0}\le C\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}         \label{eq:terminal-history-slow-jet-gate}
\end{equation}
make this observation shear uniform.  Neither these two selected-window
derivative bounds nor
the hypothesis \(\kappa_{p,\delta}=O(1)\) follows from an ordinary terminal
\(\mathsf T^1\)-bound.  The corollary therefore gives a conditional linear
event-aligned perturbation of the proved reduced-reference square core.  It
is not the full derivative at a nonzero endpoint reference, an endpoint RFDE
reference itself, or a physical family.  The claim that \(K_{Z,L}\) stays out
of the denominator applies only to this isolated free-interface block.  If
\(h\) is later coupled to another matching row, the history shear may enter
the larger global Schur or Woodbury matrix.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[Unavoidable \(S_\delta^2\) loss for rough normal data]
\label[proposition]{prop:rough-normal-prefix-action-obstruction}
The old rough norm cannot replace the weighted-speed/action graph norm in
\cref{eq:prefix-action-normal-data-norm} while retaining a uniform
\(\rho_\delta\)-normalized causal phase bound.  On the attracting branch
there are data
\(f_\delta\in\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{a,\mathrm{pa}}\)
(and hence in \(\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{a,0}\)) such that
\begin{equation}
 \|f_\delta\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{a,0}}\le C,\qquad
 \|\mathscr Q_af_\delta\|_{\mathsf H_{a,\mathrm{pa}}}
 \ge c\frac{R_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}
 =2\alpha c\varkappa^2S_\delta^2.               \label{eq:rough-normal-prefix-action-loss}
\end{equation}
For each fixed \(\varkappa>0\), the left-hand side in the second inequality
is also at most \(C_\varkappa S_\delta^2\).
For the corresponding solution of
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr L_{a,\mathrm{ent}}(y_\delta,a_\delta)
 =(f_\delta,0,0),                                \label{eq:rough-normal-prefix-action-test}
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}
 c_\varkappa S_\delta^2
 \le\|a_\delta\|_{\mathsf A_{a,\mathrm{pa}}}
 \le C_\varkappa S_\delta^2.                    \label{eq:rough-normal-causal-phase-loss}
\end{equation}
Consequently there is no dimension- and \(\delta\)-uniform inverse from
the old unrestricted rough normal range
\(\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}\) into the causal-entry phase space.
The leading obstruction is already present in the no-delay normal block.
The delayed raw-compatible phase return is an \(o(1)\) correction in the
displayed graph norm and cannot cancel it.  A later terminal theorem must
therefore use \(\lambda_\delta\)-scaled bulk data, separately estimated
localized endpoint columns, and a corresponding hybrid action graph space.
\end{proposition}

For the fold-side anchored formulation, define
\begin{align}
 \mathsf A_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}^1(I_\sigma)
 &=\left\{a\in C^1(I_\sigma):
       \|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}<\infty\right\},\notag\\
 \|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}
 &=\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|a(r)|}{|r|}
       +\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|a'(r)|,\notag\\
 \mathsf H_{\phi,\infty}^0(I_\sigma)
 &=C(I_\sigma),\qquad
 \|h\|_{\phi,\infty}=\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|h(r)|.  \label{eq:relative-phase-spaces}
\end{align}

\begin{lemma}[Inner-anchored nonlinear reduced-flow phase chart]
\label[lemma]{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart}
Let
\[
 I_a=[\rho_\delta,r_{\rm out}],\qquad
 I_r=[-r_{\rm out},-\rho_\delta],\qquad
 \varepsilon=\delta^2,
\]
and put
\[
 e_{a,*}=\rho_\delta,\qquad e_{r,*}=-\rho_\delta,\qquad
 m_\delta(r)=|r|+\frac{\varepsilon}{|r|^2}.
\]
For \(\chi>0\), put
\[
 \mathcal C_{\sigma,\chi}
 :=\left\{u:\ \text{there exists }r\in I_\sigma
       \text{ with }|u-r|<\chi|r|\right\}.
\]
Fix \(0<\chi_0<1/8\), and assume that each \(I_\sigma\) has a
same-sign open enlargement \(J_\sigma\) satisfying
\[
 \mathcal C_{\sigma,4\chi_0}
 \subset J_\sigma .
\]
Assume on \(J_\sigma\), uniformly in all retained indices, that
\begin{equation}
 0<q_-\le-q_0(r)\le q_+,\qquad
 |r|\,|q_0'(r)|\le C_q,                            \label{eq:anchored-phase-q-class}
\end{equation}
There is \(c_\phi>0\), independent of \(N,\delta\) and the branch, such
that the open set
\[
 \mathcal U_{\sigma,\phi}
 =\{a\in\mathsf A_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}^1(I_\sigma):
          \|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}<c_\phi\}
\]
has the following property.  For \(a\in\mathcal U_{\sigma,\phi}\), set
\begin{equation}
 \Theta_a(r)=\Phi_{q_0}^{a(r)}(r).                 \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-chart}
\end{equation}
Then \(\Theta_a(I_\sigma)\subset J_\sigma\), and the map
\[
 a\longmapsto\Theta_a:
   \mathcal U_{\sigma,\phi}\to C^1(I_\sigma)
\]
is \(C^1\).  Its differential and the exact phase identities are
\begin{align}
 D_a\Theta_a[h](r)
 &=q_0(\Theta_a(r))h(r),                           \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-chart-a-derivative}\\
 \Theta_a'(r)
 &=\frac{q_0(\Theta_a(r))}{q_0(r)}
       \{1+q_0(r)a'(r)\},                          \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-chart-derivative}\\
 q_0(r)\Theta_a'(r)-q_0(\Theta_a(r))
 &=q_0(r)q_0(\Theta_a(r))a'(r).                    \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-row-identity}
\end{align}
Define the nonlinear phase action by
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a)(r)
 =q_0(r)q_0(\Theta_a(r))a'(r).                     \label{eq:anchored-nonlinear-phase-action}
\end{equation}
The map
\[
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}:
 \mathcal U_{\sigma,\phi}\longrightarrow
             \mathsf H_{\phi,\infty}^0(I_\sigma)
\]
is \(C^1\), and its differential is
\begin{align}
 D\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a)h
 ={}&q_0(r)q_0(\Theta_a(r))h'(r)\notag\\
 &+q_0(r)q_0'(\Theta_a(r))q_0(\Theta_a(r))
             a'(r)h(r),                            \label{eq:anchored-nonlinear-phase-action-derivative}\\
 D\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(0)h
 ={}&q_0(r)^2h'(r).                                \label{eq:anchored-nonlinear-phase-linearization}
\end{align}
Consequently the full anchored scalar map
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^{\rm nl}(a)
 =\bigl(\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a),
             a(e_{\sigma,*})\bigr)                 \label{eq:anchored-nonlinear-phase-full-map}
\end{equation}
is \(C^1\) from \(\mathcal U_{\sigma,\phi}\) to
\(\mathsf H_{\phi,\infty}^0(I_\sigma)\times\R\), and
\begin{equation}
 D\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^{\rm nl}(0)h
 =\bigl(q_0^2h',h(e_{\sigma,*})\bigr).             \label{eq:anchored-nonlinear-full-linearization}
\end{equation}
In particular, let \(\widehat Q:I_\sigma\to\R\) denote a speed
residual already pulled back to the fixed reduced coordinate.  If
\[
 \partial_s\bar r(s)=\delta q_0(\bar r(s)),
 \qquad
 r_a(s)=\Theta_a(\bar r(s)),
\]
then
\[
 \partial_sr_a(s)
 =\delta\left\{q_0(r_a(s))
          +\widehat Q(\bar r(s))\right\}
\]
is equivalent, since \(\bar r\) covers \(I_\sigma\), to
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a)(r)
 =\widehat Q(r).                                   \label{eq:anchored-nonlinear-phase-equation}
\end{equation}

There are constants \(c_*,C_*,D_*>0\), depending only on
\(q_\pm,C_q,\chi_0\) and the common collar geometry, with the following
property.  In particular they are independent of
\(N,\delta,\nu,\eta\) and of the branch.  If
\(\widehat Q\in C(I_\sigma)\) and
\begin{equation}
 |\widehat Q(r)|\le Dm_\delta(r),\qquad
 D\Lambda_\delta\le D_*,\qquad
 \Lambda_\delta=r_{\rm out}
       +\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2},          \label{eq:anchored-phase-speed-class}
\end{equation}
then the anchored problem
\begin{equation}
 q_0(r)q_0(\Theta_a(r))a'(r)=\widehat Q(r),\qquad
 a(e_{\sigma,*})=0,                               \label{eq:anchored-phase-IVP}
\end{equation}
has a unique solution \(a\in\mathcal U_{\sigma,\phi}\).  It satisfies
\begin{align}
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}
 &\le C_*DM_\delta,
 &M_\delta&=r_{\rm out}^2
                  +\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta},
                                                               \label{eq:anchored-phase-A1-bound}\\
 \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|a(r)|}{|r|}
 +\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|q_0(r)a'(r)|
 &\le C_*D\Lambda_\delta,                          \label{eq:anchored-phase-relative-bound}\\
 \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|\Theta_a(r)-r|}{|r|}
 +\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}|\Theta_a'(r)-1|
 &\le C_*D\Lambda_\delta.                          \label{eq:anchored-phase-chart-smallness}
\end{align}
After decreasing \(D_*\) if necessary,
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{sgn}\Theta_a(r)=\operatorname{sgn}r,\qquad
 c_*\le\Theta_a'(r)\le C_*,
 \qquad r\in I_\sigma,                             \label{eq:anchored-phase-orientation}
\end{equation}
so \(\Theta_a\) is an orientation-preserving \(C^1\) diffeomorphism onto
its same-sign image.  Moreover,
\begin{equation}
 q_0(\Theta_a(r))+\widehat Q(r)
 =q_0(r)\Theta_a'(r)<0.                            \label{eq:anchored-phase-speed-sign}
\end{equation}
Thus the reconstructed scalar reduced speed, not yet a speed along a
constructed RFDE tracker, retains its sign.
Since \(a(e_{\sigma,*})=0\),
\[
 \Theta_a(e_{\sigma,*})=e_{\sigma,*}.
\]
Thus the attracting chart is fixed at its inner terminal section
\(e_{a,*}=\rho_\delta\), whereas the repelling chart is fixed at its
inner entry section \(e_{r,*}=-\rho_\delta\).

Since \(\rho_\delta=\delta S_\delta/(2\alpha)\),
\begin{equation}
 \Lambda_\delta=r_{\rm out}+\frac{4\alpha^2}{S_\delta^2},
 \qquad
 M_\delta=r_{\rm out}^2+\frac{2\alpha\delta}{S_\delta}.       \label{eq:anchored-phase-small-parameters}
\end{equation}
Consequently, for every fixed dimension-uniform upper bound
\(D\le D_0\), condition
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-speed-class} follows by first choosing
\(r_{\rm out}\) so that \(D_0r_{\rm out}\) is sufficiently small and
then decreasing \(\delta\).
\end{lemma}

The exponent in \(\Theta_a=\Phi_{q_0}^{a}\) is \(a\), not
\(\delta a\).  Indeed, a fold-time displacement \(\Delta S\) produces the
\(q_0\)-flow time \(\delta\Delta S=\widehat\tau\), while the phase
coordinate satisfies \(a=\xi/q_0=\widehat\tau\).  Replacing \(a\) by
\(\delta a\) would incorrectly linearize the action as
\(\delta q_0^2a'\).  The two inner anchors are a nonlinear gauge choice;
they are not silently identified with the branch-entry row of
\cref{prop:scaled-phase-event-core}.  The next proposition instead
constructs the corresponding one-row linear inverse directly.

\begin{proposition}[Endpoint-recut obstruction for the inner-anchored phase core]
\label[proposition]{prop:inner-anchor-endpoint-recut-obstruction}
Retain \cref{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart}, fix a sufficiently
small \(r_{\rm out}>0\), and put
\begin{equation}
 \lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}=r_{\rm out}+S_\delta^{-2}.
                                                        \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-lambda}
\end{equation}
There is a dimension-uniform \(d_0>0\) such that, for all sufficiently
small \(\delta\), the admissible pulled-back scalar source
\begin{equation}
 \widehat Q_{\sigma,\delta}(r)
 =D_\delta|r|,\qquad
 D_\delta=d_0\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep},             \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-source}
\end{equation}
belongs to the speed class in
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-speed-class}.  Let \(a_{\sigma,\delta}\) be the
corresponding inner-anchored solution.  With
\begin{equation*}
 e_{a,o}=r_{\rm out},\qquad e_{r,o}=-r_{\rm out},
\end{equation*}
one has
\begin{align}
 a_{a,\delta}(e_{a,o})&>0,
 &a_{r,\delta}(e_{r,o})&<0,                       \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-phase-signs}\\
 cD_\delta(r_{\rm out}^2-\rho_\delta^2)
 &\le |a_{\sigma,\delta}(e_{\sigma,o})|
 \le CD_\delta(r_{\rm out}^2-\rho_\delta^2),      \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-phase-size}\\
 \Theta_{a_{a,\delta}}(r_{\rm out})&<r_{\rm out},
 &\Theta_{a_{r,\delta}}(-r_{\rm out})&>-r_{\rm out}.
                                                        \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-sidedness}
\end{align}
For these two admissible inner-anchored solutions, neither image
\(\Theta_{a_{\sigma,\delta}}(I_\sigma)\) contains its original outer
endpoint.  The attracting forward scalar path starts at
\(\Theta_{a_{a,\delta}}(r_{\rm out})<r_{\rm out}\) and ends at
\(\rho_\delta\), whereas the repelling path starts at \(-\rho_\delta\) and
ends at \(\Theta_{a_{r,\delta}}(-r_{\rm out})>-r_{\rm out}\).  The strict
scalar speed sign in \cref{eq:anchored-phase-speed-sign} does not repair this
failure.  On the attracting side the missing endpoint lies behind the
available forward segment; on the repelling side it requires an as yet
unconstructed forward continuation.

The same obstruction is visible in the bordered rows.  The unique constant
shift preserving \(a'\) and imposing the attracting causal-entry row, and
the associated algebraic scalar event-row corrections, are
\begin{align}
 \widetilde a_{a,\delta}
 &=a_{a,\delta}-a_{a,\delta}(r_{\rm out}),
 &\widehat\tau_{a,\delta}
 &=a_{a,\delta}(r_{\rm out}),\notag\\
 \widetilde a_{r,\delta}
 &=a_{r,\delta},
 &\widehat\tau_{r,\delta}
 &=-a_{r,\delta}(-r_{\rm out}).                  \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-row-reanchoring}
\end{align}
They satisfy
\begin{align}
 \widetilde a_{a,\delta}(r_{\rm out})&=0,
 &\widetilde a_{a,\delta}(\rho_\delta)
       +\widehat\tau_{a,\delta}&=0,\notag\\
 \widetilde a_{r,\delta}(-\rho_\delta)&=0,
 &\widetilde a_{r,\delta}(-r_{\rm out})
       +\widehat\tau_{r,\delta}&=0.              \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-rows}
\end{align}
For fixed \(r_{\rm out}\), as \(\delta\downarrow0\),
\begin{equation}
 \min_{\sigma\in\{a,r\}}
 \frac{|\widehat\tau_{\sigma,\delta}|}{\rho_\delta}
 \longrightarrow\infty,
 \qquad
 \min_{\sigma\in\{a,r\}}
 \frac{|\widehat\tau_{\sigma,\delta}|/\delta}{S_\delta}
 \longrightarrow\infty.                         \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-scale-divergence}
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \|\widetilde a_{a,\delta}\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}
 \ge
 \frac{|\widehat\tau_{a,\delta}|}{\rho_\delta}
 \longrightarrow\infty.                         \label{eq:endpoint-obstruction-relative-norm}
\end{equation}
Here \(\widehat\tau_{\sigma,\delta}\) is scalar bordered-row bookkeeping, and
\(\widehat\tau_{\sigma,\delta}/\delta\) is only its represented fold-time
scale; no nonlinear physical moving-event time has been constructed.
Indeed, \(\widetilde a_{a,\delta}\) need not solve the same nonlinear phase
action equation.  Hence the present weighted speed class, inner-anchored
relative inverse, strict speed sign, and an \(O(S_\delta)\) terminal window
do not imply a literal original-endpoint recut.  Such a theorem must instead
solve a new causal-entry plus terminal-event moving-duration boundary
problem, or impose an additional endpoint cancellation
\(|\widehat\tau|=O(\rho_\delta)\) together with the missing one-sided
attachment.

This is a scalar phase-core nonimplication result.  The source in
\cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-source} is not asserted to be the residual of
a full RFDE tracker, and the proposition does not exclude additional
model-specific cancellation.  It excludes only the attempted deduction of
original-endpoint recutting from the scalar phase-core hypotheses and
estimates of
\cref{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart} alone.
\end{proposition}

The obstruction is a fixed-outer-radius statement about an inner-anchored
reference chart.  It does not say that a scalar causal endpoint problem has
no solution.  For a given Eulerian speed, the correct phase is the
difference of two flight-time coordinates.  This gives an exact nonlinear
terminal row and also identifies a receding outer scale on which its
duration fits the \(O(S_\delta)\) endpoint window.

\begin{theorem}[Receding-collar causal flight time and terminal Schur row]
\label[theorem]{thm:receding-collar-causal-flight-time}
Retain the common same-sign scalar-speed bounds in
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-q-class}.  Fix \(C_Q>0\).  There is
\(0<\varkappa_{\rm ep}^*<1\), depending only on \(C_Q\) and the common
scalar collar bounds, with the following property.  For every fixed
\(0<\varkappa_{\rm ep}\le\varkappa_{\rm ep}^*\), there is
\(\delta_{\rm ep}(\varkappa_{\rm ep})>0\).  Whenever
\(0<\delta<\delta_{\rm ep}(\varkappa_{\rm ep})\), set
\begin{equation}
 r_{{\rm out},\delta}
 =\varkappa_{\rm ep}(\delta S_\delta^3)^{1/2},
 \qquad
 \lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}
 =r_{{\rm out},\delta}+S_\delta^{-2},
 \qquad
 M_\delta^{\rm ep}
 =r_{{\rm out},\delta}^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}.
                                                        \label{eq:receding-flight-scales}
\end{equation}
In this theorem \(r_{\rm out}=r_{{\rm out},\delta}\), and
\[
 (e_{a,0},e_{a,1})=(r_{{\rm out},\delta},\rho_\delta),
 \qquad
 (e_{r,0},e_{r,1})=(-\rho_\delta,-r_{{\rm out},\delta}).
\]
Write \(I_\sigma\) for the closed interval between these endpoints and put
\[
 m_\delta(r)=|r|+\frac{\varepsilon}{|r|^2}.
\]
Let \(\widehat Q_\sigma\in C(I_\sigma)\) be an
\emph{Eulerian} speed correction: its argument is the current physical
scalar coordinate, not a fixed-reference pullback.  Assume
\begin{equation}
 |\widehat Q_\sigma(r)|\le D_\delta m_\delta(r),
 \qquad
 0\le D_\delta\le C_Q\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}.     \label{eq:receding-flight-source-class}
\end{equation}
Then
\[
 q_\sigma(r)=q_0(r)+\widehat Q_\sigma(r)
 \le-\frac{q_-}{2}<0
\]
on \(I_\sigma\).  Define
\begin{align}
 \theta_\sigma(r)
 &=\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r\frac{\dd u}{q_\sigma(u)},
 &
 \theta_\sigma^0(r)
 &=\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r\frac{\dd u}{q_0(u)},\notag\\
 \beta_\sigma(r)
 &=\theta_\sigma^0(r)-\theta_\sigma(r),
 &
 \widehat\tau_\sigma
 &=\theta_\sigma(e_{\sigma,1})
       -\theta_\sigma^0(e_{\sigma,1}).            \label{eq:receding-flight-coordinates}
\end{align}
The pair \((\beta_\sigma,\widehat\tau_\sigma)\) is the unique solution of
the exact nonlinear bordered problem
\begin{align}
 \mathscr B_{\sigma}^{\rm tof}(\beta_\sigma)(r)
 &:=\frac{q_0(r)^2\beta_\sigma'(r)}
          {1-q_0(r)\beta_\sigma'(r)}
   =\widehat Q_\sigma(r),\notag\\
 \beta_\sigma(e_{\sigma,0})&=0,\qquad
 \beta_\sigma(e_{\sigma,1})
       +\widehat\tau_\sigma=0.                   \label{eq:receding-flight-bordered-problem}
\end{align}
In particular,
\begin{align}
 \beta_\sigma'(r)
 &=\frac{\widehat Q_\sigma(r)}
       {q_0(r)\{q_0(r)+\widehat Q_\sigma(r)\}},
                                                        \label{eq:receding-flight-beta-prime}\\
 \widehat\tau_\sigma
 &=\int_{e_{\sigma,1}}^{e_{\sigma,0}}
    \frac{\widehat Q_\sigma(r)}
       {q_0(r)\{q_0(r)+\widehat Q_\sigma(r)\}}\,\dd r.
                                                        \label{eq:receding-flight-duration}
\end{align}
Its exact source differential is
\begin{equation}
 D_{\widehat Q}\widehat\tau_\sigma[H]
 =\int_{e_{\sigma,1}}^{e_{\sigma,0}}
       \frac{H(r)}
        {\{q_0(r)+\widehat Q_\sigma(r)\}^2}\,\dd r.
                                                        \label{eq:receding-flight-duration-differential}
\end{equation}
The following estimates are uniform in \(N,\delta\), the branch, and
\(0<\varkappa_{\rm ep}\le\varkappa_{\rm ep}^*\):
\begin{align}
 \sup_{I_\sigma}
   \frac{|\beta_\sigma'(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}
 &\le CD_\delta,\notag\\
 \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|\beta_\sigma(r)|}{|r|}
   +\frac{|\widehat\tau_\sigma|}{\rho_\delta}
 &\le C C_Q\{\varkappa_{\rm ep}^2+S_\delta^{-4}\},
                                                        \label{eq:receding-flight-relative-bounds}\\
 \frac{|\widehat\tau_\sigma|}{\delta}
 &\le C C_Q\{\varkappa_{\rm ep}^2S_\delta
                         +S_\delta^{-3}\}.        \label{eq:receding-flight-fold-time-bound}
\end{align}

The nonlinear terminal row has a uniform action-weighted Schur inverse.
Put
\begin{align}
 \|h\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,{\rm ep}}}
 &=\frac1{\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}}
   \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|h(r)|}{m_\delta(r)},\notag\\
\|b\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,{\rm ep}}}
 &=\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|b(r)|}{|r|}
   +\frac1{\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}}
      \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|b'(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}.
                                                        \label{eq:receding-flight-linear-norms}
\end{align}
The rational bulk map \(\mathscr B_\sigma^{\rm tof}\) is understood on the
connected component of zero in the open set
\begin{equation}
 \left\{\beta\in C^1(I_\sigma):
  \inf_{r\in I_\sigma}|1-q_0(r)\beta'(r)|>0\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:receding-flight-rational-domain}
\end{equation}
On the source ball defined by the strict form of
\cref{eq:receding-flight-source-class}, the map
\[
 \widehat Q_\sigma\longmapsto
       (\beta_\sigma,\widehat\tau_\sigma)
\]
is real analytic from \(C(I_\sigma)\), with its \(C^0\) topology, into
\(\mathsf A_{\sigma,{\rm ep}}\times\mathbb R\).  At every such solution, the
differential of
\begin{equation}
 (\beta,\widehat\tau)\longmapsto
 \left(\mathscr B_\sigma^{\rm tof}(\beta),
       \beta(e_{\sigma,0}),
       \beta(e_{\sigma,1})+\widehat\tau\right)    \label{eq:receding-flight-bordered-map}
\end{equation}
is an isomorphism.  If its data are \((h,b_0,b_E)\) and its unknown
variation is \((b,\zeta)\), then
\begin{equation}
 \|b\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,{\rm ep}}}
       +\frac{|\zeta|}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\left\{
   \|h\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,{\rm ep}}}
       +\frac{|b_0|+|b_E|}{\rho_\delta}\right\}.  \label{eq:receding-flight-bordered-inverse}
\end{equation}
The uniform assertion in
\cref{eq:receding-flight-bordered-inverse} concerns the inverse; no uniform
forward trace bound in these norms is claimed.

Finally, let
\[
 S_\sigma
 =\delta^{-1}\theta_\sigma(e_{\sigma,1})
 =S_\sigma^0+\frac{\widehat\tau_\sigma}{\delta},
 \qquad
 S_\sigma^0
 =\delta^{-1}\theta_\sigma^0(e_{\sigma,1}).
\]
The scalar initial-value problem
\begin{equation}
 r_\sigma'(s)=\delta q_\sigma(r_\sigma(s)),
 \qquad r_\sigma(0)=e_{\sigma,0},                \label{eq:receding-flight-physical-ivp}
\end{equation}
has a unique strictly decreasing solution on \([0,S_\sigma]\), and
\[
 r_\sigma(S_\sigma)=e_{\sigma,1}.
\]
Thus the prescribed scalar causal entry, the hit at the original
scalar-coordinate terminal endpoint, its exact duration, and the normalized
scalar terminal Schur row are closed on the receding collar.  This theorem
does not put the existing fixed-section
RFDE zeros on the diagonal family
\(r_{\rm out}=r_{{\rm out},\delta}\).  It also does not construct an
arbitrary-source normal inverse, a raw-compatible endpoint history, or a
past physical orbit.  Those are separate requirements for an
original-endpoint outer tracker and a complete-history connection.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[The exact endpoint moment is nonlinear]
\label[remark]{rem:exact-endpoint-flight-moment}
For a fixed \(r_{\rm out}\), strict negativity of
\(q_0+\widehat Q_\sigma\) already gives a unique scalar causal hit.  What
can fail is the normalized terminal window.  By
\cref{eq:receding-flight-duration}, the condition
\[
 |\widehat\tau_\sigma|\le C\rho_\delta
\]
is equivalent to the exact nonlinear flight-time moment bound
\begin{equation}
 \left|
 \int_{e_{\sigma,1}}^{e_{\sigma,0}}
 \frac{\widehat Q_\sigma(r)}
      {q_0(r)\{q_0(r)+\widehat Q_\sigma(r)\}}\,\dd r
 \right|
 \le C\rho_\delta.                               \label{eq:exact-endpoint-flight-moment}
\end{equation}
Cancelling only its first variation is insufficient, because the exact
identity
\begin{equation}
 \widehat\tau_\sigma
 =\int_{e_{\sigma,1}}^{e_{\sigma,0}}
       \frac{\widehat Q_\sigma}{q_0^2}\,\dd r
 -\int_{e_{\sigma,1}}^{e_{\sigma,0}}
       \frac{\widehat Q_\sigma^2}
        {q_0^2(q_0+\widehat Q_\sigma)}\,\dd r    \label{eq:exact-endpoint-flight-quadratic-remainder}
\end{equation}
has a sign-definite second term whenever the speed remains negative.  Thus
a sign-changing nonzero source can have zero linear moment while retaining
a nonzero quadratic duration.  The receding scale in
\cref{thm:receding-collar-causal-flight-time} controls the full nonlinear
moment, not merely its linearization.
\end{remark}

The RFDE phase row does not initially produce an Eulerian source.  It
produces a source pulled back to the fixed reduced coordinate.  The next
result closes this coordinate seam.  A terminal flow tube is indispensable:
the reference endpoint that maps to the fixed physical endpoint need not
belong to the original closed interval.

\begin{theorem}[Reference-pulled causal phase and moving endpoint]
\label[theorem]{thm:reference-pulled-causal-endpoint}
Retain \cref{thm:receding-collar-causal-flight-time} and write
\[
 R_\delta=r_{{\rm out},\delta},\qquad
 \lambda_\delta=\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep},\qquad
 M_\delta=M_\delta^{\rm ep}.
\]
Choose a fixed \(c_T>0\) so small that \(q_+c_T<1/4\), and put
\begin{equation}
 I_\sigma^\sharp
 =\operatorname{hull}\left(
 I_\sigma\cup
 \{\Phi_{q_0}^{t}(e_{\sigma,1}):|t|\le c_T\rho_\delta\}
 \right).                                      \label{eq:reference-pulled-terminal-tube}
\end{equation}
After decreasing the common same-sign collar, and then taking
\(\delta\) sufficiently small for each fixed
\(\varkappa_{\rm ep}>0\), one has
\begin{equation}
 I_\sigma^\sharp\Subset J_\sigma,\qquad
 |r|\ge\tfrac12\rho_\delta\quad(r\in I_\sigma^\sharp),
 \qquad
 \int_{I_\sigma^\sharp}m_\delta(r)\,\dd r\le CM_\delta.
                                                        \label{eq:reference-pulled-tube-geometry}
\end{equation}

Fix \(C_Q>0\).  There is
\(0<\varkappa_{\rm rp}^*\le\varkappa_{\rm ep}^*\) such that, whenever
\(0<\varkappa_{\rm ep}\le\varkappa_{\rm rp}^*\), then for sufficiently
small \(\delta\), every
\(Q_{\sigma}^{\rm ref}\in C(I_\sigma^\sharp)\) satisfying
\begin{equation}
 |Q_{\sigma}^{\rm ref}(r)|\le D_\delta m_\delta(r),
 \qquad 0\le D_\delta\le C_Q\lambda_\delta             \label{eq:reference-pulled-source-class}
\end{equation}
determines a unique pair
\begin{equation*}
 (a_\sigma,\tau_\sigma)\in
 C^1(I_\sigma^\sharp)\times
 (-c_T\rho_\delta,c_T\rho_\delta)
\end{equation*}
in the small component of zero that solves
\begin{align}
 q_0(r)q_0(\Theta_{a_\sigma}(r))a_\sigma'(r)
 &=Q_{\sigma}^{\rm ref}(r),
 &a_\sigma(e_{\sigma,0})&=0,                   \label{eq:reference-pulled-causal-phase}\\
 a_\sigma(r_{\sigma,\tau})+\tau_\sigma&=0,
 &r_{\sigma,\tau}
 &=\Phi_{q_0}^{\tau_\sigma}(e_{\sigma,1}),     \label{eq:reference-pulled-terminal-row}
\end{align}
where
\(\Theta_a(r)=\Phi_{q_0}^{a(r)}(r)\).  It satisfies
\begin{align}
 \sup_{I_\sigma^\sharp}
       \frac{|a_\sigma'(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}
 &\le CD_\delta,\notag\\
 \frac{\|a_\sigma\|_\infty+|\tau_\sigma|}{\rho_\delta}
 &\le C D_\delta\frac{M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 \le CC_Q\{\varkappa_{\rm ep}^2+S_\delta^{-4}\},
                                                        \label{eq:reference-pulled-relative-bound}\\
 \frac{|\tau_\sigma|}{\delta}
 &\le CC_Q\{\varkappa_{\rm ep}^2S_\delta+S_\delta^{-3}\}.
                                                        \label{eq:reference-pulled-fold-time-bound}
\end{align}

This reference-pulled problem is exactly conjugate to the Eulerian
flight-time problem.  On the image of \(\Theta_{a_\sigma}\), define
\begin{equation}
 Q_\sigma^E(R)
 =Q_\sigma^{\rm ref}(\Theta_{a_\sigma}^{-1}(R)),
 \qquad q_\sigma^E(R)=q_0(R)+Q_\sigma^E(R).       \label{eq:reference-pulled-Eulerian-source}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 q_\sigma^E(\Theta_{a_\sigma}(r))
 =q_0(r)\Theta_{a_\sigma}'(r).                  \label{eq:reference-pulled-speed-conjugacy}
\end{equation}
If
\begin{align*}
 \theta_0(r)&=\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r\frac{\dd u}{q_0(u)},&
 \theta_E(R)&=\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^R\frac{\dd U}{q_\sigma^E(U)},
\end{align*}
then
\begin{align}
 \theta_E(\Theta_{a_\sigma}(r))&=\theta_0(r),&
 \theta_0(\Theta_{a_\sigma}(r))-\theta_0(r)&=a_\sigma(r).
                                                        \label{eq:reference-pulled-flight-conjugacy}
\end{align}
Consequently the Eulerian flight defect
\(\beta_\sigma=\theta_0-\theta_E\) obeys
\begin{equation}
 \beta_\sigma(\Theta_{a_\sigma}(r))=a_\sigma(r),
 \qquad
 \beta_\sigma(e_{\sigma,1})+\tau_\sigma=0.      \label{eq:reference-pulled-beta-conjugacy}
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 R_\sigma(s)=\Theta_{a_\sigma}
   (\Phi_{q_0}^{\delta s}(e_{\sigma,0}))         \label{eq:reference-pulled-physical-path}
\end{equation}
solves
\(R_\sigma'=\delta q_\sigma^E(R_\sigma)\), starts at
\(e_{\sigma,0}\), and reaches \(e_{\sigma,1}\) at
\begin{equation}
 S_\sigma=S_\sigma^0+\frac{\tau_\sigma}{\delta}.          \label{eq:reference-pulled-duration}
\end{equation}

The nonlinear bordered differential has a uniform inverse in normalized
causal--endpoint norms.  Define
\begin{align}
 \|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,{\rm ce}}}
 &=\frac1{\lambda_\delta}
      \sup_{I_\sigma^\sharp}\frac{|k(r)|}{m_\delta(r)},\notag\\
 \|h\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,{\rm ce}}}
 &=\frac{\|h\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta}
   +\frac1{\lambda_\delta}
      \sup_{I_\sigma^\sharp}\frac{|h'(r)|}{m_\delta(r)}.
                                                        \label{eq:reference-pulled-linear-norms}
\end{align}
At a solution, put
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak F_\sigma(a,\tau)
 =\left(q_0q_0(\Theta_a)a',\ a(e_{\sigma,0}),
    a(\Phi_{q_0}^{\tau}(e_{\sigma,1}))+\tau\right).       \label{eq:reference-pulled-bordered-map}
\end{equation}
Then \(D\mathfrak F_\sigma(a_\sigma,\tau_\sigma)\) is an isomorphism.
For data \((k,b_0,b_E)\) and inverse image \((h,\zeta)\),
\begin{equation}
 \|h\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,{\rm ce}}}
      +\frac{|\zeta|}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\left\{\|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,{\rm ce}}}
      +\frac{|b_0|+|b_E|}{\rho_\delta}\right\}.           \label{eq:reference-pulled-bordered-inverse}
\end{equation}
Its exact terminal coefficient is
\begin{equation}
 c_{\sigma,{\rm ter}}
 =1+q_0(r_{\sigma,\tau})a_\sigma'(r_{\sigma,\tau})
 =\frac{q_\sigma^E(e_{\sigma,1})}{q_0(e_{\sigma,1})},
 \qquad \frac12\le c_{\sigma,{\rm ter}}\le\frac32.       \label{eq:reference-pulled-terminal-coefficient}
\end{equation}

Thus a fixed-reference source, a causal entry, the shifted reference
endpoint, and the original scalar-coordinate endpoint are now one exact
scalar object.  The theorem does not construct the RFDE moving-duration column.
In the full phase--normal border that column changes
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-terminal-coefficient} by an off-diagonal Schur
term; the scalar coefficient above cannot be substituted for that still
unproved RFDE estimate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Inner-anchored phase Schur inverse]
\label[proposition]{prop:inner-anchored-relative-phase-schur}
Retain
\cref{thm:raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse,%
lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart} and the spaces in
\cref{eq:relative-phase-spaces}.  On
\(\mathsf H_{\phi,\infty}^0(I_\sigma)\times\R\), use the
inner-row data norm
\begin{equation}
 \|(h,b)\|_{\phi,*}
 =\|h\|_{\phi,\infty}+\frac{|b|}{\rho_\delta}.     \label{eq:relative-phase-data-norm}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^0a
 =\bigl(q_0^2a',a(e_{\sigma,*})\bigr)              \label{eq:inner-anchored-phase-linear-operator}
\end{equation}
is an isomorphism
\[
 \mathsf A_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}^1(I_\sigma)
 \longrightarrow
 \mathsf H_{\phi,\infty}^0(I_\sigma)\times\R
\]
with a dimension-uniform inverse.
The scalar \(b\) is included to make the linear map onto; the nonlinear
anchored chart in \cref{eq:anchored-phase-IVP} uses the slice \(b=0\).

The fixed-base linear raw-compatible true-speed action from
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-true-action}, restricted to the relative space,
satisfies the stronger pointwise estimate
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\|_{\phi,\infty}
 \le C r_{\rm out}\left\{
        r_{\rm out}+\frac1{S_\delta^2}
                    +\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\right\}
       \|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}.                  \label{eq:relative-raw-action-smallness}
\end{equation}
Consequently, after first decreasing \(r_{\rm out}\) and then
\(\delta_0\),
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr S_{\sigma,*}^{\rm rc}
 =\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^0
       -(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc},0)            \label{eq:inner-anchored-relative-schur}
\end{equation}
is an isomorphism between the same spaces, with dimension-uniform inverse.

Put
\[
 \mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty y=Q_{\rm n}[y]
       \in\mathsf H_{\phi,\infty}^0(I_\sigma).
\]
For
\(f\in\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}\) and
\(g\in\mathsf H_{\phi,\infty}^0(I_\sigma)\times\R\), the coupled problem
\begin{align}
 \mathscr N_\sigma\{x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\}&=f,\notag\\
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^0a
  -\bigl(\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty x
       +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,0\bigr)&=g       \label{eq:inner-anchored-coupled-block}
\end{align}
has the unique solution
\begin{align}
 y&=G_\sigma f,\notag\\
 a&=(\mathscr S_{\sigma,*}^{\rm rc})^{-1}
       \{(\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty y,0)+g\},\notag\\
 x&=T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+y.                         \label{eq:inner-anchored-schur-inverse}
\end{align}
If
\begin{align}
 \mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{*,\rm rc}
 &=\{(x,a):a\in\mathsf A_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}^1,\
       x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\in\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\},\notag\\
 \|(x,a)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{*,\rm rc}}
 &=\|x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)}
       +\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}},                 \label{eq:inner-anchored-graph-norm}
\end{align}
then this is a Banach graph space and
\begin{equation}
 \|(x,a)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{*,\rm rc}}
 \le C\left\{\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}
                    +\|g\|_{\phi,*}\right\}.       \label{eq:inner-anchored-schur-bound}
\end{equation}

On the attracting branch the inner anchor does not set the outer entry
value \(a(r_{\rm out})\) to zero; rather
\(|a(r_{\rm out})|\le r_{\rm out}\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}\), which is
the extra small factor in the raw collar term of
\cref{eq:relative-raw-action-smallness}.  This proposition fixes the
fold-side phase and removes the constant-mode obstruction at the scalar
phase/fixed-base linear level.  It introduces no nonlinear normal residual
or moving terminal event and proves no nonlinear tracker or physical
complete-history connection.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Relative structured phase response and graph--action norm]
\label[proposition]{prop:relative-structured-phase-response}
Retain
\cref{lem:reduced-flow-phase-defect,%
prop:phase-delay-action-shift,%
prop:normal-to-phase-action-coupling,%
lem:raw-phase-boundary-column-assembly,%
prop:inner-anchored-relative-phase-schur}.
Put
\[
 A=\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}},\qquad
 \kappa_\delta=r_{\rm out}+S_\delta^{-1},
\]
and let
\(T_\sigma^{\rm form}a\) and \(T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\) be the formal and
raw-compatible returned normal columns already defined.  After possibly
decreasing \(\delta_0\),
\begin{align}
 \|\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a\|_{C^0}
 +\|\partial_s\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a\|_{L^\infty_{\rm pw}}
 &\le C\delta A,                                  \label{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile}\\
 \|T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 +\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}
       \frac{|p_{T a}(r)+\varepsilon
                    \mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a(r)|}
            {\varepsilon|r|}
 &\le C\kappa_\delta A,                          \label{eq:relative-phase-state-bound}\\
 \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}
       \frac{|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a(r)|}{|r|}
 &\le C\kappa_\delta A.                          \label{eq:relative-phase-action-pointwise}
\end{align}
Here \(p_{Ta}\) is the \(p\)-component of
\(T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\).  The state estimate also holds with
\(T_\sigma^{\rm form}\) in place of \(T_\sigma^{\rm rc}\), with its
corresponding \(p\)-component.  Both norms in
\cref{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile} use the dimension-uniform node norm
\(\|\cdot\|_N\) in their range.  The derivative in
\cref{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile} is the classical, equivalently weak,
derivative on the fixed finite seam partition; no global \(C^1\) claim is
made.

For the formal column the shifted boundary rows are
\[
 p^\sharp(0)=0\quad(\sigma=a),\qquad
 p^\sharp(S_r)=\varepsilon
       \mathscr P_{c,r,N}a(S_r)\quad(\sigma=r),
\]
whereas the corresponding original-variable rows are
\(p(0)=0\) and \(p(S_r)=0\).
Moreover \(\mathscr P_{c,r,N}a(0)=0\), so the repelling feedback remains
indexed by \(p^\sharp(0)=p(0)\).

For \((x,a)\in\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{*,\rm rc}\), set
\[
 p_{x,a}^\sharp=p_x+\varepsilon\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,
 \qquad
 Q_{x,a}^\sharp=\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty x
                    +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a
\]
and define
\begin{align}
 \|(x,a)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}}
 ={}&\|(x,a)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{*,\rm rc}}
 +\varepsilon^{-1}
       \|\gamma_x\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^{\sharp}\notag\\
 &+\sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\left\{
   \frac{\|Z_x(r)\|_N}{\varepsilon}
  +\frac{|p_{x,a}^\sharp(r)|}{\varepsilon|r|}
  +\frac{|Q_{x,a}^\sharp(r)|}{|r|}\right\}.       \label{eq:relative-graph-action-norm}
\end{align}
This is a Banach norm, uniformly equivalent to the anchored graph norm:
\begin{equation}
 \|(x,a)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{*,\rm rc}}
 \le\|(x,a)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}}
 \le C\|(x,a)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{*,\rm rc}}.       \label{eq:relative-graph-action-equivalence}
\end{equation}
Hence the anchored coupled inverse remains dimension- and
\(\delta\)-uniform in the relative graph--action norm.  This is still a
fixed-base linear estimate; it supplies no nonlinear old-history assembler
or nonlinear residual.
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[Exact nonlinear reduced-flow phase-delay defect]
\label[lemma]{lem:nonlinear-reduced-flow-phase-delay}
Retain
\cref{prop:unprepared-outer-skeleton,%
prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer,%
lem:reduced-flow-phase-defect,%
lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart}
and the common layer-sum bounds.  Use the \(C^3\) critical-curve bounds and
the shared-resource speed estimates, valid on the same-sign collar,
\begin{equation}
 |q_0'(r)|\le C,\qquad |r|\,|q_0''(r)|\le C.       \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-two-jets}
\end{equation}
Decrease \(c_\phi\) if necessary so that every phase-flow point below
remains in that collar.

Let
\(\bar r_\sigma:[-\theta_m,S_\sigma]\to J_\sigma\) be the backward
one-delay \(q_0\)-extension of the reduced path, so that
\[
 \partial_s\bar r_\sigma=\delta q_0(\bar r_\sigma),
 \qquad e_{\sigma,0}=\bar r_\sigma(0).
\]
For \(a\in\mathcal U_{\sigma,\phi}\), define
\begin{align}
 \widehat a_\sigma(s)
 &=
 \begin{cases}
  a(e_{\sigma,0}),&-\theta_m\le s<0,\\
  a(\bar r_\sigma(s)),&0\le s\le S_\sigma,
 \end{cases}\notag\\
 R_{\sigma,a}(s)
 &=\Phi_{q_0}^{\widehat a_\sigma(s)}
                  (\bar r_\sigma(s)),\notag\\
 \Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)(s)
 &=\Delta\mathcal D_N^{\rm ft}[R_{\sigma,a},0](s),
       \qquad 0\le s\le S_\sigma.                 \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-functional}
\end{align}
In the last line, the subtracted reduced delay is evaluated at
\(R_{\sigma,a}(s)\), exactly as in
\cref{eq:fold-time-tracker-delay}.

Let
\[
 \Sigma_\sigma
 =\{\theta_k:0<\theta_k<S_\sigma\}
\]
with repetitions deleted.  For continuous \(\mathbb R^N\)-valued functions that
are \(W^{1,\infty}\) on the components of
\([0,S_\sigma]\setminus\Sigma_\sigma\), put
\begin{align}
 \|F\|_{\mathrm{pw},1}
 &=\sup_s\|F(s)\|_N
   +\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{s\notin\Sigma_\sigma}
          \|\partial_sF(s)\|_N,\notag\\
 \|F\|_{\Pi,\mathrm{rel}}
 &=\sup_s\frac{\|F(s)\|_N}
                    {\delta|\bar r_\sigma(s)|}
   +\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{s\notin\Sigma_\sigma}
       \frac{\|\partial_sF(s)\|_N}
                    {\delta|\bar r_\sigma(s)|}.   \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-range-norms}
\end{align}
Then \(\Pi_{\sigma,N}\) maps a fixed relative-phase ball into this
continuous finite-seam space,
\begin{equation}
 \Pi_{\sigma,N}(0)=0,\qquad
 D\Pi_{\sigma,N}(0)a=\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a.       \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-linearization}
\end{equation}
Set
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal R_{\Pi,\sigma,N}(a)
 =\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)-\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a.        \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-remainder}
\end{equation}
There is a dimension- and branch-uniform constant \(C\) such that, with
\(A=\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}\),
\begin{align}
 \|\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)\|_{\mathrm{pw},1}
 &\le C\delta A,\notag\\
 \|\mathcal R_{\Pi,\sigma,N}(a)\|_{\Pi,\mathrm{rel}}
 &\le CA^2.                                       \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-quadratic-bound}
\end{align}
If \(a,b\) belong to the same ball and
\[
 A=\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}},\qquad
 B=\|b\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}},\qquad
 H=\|a-b\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}},
\]
then
\begin{align}
 \|\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)-\Pi_{\sigma,N}(b)\|_{\mathrm{pw},1}
 &\le C\delta H,\notag\\
 \|\mathcal R_{\Pi,\sigma,N}(a)
       -\mathcal R_{\Pi,\sigma,N}(b)\|_{\Pi,\mathrm{rel}}
 &\le C(A+B)H.                                    \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-two-point}
\end{align}
The same estimates hold after collective or transverse projection.  A
constant complete phase is annihilated exactly.  This is the nonlinear
defect of the formal \(q_0\)-flow history.  It neither identifies that
history with the raw-compatible nonlinear collar nor supplies the collar's
second endpoint jet.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Second-jet raw-compatible history assembler]
\label[lemma]{lem:second-jet-raw-history-assembler}
Retain
\cref{prop:compatible-endpoint-jet-collar,%
lem:raw-compatible-moving-core-collar,%
lem:raw-compatible-base-collar-refinement,%
lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart}
at a fixed order \(h\ge3\), together with the critical and backtrack
estimates through order \(h+3\) on the common enlargements \(J_\sigma\).
Choose \(\chi>0\) so small that
\begin{equation}
 2q_+\chi<\min\{4\chi_0,1/2\},                   \label{eq:raw-assembler-relative-width}
\end{equation}
and, for \(\sigma\in\{a,r\}\), put
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde I_{\sigma,\chi}
 =\overline{\{e:\ \text{there is }r\in I_\sigma
                    \text{ with }|e-r|<2q_+\chi|r|\}}
 \Subset J_\sigma .                              \label{eq:raw-assembler-enlarged-collar}
\end{equation}
After decreasing \(\delta_0\), the finite collar construction in the three
results above, including its critical, backtrack, and compatibility-defect
estimates, extends to these same-sign relative endpoint collars.  For the
voltage and resource compatibility defects \(b_{j,e}\) and \(d_{j,e}\)
on that extension, one has
\begin{equation}
 \max_{0\le j\le h}\delta^j
 \left\{\|\mathcal L_e^\ell b_{j,e}\|_N
             +|\mathcal L_e^\ell d_{j,e}|\right\}
 \le C_h\varepsilon |e|^{1-\ell},
 \qquad \ell=0,1,2,                              \label{eq:second-endpoint-collar-jets}
\end{equation}
uniformly for \(e\in\widetilde I_{a,\chi}\cup
\widetilde I_{r,\chi}\), where
\(\mathcal L_e=q_0(e)\partial_e\).  Thus, if
\(\Delta\phi_e=\phi_e^{\rm rc}-\phi_e^0\), then for every reference
\(e_*\in I_\sigma\) and every \(e\) in its relative collar with
\(|e|\asymp|e_*|\), in the fixed-reference cap norm introduced below,
\begin{equation}
 \|\Delta\phi_e\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}
 +|e|\,\|\mathcal L_e\Delta\phi_e\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}
 +|e|^2\,\|\mathcal L_e^2\Delta\phi_e\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}
 \le C_h\varepsilon|e|.                           \label{eq:second-endpoint-collar-cap}
\end{equation}

More precisely, fix \(e_*\in I_\sigma\) and put
\[
 e_c=\Phi_{q_0}^{c}(e_*),\qquad |c|\le\chi|e_*|.
\]
The whole tube
\begin{equation}
 \left\{\Phi_{q_0}^{t}(e_*),\
   \Phi_{q_0}^{-\tau}(\Phi_{q_0}^{t}(e_*)):
   |t|\le\chi|e_*|,\ 0\le\tau\le\delta\theta_m\right\}
                                                        \label{eq:raw-assembler-flow-backtrack-tube}
\end{equation}
lies in \(\widetilde I_{\sigma,\chi}\), and
\begin{equation}
 \frac34|e_*|\le|\Phi_{q_0}^{t}(e_*)|
       \le\frac54|e_*|,
 \qquad |t|\le\chi|e_*|.                        \label{eq:raw-assembler-relative-flow-bound}
\end{equation}
This includes the cases \(e_*=\pm\rho_\delta\) and
\(e_*=\pm r_{\rm out}\); the shifted endpoint need not remain in the
original closed interval.

All cap norms in the rest of the statement use the single reference weight
\(\kappa_{e_*}\), not the moving weight \(\kappa_{e_c}\).  For
\[
 U=(Z,p,\zeta)\in
 E_N\times\mathbb R\times\mathscr K_{N,\delta}^h
\]
set
\begin{equation}
 \|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}
 =\|Z\|_N+\frac{|p|}{|e_*|}
       +\|\zeta\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}.            \label{eq:nonlinear-collar-data-norm}
\end{equation}
The finite triangular recursion defining \(\Gamma_{e_c}^h\) has a unique
extension to an open, dimension-uniform ball
\(\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}<r_h\) in this fixed-reference norm;
write its value at the displayed affine-core data as
\(\Gamma_{e_c;e_*}^{h,\mathrm{fr}}(U;\nu,\eta)\).  This extension is
defined directly by the compatibility recursion, not by comparing the two
fading weights, and it agrees with \(\Gamma_{e_c}^h\) wherever both are
defined.  On that open ball define the exact centered voltage-history
assembler
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,U)
 =\operatorname{pr}_\phi
   \Gamma_{e_c;e_*}^{h,\mathrm{fr}}(U;\nu,\eta)
       -\phi_{e_c}^0.                             \label{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-assembler}
\end{equation}
