It is \(C^2\) into the fixed-reference \(C^h\) history space; the
anisotropic derivatives needed below have dimension-uniform bounds.  At
its base point,
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(0,0)=\Delta\phi_{e_*},
 \qquad
 \partial_c\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(0,0)
       =\gamma_{e_*}^{\rm ph}.                    \label{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-first-jets}
\end{equation}
There is \(0<r_h'<r_h\), dimension-uniform, such that for
\(|c|\le\chi|e_*|\), \(\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}\le r_h'\), and data
directions \(H,H_1,H_2\),
\begin{align}
 \|\partial_c^2\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,0)
       \|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}
 &\le C_h\frac{\varepsilon}{|e_*|},\notag\\
 \|\partial_cD_U\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,U)H
       \|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}
 &\le \frac{C_h}{|e_*|}\|H\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*},\notag\\
 \|D_U^2\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,U)[H_1,H_2]
       \|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}
 &\le C_h\prod_{i=1}^2\|H_i\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}. \label{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-C2-bounds}
\end{align}
Consequently,
\begin{align}
 &\|\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,U)
      -\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(0,0)
      -c\gamma_{e_*}^{\rm ph}
      -D_U\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(0,0)U
        \|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}\notag\\
 &\qquad\le C_h\left[
   \varepsilon|e_*|
       \left(\frac{|c|}{|e_*|}\right)^2
 +\frac{|c|}{|e_*|}\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}
  +\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}^2\right].             \label{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-taylor}
\end{align}
Here and below the remainder is exactly the four-term difference
\begin{align}
 \operatorname{Rem}\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,U)
 :={}&\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,U)
      -\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(0,0)
      -c\gamma_{e_*}^{\rm ph}\notag\\
 &{}-D_U\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(0,0)U.          \label{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-remainder-definition}
\end{align}
In particular, if
\(\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}\le\varepsilon R\le r_h'\), then
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon^{-1}
 \|\operatorname{Rem}\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,U)
       \|_{N,e_*,\delta}^{\sharp}
 \le C_h\left(\frac{|c|}{|e_*|}+R\right)^2.       \label{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-graph-remainder}
\end{equation}
The full moving formal collar is subtracted in
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-assembler}; subtracting only its tangent
would not give
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-graph-remainder}.  The construction uses a
fixed \(\theta\)-history chart and never translates an arbitrary
\(C^0\) history.  It constructs an order-\(h\) compatible old-history
assembler, not a past orbit, a nonlinear tracker, or a physical connection.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-section nonlinear graph--action residual]
\label[theorem]{thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual}
Retain
\cref{prop:nonlinear-resource-defect-normal-form,%
prop:fold-time-tracker-normal-form,%
thm:raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse,%
prop:inner-anchored-relative-phase-schur,%
prop:relative-structured-phase-response,%
lem:nonlinear-reduced-flow-phase-delay,%
lem:second-jet-raw-history-assembler}
at order \(h\ge3\).  For each \(N\), work pointwise at a fixed admissible
parameter pair
\[
 |\nu|\le\nu_*,
 \qquad
 \eta\in\mathfrak T_N^{\rm red},
 \qquad
 \|\eta\|_{\mathfrak T_N}<\eta_0.
\]
No parameter differentiability is asserted in this theorem.
Write
\[
 r_0(s)=\bar r_\sigma(s),\qquad
 r_a(s)=\Theta_a(r_0(s)),\qquad
 c_\sigma(a)=a(e_{\sigma,0}),
\]
where
\[
 e_{a,0}=r_{\rm out},\qquad e_{r,0}=-\rho_\delta,
 \qquad e_{a,*}=\rho_\delta,\qquad e_{r,*}=-\rho_\delta.
\]
The repeated value \(e_{r,0}=e_{r,*}\) has two different roles: it is the
repelling entry endpoint and also the fold-side phase anchor.

For \(x=(\gamma_x,Z_x,p_x)\) define the two canonical zero-core history
packages
\begin{align}
 \mathscr H_a^{\rm can}(x,a)
 &=\mathscr H_{r_{\rm out}}^{\rm rc}
       \bigl(c_a(a),(0,0,0)\bigr),\notag\\
 \mathscr H_r^{\rm can}(x,a)
 &=\mathscr H_{-\rho_\delta}^{\rm rc}
       \bigl(c_r(a),(0,p_x(0),0)\bigr),           \label{eq:canonical-nonlinear-history-packages}
\end{align}
and the nonredundant baseline rows
\begin{align}
 B_{\gamma,a}^{\rm nl}(x,a)
 &=\mathscr H_a^{\rm can}(x,a),\notag\\
 B_{\gamma,r}^{\rm nl}(x,a)
 &=\mathscr H_r^{\rm can}(x,a)-\mathfrak C_ep_x(0).
                                                        \label{eq:canonical-nonlinear-history-rows}
\end{align}
Thus the attracting finite resource row is \(p_x(0)=0\), whereas the
repelling row is \(p_x(S_r)=0\) and \(p_x(0)\) remains the output that is
fed into the compatible collar.  No variable \(Z_x(0)\) or arbitrary core
is inserted into
\cref{eq:canonical-nonlinear-history-packages}; doing so would create a
new linear history feedback not contained in the inverse of
\cref{prop:inner-anchored-relative-phase-schur}.

Put
\begin{align}
 D_N(x)&=\mathscr D_N^{\rm lin}[\gamma_x,Z_x],
 &D_{c,N}(x)&=\pi_N^TD_N(x),
 &D_{\perp,N}(x)&=P_{\perp,N}D_N(x),\notag\\
 \Pi_N(a)&=\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a),
 &\Pi_{c,N}(a)&=\pi_N^T\Pi_N(a),
 &\Pi_{\perp,N}(a)&=P_{\perp,N}\Pi_N(a).
                                                        \label{eq:assembled-nonlinear-delay-pieces}
\end{align}
Every coefficient carrying the subscript \(a\) below is evaluated at
\(r_a(s)\), and a subscript \(0\) means evaluation at \(r_0(s)\); for
example
\[
 C_a=\mathcal C_N(r_a),\quad
 z_a'=z_{0,N}'(r_a),\quad
 w_a'=w_{0,N}'(r_a),\quad
 \ell_a=\ell_N(r_a),\quad
 d_a=(\mathcal D_{0,c,N}-q_0)'(r_a).
\]
Define the exact reconstructed speed correction
\begin{equation}
 Q^{\rm nl}_\sigma(x,a)
 =\varepsilon^{-1}
    \{p_x-\ell_aZ_x-h_{c,N}(r_a,Z_x)\}
       +\Pi_{c,N}(a)+D_{c,N}(x),                 \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-speed}
\end{equation}
and let
\(Q^{\rm nl,\flat}_\sigma(x,a)(r_0(s))\)
denote the same function pulled back to the fixed reduced coordinate.
The two exact nonlinear normal sources, relative to the fixed-base block
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block}, are
\begin{align}
 S_{Z,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(x,a)
={}&(C_a-C_0)Z_x-(z_a'-z_0')p_x-R_{z,N}(r_a)
       +z_a'h_{c,N}(r_a,Z_x)-h_{\perp,N}(r_a,Z_x)\notag\\
 &+\varepsilon\{\Pi_{\perp,N}(a)-z_a'\Pi_{c,N}(a)
             -(z_a'-z_0')D_{c,N}(x)\},           \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-Z-source}\\
 S_{p,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(x,a)
={}&(w_a'-w_0')p_x-(w_a'\ell_a-w_0'\ell_0)Z_x
       -w_a'h_{c,N}(r_a,Z_x)\notag\\
 &+\varepsilon(w_a'-w_0')D_{c,N}(x)
       +\varepsilon w_a'\Pi_{c,N}(a)\notag\\
 &+\varepsilon^2\{(q_0(r_a)+Q^{\rm nl}_\sigma(x,a))d_a
                         -d_0Q_{\rm n}[x]\}.     \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-p-source}
\end{align}
Set
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm nl}(x,a)
 =\bigl(B_{\gamma,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(x,a),0,
            S_{Z,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(x,a),
            S_{p,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(x,a)\bigr)     \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-normal-package}
\end{equation}
and finally
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_\sigma(x,a)
 =\left(
   \mathscr N_\sigma x-\mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm nl}(x,a),
   \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^{\rm nl}(a)
      -\bigl(Q^{\rm nl,\flat}_\sigma(x,a),0\bigr)
       \right).                                  \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-residual}
\end{equation}

There are
\[
 \bar r_{\rm out}>0,
 \qquad R_0,C_b,C_2,M>0,
\]
with the following uniformity.  For every
\(0<r_{\rm out}\le\bar r_{\rm out}\), first decrease
\(\delta_0=\delta_0(r_{\rm out})\) so that all retained smallness
conditions hold, including
\(\rho_\delta\le r_{\rm out}/2\).  Then the same four constants work for
all \(0<\delta<\delta_0\), all admissible fixed parameter pairs, all
\(N\), and both branches.  In particular,
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-residual} is a well-defined \(C^1\) map
on the open \(R_0\)-ball of
\(\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}\) into
\[
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}
    \times\bigl(\mathsf H_{\phi,\infty}^0(I_\sigma)\times\R\bigr).
\]
Its base value is exactly
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)
 =\left(
  -\Delta\phi_{e_{\sigma,0}},0,
       R_{z,N}(r_0),-\varepsilon R_{q,N}(r_0),0,0
        \right),                                 \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-base-residual}
\end{equation}
with the entries grouped according to
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-boundary-operator} and the anchored phase row.
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)\|
 \le C_b\lambda_\delta,
 \qquad
 \lambda_\delta=r_{\rm out}+S_\delta^{-2}.      \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-base-bound}
\end{equation}

The differential at the fixed reduced base is precisely the already
inverted anchored raw-compatible Schur block:
\begin{equation}
 D\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)(x,a)
 =\left(
   \mathscr N_\sigma\{x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\},
   \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^0a
      -\bigl(\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty x
                  +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,0\bigr)
       \right).                                  \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-linearization}
\end{equation}
Consequently it is an isomorphism and
\begin{equation}
 \|D\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)^{-1}\|\le M.          \label{eq:fixed-section-linear-inverse-bound}
\end{equation}
If
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal Q_\sigma(u)
 =\mathcal F_\sigma(u)-\mathcal F_\sigma(0)
                      -D\mathcal F_\sigma(0)u,
 \qquad u=(x,a),                                 \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-remainder}
\end{equation}
then, for \(u,v\) in the \(R_0\)-ball,
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal Q_\sigma(u)-\mathcal Q_\sigma(v)\|
 \le C_2(\|u\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}}
          +\|v\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}})
       \|u-v\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}}.  \label{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-two-point}
\end{equation}
All norms in
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-base-bound,eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-two-point}
are the displayed product norms.

At any zero of \(\mathcal F_\sigma\), the seam relation in
\(\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\) and
\cref{eq:canonical-nonlinear-history-rows} imply
\[
 \gamma_x=\mathscr H_\sigma^{\rm can}(x,a),
 \qquad Z_x(0)=0.
\]
Use the formal complete phase history
\(R_{\sigma,a}\) from
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-functional}, including its constant-phase
old extension, and define the complete voltage history
\begin{equation}
 \widehat v_{x,a}(s)=
 \begin{cases}
  \mathbf1+R_{\sigma,a}(s)\mathbf1
       +z_{0,N}(R_{\sigma,a}(s))+\gamma_x(s),
       &-\theta_m\le s<0,\\
  \mathbf1+r_a(s)\mathbf1+z_{0,N}(r_a(s))+Z_x(s),
       &0\le s\le S_\sigma.
 \end{cases}                                      \label{eq:fixed-section-complete-voltage-history}
\end{equation}
Then the exact total delay defect, for \(0\le s\le S_\sigma\), is
\begin{equation}
 K\sum_kL_{k,N}
   \{\widehat v_{x,a}(s)-\widehat v_{x,a}(s-\theta_k)\}
       -\mathcal D_{0,N}(r_a(s))
 =\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)(s)
       +\mathscr D_N^{\rm lin}[\gamma_x,Z_x](s). \label{eq:fixed-section-exact-delay-splitting}
\end{equation}
For the old segment put
\begin{align}
 r_-(s)
 &=R_{\sigma,a}(s)+\pi_N^T\gamma_x(s),\notag\\
 Z_-(s)
 &=z_{0,N}(R_{\sigma,a}(s))+P_{\perp,N}\gamma_x(s)
       -z_{0,N}(r_-(s))\in E_N.                  \label{eq:fixed-section-physical-old-decomposition}
\end{align}
At a zero, this old pair joins continuously at \(s=0\) to
\((r_a,Z_x)\), and its reconstructed voltage is identically the first
line of \cref{eq:fixed-section-complete-voltage-history}.  Consequently
such a zero satisfies the unmodified fold-time RFDE on the forward branch
interval and the prescribed inner phase section.  The theorem only
assembles and estimates the residual; it does not assert that a zero
exists.  Even after a local zero is obtained, the zero-core collar is a
canonical compatible preparation, not a proved past orbit.  No moving
terminal event, branch-to-branch handoff, preparation-independent physical
history relation, or complete-history root is proved here.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Canonical fixed-section zeros on the two outer branches]
\label[corollary]{cor:fixed-section-canonical-zero}
Retain
\cref{thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual} and define
\begin{equation}
 R_{\rm u}
 =\min\left\{\frac{R_0}{2},\frac1{4MC_2}\right\},
 \qquad
 \lambda_{\rm N}=\frac{R_{\rm u}}{2MC_b},
 \qquad
 r_\delta=2MC_b\lambda_\delta.                  \label{eq:fixed-section-zero-radii}
\end{equation}
There is a uniform \(0<\lambda_*\le\lambda_{\rm N}\) such that whenever
\(\lambda_\delta<\lambda_*\), for each
\(\sigma\in\{a,r\}\) there is a zero
\[
 u_{\sigma,\delta}
 =(x_{\sigma,\delta},a_{\sigma,\delta})
 \in\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}
\]
of \(\mathcal F_\sigma\).  It is the unique zero in the open
\(R_{\rm u}\)-ball and satisfies
\begin{align}
 \|u_{\sigma,\delta}\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}}
 &\le r_\delta
 \le 2MC_b\lambda_\delta,                         \label{eq:fixed-section-zero-size}\\
 \|u_{\sigma,\delta}
      +\bigl[D\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)\bigr]^{-1}
          \mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)\|
 &\le MC_2r_\delta^2
 \le C\lambda_\delta^2.                           \label{eq:fixed-section-zero-newton-jet}
\end{align}
The derivative at the nonlinear zero remains an isomorphism and satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \bigl\|[D\mathcal F_\sigma(u_{\sigma,\delta})]^{-1}\bigr\|
 \le2M.                                           \label{eq:fixed-section-zero-inverse}
\end{equation}
The exact speed along this zero obeys
\begin{equation}
 q_0\!\left(\Theta_{a_{\sigma,\delta}}(r)\right)
 +Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}
      (x_{\sigma,\delta},a_{\sigma,\delta})(r)
 =q_0(r)\Theta_{a_{\sigma,\delta}}'(r)
 \le-c_*q_-<0,
 \qquad r\in I_\sigma.                            \label{eq:fixed-section-zero-speed-sign}
\end{equation}
Thus the complete voltage history in
\cref{eq:fixed-section-complete-voltage-history}, together with the
reconstructed current resource
\(
 w(s)=w_{\rm seed,N}(r_a(s))-p_x(s),
\)
determines an exact monotone forward solution segment of the unmodified
RFDE, issued from the canonical zero-core raw-compatible voltage history
and its compatible resource value and phase-anchored at the prescribed
inner section.

The smallness hypothesis is attained in the required order: first choose
\[
 0<r_{\rm out}<\min\{\bar r_{\rm out},\lambda_*/2\},
\]
and then decrease \(\delta_0(r_{\rm out})\) so that, in addition to all
retained conditions, \(S_\delta^{-2}<\lambda_*/2\).
The resulting zero is pointwise in the fixed parameter pair and unique
only in the displayed canonical graph slice.  It is not a parameterized
solution branch, a past-complete orbit, an invariant slow manifold, a
branch-to-branch handoff, a preparation-independent physical connection,
or a canard root.
\end{corollary}

\begin{lemma}[Joint diagonal wedge and causal-slice kinetic bounds]
\label[lemma]{lem:joint-diagonal-wedge-kinetic}
Retain the diagonal scales in
\cref{eq:outer-matching-radius,eq:prefix-action-diagonal-scales} and the
linear results through
\cref{thm:speed-bordered-affine-endpoint-core}.  Fix
\(\varkappa_0>0\).  There are \(\mathfrak w_*>0\), \(\delta_*>0\), and a
constant \(C\), independent of \(N,\delta,\varkappa\), the branch, and the
retained parameters, such that the following conclusions hold on the
admissible wedge
\begin{equation}
 0<\varkappa\le\varkappa_0,\qquad
 0<\delta<\delta_*,\qquad
 \mathfrak w_{\delta,\varkappa}
 :=\frac{\delta}{R_\delta^2}
 =\frac1{\varkappa^2S_\delta^3}\le\mathfrak w_* . \label{eq:joint-diagonal-admissible-wedge}
\end{equation}
The fixed constants \(p,\alpha,\vartheta,A,M\), the regularity order, the
coefficient bounds, and the common node-norm bounds are chosen as in the
retained results; \(C\) may depend on those fixed data.
In particular, all Green, terminal-observation, three-summand, speed-row,
speed-projected phase, and bordered-inverse constants used in
\cref{cor:terminal-source-split-hybrid-core,thm:speed-bordered-affine-endpoint-core}
may be chosen to be this same joint constant.  Moreover,
\begin{align}
 \sup_{\substack{0<\varkappa\le\varkappa_0\\
        \mathfrak w_{\delta,\varkappa}\le\mathfrak w_*}}
 \eta_{\mathrm{sp},\delta}
 &\longrightarrow0\qquad(\delta\downarrow0),\notag\\
 \Omega_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm aff}
 &\le C\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},
 &
 \mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}
 &:=\varkappa^2+S_\delta^{-2}
          +\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta^{7/2},          \label{eq:joint-diagonal-affine-budget}
\end{align}
where the first convergence is uniform on the wedge and
\begin{equation}
 \lambda_\delta+\rho_\delta+M_\delta
       +\frac{\rho_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
 \le C\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}.            \label{eq:joint-diagonal-small-scales}
\end{equation}

For the kinetic statement, let
\begin{equation}
 f_y=\mathscr N_\sigma^{\rm sp,\infty}y
 =f_{y,\rm B}+f_{y,\rm bd}+f_{y,T}               \label{eq:kinetic-normal-data-split}
\end{equation}
be the unique decomposition in
\cref{eq:terminal-source-three-summand-range}, and abbreviate
\begin{align}
 B_\sigma(y)&=\|f_{y,\rm B}\|_
  {\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{0,\lambda,
        \mathrm{tf},\infty}},\notag\\
 D_\sigma(y)&=\|f_{y,\rm bd}\|_
       {\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm bd}},\qquad
 T_\sigma(y)=\|f_{y,T}\|_
       {\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}}.  \label{eq:kinetic-normal-data-sizes}
\end{align}
If \(a(e_{\sigma,0})=0\), put
\(x=T_\sigma^{\rm sp}a+y\),
\begin{equation}
 p_x^\sharp=p_x+\varepsilon\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,
 \qquad
 Q_x^\sharp=\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty x
                  +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,     \label{eq:kinetic-shifted-state}
\end{equation}
and let \(\mathfrak K_\sigma(x,a)\) be the sum of
\begin{align}
 &\varepsilon^{-1}\|\gamma_x\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^{\sharp},\notag\\
 &\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0<s<s_{\sigma,T}}
 \left\{\frac{\|Z_x(s)\|_N}{\varepsilon}
 +\frac{|p_x^\sharp(s)|}{\varepsilon|\bar r_\sigma(s)|}
 +\frac{|Q_x^\sharp(\bar r_\sigma(s))|}
             {m_\delta(\bar r_\sigma(s))}\right\},\notag\\
 &\|(Z_{x,T},p_{x,T})\|_
          {\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 +\sup_{0\le t\le L_b}
      \frac{|Q_x^\sharp(\bar r_{\sigma,T}(t))|}{m_{\sigma,T}}.
                                                        \label{eq:piecewise-kinetic-observable}
\end{align}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak K_\sigma(x,a)
 \le C\left\{
   \lambda_\delta B_\sigma(y)+D_\sigma(y)+T_\sigma(y)
       +\lambda_\delta
          \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}\right\}. \label{eq:piecewise-kinetic-bound}
\end{equation}
The same estimate holds for differences.  The factor
\(\lambda_\delta\) in the last term uses the causal slice
\(a(e_{\sigma,0})=0\); it is not a consequence of the older unrestricted
relative-phase state estimate.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-envelope RFDE segment with vanishing repelling terminal speed correction]
\label[theorem]{thm:fixed-envelope-speed-row-zero}
Retain
\cref{lem:joint-diagonal-wedge-kinetic,lem:second-jet-raw-history-assembler,%
thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual}
at order \(h\ge3\).  There is a joint \(\varkappa_*>0\) such that all
statements below hold when the wedge in
\cref{eq:joint-diagonal-admissible-wedge} is formed with
\(0<\varkappa_0\le\varkappa_*\).  Work pointwise at one admissible
parameter pair.
For
\begin{equation}
 u=(y,a,\tau),\qquad x=T_\sigma^{\rm sp}a+y,      \label{eq:fixed-envelope-nonlinear-coordinates}
\end{equation}
use the exact maps
\(Q_\sigma^{\rm nl},S_{Z,\sigma}^{\rm nl},
S_{p,\sigma}^{\rm nl}\) and
\(B_{\gamma,\sigma}^{\rm nl}\) from
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-speed,%
eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-Z-source,%
eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-p-source,%
eq:canonical-nonlinear-history-rows}.
Define the new finite nonlinear source by
\begin{align}
 B_{a,\rm fin}^{\rm sp,nl}(x,a)&=0,\notag\\
 B_{r,\rm fin}^{\rm sp,nl}(x,a)
 &=\mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}x
   -\varepsilon Q_r^{\rm nl,\flat}(x,a)(e_{r,1}),          \label{eq:fixed-envelope-nonlinear-finite-source}
\end{align}
and put
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm sp,nl}(x,a)
 =\bigl(B_{\gamma,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(x,a),
 B_{\sigma,\rm fin}^{\rm sp,nl}(x,a),
 S_{Z,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(x,a),S_{p,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(x,a)
 \bigr).                                         \label{eq:fixed-envelope-nonlinear-normal-source}
\end{equation}
Set
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr R_\sigma^{\rm sp,nl}(x,a)
 =\mathscr N_\sigma^{\rm sp,\infty}x
       -\mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm sp,nl}(x,a).      \label{eq:fixed-envelope-exact-normal-residual}
\end{equation}
Thus the repelling finite component of
\(\mathscr R_r^{\rm sp,nl}(x,a)\) is exactly
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon Q_r^{\rm nl,\flat}(x,a)(e_{r,1}).  \label{eq:fixed-envelope-exact-speed-row}
\end{equation}

Split the two distributed components of this exact normal residual by
the indicators in \cref{eq:affine-hard-source-split}; retain its history
and scalar finite component in the boundary summand.  Denote the resulting
unique normal-range decomposition by
\(\mathscr R_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\rm nl}\),
\(\mathscr R_{\sigma,\rm bd}^{\rm nl}\), and
\(\mathscr R_{\sigma,T}^{\rm nl}\).  Define
\begin{align}
 \mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm fe}(u)
 =\bigl(&\mathscr R_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\rm nl}
          +\mathscr R_{\sigma,\rm bd}^{\rm nl}
          +\mathscr R_{\sigma,T}^{\rm nl},\notag\\
 &\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a)
       -Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}(x,a),
   a(e_{\sigma,0}),a(e_{\sigma,1})+\tau\bigr).  \label{eq:fixed-envelope-exact-residual}
\end{align}
This is an exact fixed-envelope boundary-value residual for the unmodified
RFDE equations, with differential rows interpreted a.e. at the one hard
source interface.  It satisfies
\begin{align}
 \mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm fe}(0)
 &=-\bigl(f_\sigma^{\rm aff},0,0,0\bigr),\notag\\
 D\mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm fe}(0)
 &=\mathbb L_\sigma^{\rm aff}.                  \label{eq:fixed-envelope-base-and-linearization}
\end{align}

We state the nonlinear estimate in the anisotropic correction norm that
resolves the non-small affine bulk datum.  Let
\begin{align}
 \|h\|_{\sigma,*}
 :={}&B_\sigma(h_y)
 +\frac{D_\sigma(h_y)+T_\sigma(h_y)}{\lambda_\delta}
 +\frac1{\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}}
   \left\{\|h_a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
          +\frac{|h_\tau|}{\rho_\delta}\right\},          \label{eq:fixed-envelope-star-domain-norm}\\
 \|(f,k,b_0,b_E)\|_{\sigma,*}^{\rm ran}
 :={}&\|f_{\rm B}\|+\frac{\|f_{\rm bd}\|+\|f_T\|}
                                  {\lambda_\delta}\notag\\
 &+\frac1{\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}}
   \left\{\|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
       +\frac{|b_0|+|b_E|}{\rho_\delta}\right\}.          \label{eq:fixed-envelope-star-range-norm}
\end{align}
Here the three unlabeled normal norms are those of
\cref{eq:terminal-source-three-summand-range}.  On the causal correction
slice \(h_a(e_{\sigma,0})=0\), equivalently on the range slice \(b_0=0\),
the restricted inverse of \(\mathbb L_\sigma^{\rm aff}\) is uniformly
bounded between these two star norms.

Let
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb Q_\sigma^{\rm fe}(u)
 =\mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm fe}(u)
   -\mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm fe}(0)
   -\mathbb L_\sigma^{\rm aff}u.                \label{eq:fixed-envelope-nonlinear-remainder}
\end{equation}
There are joint constants \(C_Q,r_*>0\) such that the affine predictor
\(u_\sigma^{\rm aff}\) in
\cref{eq:affine-endpoint-predictor} satisfies
\begin{equation}
 B_\sigma(y_\sigma^{\rm aff})\le C_Q,\quad
 D_\sigma(y_\sigma^{\rm aff})
      +T_\sigma(y_\sigma^{\rm aff})\le C_Q\lambda_\delta,\quad
 \|a_\sigma^{\rm aff}\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
      +\frac{|\tau_\sigma^{\rm aff}|}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C_Q\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}.         \label{eq:fixed-envelope-affine-box}
\end{equation}
After decreasing \(r_*\) uniformly, the exact residual restricts to a
\(C^1\) map from the affine causal star tube
\[
 \{u_\sigma^{\rm aff}+h:
       h_a(e_{\sigma,0})=0,\ \|h\|_{\sigma,*}<r_*\}
\]
into the corresponding three-summand range slice.
For \(h,k\) on the causal slice with
\(\|h\|_{\sigma,*},\|k\|_{\sigma,*}\le r_*\),
the exact nonlinear remainder belongs to the displayed three-summand
range and obeys
\begin{align}
 \|\mathbb Q_\sigma^{\rm fe}(u_\sigma^{\rm aff})\|_
        {\sigma,*}^{\rm ran}
 &\le C_Q\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},\notag\\
 \|\mathbb Q_\sigma^{\rm fe}(u_\sigma^{\rm aff}+h)
       -\mathbb Q_\sigma^{\rm fe}(u_\sigma^{\rm aff}+k)\|_
        {\sigma,*}^{\rm ran}
 &\le C_Q\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}
       \|h-k\|_{\sigma,*}.                     \label{eq:fixed-envelope-star-two-point}
\end{align}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\|h\|_{\sigma,*}\le r_*}
 \|D\mathbb Q_\sigma^{\rm fe}
       (u_\sigma^{\rm aff}+h)\|_{\mathcal L(*,*)}
 \le C_Q\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},         \label{eq:fixed-envelope-star-derivative}
\end{equation}
where \(\mathcal L(*,*)\) denotes operators between the causal domain and
range star slices.
More precisely, the bulk, boundary-plus-terminal, and phase components
on the first line have sizes
\begin{equation}
 C_Q\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},\qquad
 C_Q\lambda_\delta\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},\qquad
 C_Q\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}^2,           \label{eq:fixed-envelope-remainder-components}
\end{equation}
respectively; their two-point versions have the same coefficients times
\(\|h-k\|_{\sigma,*}\).  The entry and event-slack components of
\(\mathbb Q_\sigma^{\rm fe}\) vanish identically.

After fixing \(0<\varkappa_0\le\varkappa_*\), then decreasing
\(\mathfrak w_*\), and finally \(\delta_*\), there is a unique zero on
the causal slice,
\begin{equation}
 u_\sigma^{\rm fe}
 =(y_\sigma^{\rm fe},a_\sigma^{\rm fe},
          \tau_\sigma^{\rm fe})                 \label{eq:fixed-envelope-nonlinear-zero}
\end{equation}
in the affine star ball
\begin{equation}
 \|u-u_\sigma^{\rm aff}\|_{\sigma,*}
 \le C\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}.           \label{eq:fixed-envelope-zero-star-radius}
\end{equation}
It satisfies the sharper component estimates
\begin{align}
 B_\sigma(y_\sigma^{\rm fe}-y_\sigma^{\rm aff})
 &\le C\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},\notag\\
 D_\sigma(y_\sigma^{\rm fe}-y_\sigma^{\rm aff})
 +T_\sigma(y_\sigma^{\rm fe}-y_\sigma^{\rm aff})
 &\le C\lambda_\delta\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},\notag\\
 \|a_\sigma^{\rm fe}-a_\sigma^{\rm aff}\|_
       {\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 +\frac{|\tau_\sigma^{\rm fe}-\tau_\sigma^{\rm aff}|}
       {\rho_\delta}
 &\le C\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}^2.       \label{eq:fixed-envelope-zero-component-correction}
\end{align}
Restricted to the causal tangent slice and the corresponding range slice,
the differential at this zero remains an isomorphism in the star norms.

Put \(x_\sigma^{\rm fe}=T_\sigma^{\rm sp}a_\sigma^{\rm fe}
+y_\sigma^{\rm fe}\).  The zero assembles an exact monotone forward
solution segment of the unmodified RFDE from its raw-compatible old
history, and
\begin{equation}
 q_0(\Theta_{a_\sigma^{\rm fe}}(r))
 +Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}
       (x_\sigma^{\rm fe},a_\sigma^{\rm fe})(r)
 =q_0(r)\Theta_{a_\sigma^{\rm fe}}'(r)
 \le-c_*q_-<0.                                  \label{eq:fixed-envelope-zero-speed-sign}
\end{equation}
On the repelling branch, in addition,
\begin{equation}
 Q_r^{\rm nl,\flat}(x_r^{\rm fe},a_r^{\rm fe})(e_{r,1})=0.
                                                        \label{eq:fixed-envelope-terminal-speed-correction-zero}
\end{equation}
This vanishing concerns the normal-plus-phase terminal speed
\emph{correction}; the total physical speed is the strictly negative
quantity in \cref{eq:fixed-envelope-zero-speed-sign}, not zero.
The last row gives only
\begin{equation}
 \tau_\sigma^{\rm fe}=-a_\sigma^{\rm fe}(e_{\sigma,1}),
 \qquad
 \Theta_{a_\sigma^{\rm fe}}(e_{\sigma,1})
 =\Phi_{q_0}^{-\tau_\sigma^{\rm fe}}(e_{\sigma,1}).       \label{eq:fixed-envelope-slack-mismatch}
\end{equation}
Thus \(\tau\) records a terminal phase mismatch.  This theorem does not
construct a moving-duration column or an original-coordinate endpoint;
in particular it does not imply \(c_{\rm ter}=1\),
\(K_p=R_{q,N}(e_{r,1})\), or
\(\tau=\delta\Delta S\).  Its old history is the canonical zero-core
raw-compatible assembler, not an arbitrary preparation or a past orbit.
By itself it does not construct the terminal second slow jet proved next,
a physical connection, or a canard root.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Post-flushing terminal second jet of the fixed-envelope segment]
\label[theorem]{thm:fixed-envelope-terminal-slow-jet}
Retain
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green,%
thm:fixed-envelope-speed-row-zero}
at order \(h\ge4\), with \(M\ge2\).  There is a fixed flushing constant
\(A_{\rm fl}>0\) such that the following conclusions hold if the constant
\(A\) in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-scales} is chosen strictly larger than
\(A_{\rm fl}\), before \(L_0=AS_\delta\) and \(L_b\) are frozen.  Put
\begin{equation}
 L_{\rm fl}=A_{\rm fl}S_\delta.                 \label{eq:terminal-slow-jet-flush-length}
\end{equation}
All constants are uniform in \(N\), the branch, the retained parameters,
and the admissible joint wedge of
\cref{eq:joint-diagonal-admissible-wedge}.

Let
\begin{equation}
 r_\sigma^{\rm fe}(s)
 =\Theta_{a_\sigma^{\rm fe}}(\bar r_\sigma(s)),\qquad
 Z_\sigma^{\rm fe}(s)=Z_{x_\sigma^{\rm fe}}(s),\qquad
 p_\sigma^{\rm fe}(s)=p_{x_\sigma^{\rm fe}}(s)             \label{eq:fixed-envelope-physical-terminal-fields}
\end{equation}
be the physical fold-time segment reconstructed from the zero in
\cref{thm:fixed-envelope-speed-row-zero}.  For a selector offset \(L\ge0\)
assume the post-flushing margin
\begin{equation}
 L_b-L-(M+2)\theta_m\ge L_{\rm fl}.             \label{eq:terminal-slow-jet-selector-margin}
\end{equation}
With the definition of \(L_b\) in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-scales}, this includes every
\begin{equation}
 0\le L\le (A-A_{\rm fl})S_\delta+1,             \label{eq:terminal-slow-jet-offset-range}
\end{equation}
in particular the actual terminal-history window \(L=0\) and a terminal
transition zone of length proportional to \(S_\delta\).  Put
\begin{align}
 \widetilde J_{\sigma,L}
 &=[S_\sigma-L-2\theta_m,S_\sigma-L],\notag\\
 J_{\sigma,L}
 &=[S_\sigma-L-\theta_m,S_\sigma-L].             \label{eq:terminal-slow-jet-windows}
\end{align}
Then \(Z_\sigma^{\rm fe}\) and \(p_\sigma^{\rm fe}\) are \(C^2\) on
these windows and
\begin{align}
 &\sup_{\widetilde J_{\sigma,L}}
   \left\{\|\partial_sZ_\sigma^{\rm fe}\|_N
       +\frac{|\partial_sp_\sigma^{\rm fe}|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}
 +\delta\sup_{J_{\sigma,L}}
   \left\{\|\partial_s^2Z_\sigma^{\rm fe}\|_N
       +\frac{|\partial_s^2p_\sigma^{\rm fe}|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta}.    \label{eq:terminal-tangent-component-bound}
\end{align}
On the repelling branch the future scalar datum used in this estimate is
the exact identity
\begin{equation}
 \partial_sp_r^{\rm fe}(S_r)
 =\delta R_{q,N}\bigl(r_r^{\rm fe}(S_r)\bigr).   \label{eq:terminal-tangent-future-row}
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \|\partial_sZ_\sigma^{\rm fe}\|_{C^0(J_{\sigma,L})}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta},
 \qquad
 \|\partial_s^2Z_\sigma^{\rm fe}\|_{C^0(J_{\sigma,L})}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}.         \label{eq:fixed-envelope-terminal-slow-jet}
\end{equation}
For any uniformly bounded scalar \(c_{q,\sigma}\), define the formal
normal-history column on the last available fixed-envelope window by
\begin{equation}
 (\widehat K_{Z,L}^{\rm fe}\beta)(\theta)
 =c_{q,\sigma}\frac{\partial_sZ_\sigma^{\rm fe}
       (S_\sigma-L+\theta)}{\delta}\,\beta,
 \qquad-\theta_m\le\theta\le0.                  \label{eq:fixed-envelope-formal-history-column}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \|\widehat K_{Z,L}^{\rm fe}\|_
       {\mathcal L(\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta},
                \mathcal H_{Z,T}^1)}\le C.       \label{eq:fixed-envelope-history-shear-bound}
\end{equation}
If a subsequently constructed duration selector satisfies all the right-envelope, containment,
and endpoint hypotheses of
\cref{lem:endpoint-centered-duration-selector}, is centered at this same
base time and history window---\(S_*=S_\sigma\) and
\(e=r_\sigma^{\rm fe}(S_\sigma)\)---and has
\(Z_*=Z_\sigma^{\rm fe}\) there and
\(c_{q,\sigma}=q_0(e)/q_{*,e}\), its actual \(K_{Z,L}\) equals
\(\widehat K_{Z,L}^{\rm fe}\) and is therefore uniformly bounded.  Thus the
present theorem closes the slow-jet size subcondition in
\cref{eq:terminal-history-slow-jet-gate}, not the existence of that
duration selector.  A selector at a later original endpoint does not inherit
this estimate without a separate identification of its observation window
with \(J_{\sigma,L}\), or a new jet theorem on its right envelope.

The conclusion concerns the \(E_N\)-valued normal voltage \(Z\), after the
collective time tangent has been separated.  It is false for the raw full
voltage or resource history, whose collective derivatives contain the
nonzero physical drift.  The theorem supplies neither a right-hand
fixed envelope of length \(O(\rho_\delta/\delta)\), nor a moving-duration
or original-endpoint zero, parameter jets, a past orbit, a connection, or
a canard root.
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[Terminal-clock pullback and exact duration border]
\label[lemma]{lem:terminal-clock-pullback-border}
Let \(\mathcal E\) be a finite-dimensional state space and consider an
autonomous discrete-delay equation
\begin{equation}
 \delta X'(t)=\mathfrak F
   \bigl(X(t),X(t-\theta_1),\ldots,X(t-\theta_m)\bigr),
 \qquad 0=\theta_0<\theta_1<\cdots<\theta_m.     \label{eq:clock-physical-rfde}
\end{equation}
For each allowed clock parameter below, the equation is imposed for
\(0\le t\le S+d_\beta\), with history on \([-\theta_m,0]\).
Assume that \(\mathfrak F\) is \(C^2\) on the relevant tube.  The case
with no positive delay is obtained by deleting all delayed slots.  Choose
\(L_\chi>0\), assume \(S>\theta_m+L_\chi\), and choose
\(\chi\in C^2([-\theta_m,S])\), \(0\le\chi\le1\), with
\begin{equation}
 \chi=0\quad\hbox{on }[-\theta_m,S-\theta_m-L_\chi],
 \qquad
 \chi=1\quad\hbox{on }[S-\theta_m,S],           \label{eq:clock-cutoff-plateaux}
\end{equation}
where in the no-delay case the second plateau reduces to the endpoint.
For a physical phase-length parameter \(\beta\), put
\begin{equation}
 d_\beta=\frac{\beta}{\delta},\qquad
 \Xi_\beta(s)=s+d_\beta\chi(s),                 \label{eq:terminal-clock-map}
\end{equation}
and assume
\begin{equation}
 |d_\beta|\,\|\chi'\|_\infty\le\frac14.         \label{eq:terminal-clock-diffeomorphism-margin}
\end{equation}
Then \(\Xi_\beta\) is an increasing \(C^2\) diffeomorphism from
\([-\theta_m,S]\) onto
\([-\theta_m,S+d_\beta]\).  Its pulled-back delay feet are
\begin{equation}
 \sigma_{k,\beta}(s)
 =\Xi_\beta^{-1}\bigl(\Xi_\beta(s)-\theta_k\bigr),          \label{eq:terminal-clock-delay-feet}
\end{equation}
and satisfy, exactly,
\begin{align}
 \sigma_{k,\beta}(s)
 &=s-\theta_k+d_\beta
      \{\chi(s)-\chi(\sigma_{k,\beta}(s))\},\notag\\
 \partial_s\sigma_{k,\beta}(s)
 &=\frac{1+d_\beta\chi'(s)}
        {1+d_\beta\chi'(\sigma_{k,\beta}(s))},\notag\\
 \partial_\beta\sigma_{k,\beta}(s)
 &=\frac{\chi(s)-\chi(\sigma_{k,\beta}(s))}
  {\delta\{1+d_\beta\chi'(\sigma_{k,\beta}(s))\}}.
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-clock-foot-identities}
\end{align}
In particular \(\sigma_{0,\beta}(s)=s\) and
\(\sigma_{k,\beta}(s)<s\) for every positive delay \(k\ge1\); every foot
remains in the fixed old-history/current interval, and
\begin{equation}
 \Xi_\beta^{-1}(\Xi_\beta(S)+\theta)=S+\theta,
 \qquad -\theta_m\le\theta\le0.                \label{eq:terminal-clock-history-fixed}
\end{equation}

For \(0\le s\le S\) and \(Y=X\circ\Xi_\beta\),
\cref{eq:clock-physical-rfde} is equivalent, without an extension of
\(Y\) to the right of \(S\), to the fixed-interval equation
\begin{equation}
 \delta Y_s(s)=\{1+d_\beta\chi'(s)\}
 \mathfrak F\bigl(Y(s),Y(\sigma_{1,\beta}(s)),\ldots,
                         Y(\sigma_{m,\beta}(s))\bigr).
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-clock-pulled-rfde}
\end{equation}
The old history and every causal-entry row are unchanged exactly.  The
history in \cref{eq:terminal-clock-history-fixed} is the actual physical
history at time \(S+d_\beta\), not a moving-window surrogate.

Let \(Y_*\in C^2\) solve \cref{eq:clock-physical-rfde} on the fixed
interval at \(\beta=0\), and write \(L_0\) for the linearization of the
distributed left-minus-right residual in
\cref{eq:terminal-clock-pulled-rfde}, together with its unchanged
old-history and causal-entry rows.  The
duration column at fixed \(Y_*\) is
\begin{align}
 C_\chi(s)={}&-\frac{\chi'(s)}{\delta}\mathfrak F_*(s)\notag\\
 &-\frac1\delta\sum_{k=1}^m
 D_k\mathfrak F_*(s)Y_*'(s-\theta_k)
       \{\chi(s)-\chi(s-\theta_k)\}.            \label{eq:terminal-clock-duration-column}
\end{align}
In operator identities, \(C_\chi\) is augmented by zero components in the
unchanged old-history and causal-entry rows.
Define the infinitesimal clock shear
\begin{equation}
 A_\chi(s)=\frac{\chi(s)}{\delta}Y_*'(s).        \label{eq:terminal-clock-domain-shear}
\end{equation}
Then the exact commutator identity is
\begin{equation}
 C_\chi+L_0A_\chi=0                             \label{eq:terminal-clock-commutator}
\end{equation}
in the distributed equation, the old-history row, and the causal-entry
rows.

Let the remaining upper rows be genuine terminal observations \(B(Y_S)\),
including, when present, a finite normal row and a terminal-history
observation, and append the normalized physical event
\begin{equation}
 E(Y)=\frac{r_Y(S)-e}{q_{0,e}},\qquad q_{0,e}\ne0.          \label{eq:terminal-clock-event-row}
\end{equation}
If that terminal history is to be a free interface, introduce an
additional unknown \(h\) in its history space and replace the corresponding
row by \(\mathcal T(Y_S)-h\).  In the formulas below one then reads
\(v\) as \((v,h)\), \(A_\chi\) as \((A_\chi,0)\), and \(DB\) as the
derivative of this augmented row.  Thus a ``free interface'' is an actual
triangular unknown, not an observation disguised as boundary data.
Define the complete upper block and its raw clock column by
\begin{equation}
 L_{\rm up}v=(L_0v,DB(Y_*)v),\qquad
 C_{\rm up}=(C_\chi,0),                           \label{eq:terminal-clock-upper-block}
\end{equation}
and suppose that \(L_{\rm up}\) is an isomorphism.  Put
\begin{equation}
 \ell v=DE(Y_*)v,\qquad
 c_{\rm ter}=\ell A_\chi
 =\frac{r_*'(S)}{\delta q_{0,e}}.               \label{eq:terminal-clock-event-coefficient}
\end{equation}
Assume \(q_{*,e}=\delta^{-1}r_*'(S)\ne0\).  If the terminal column
factors as
\begin{equation}
 DB(Y_*)A_\chi=Kc_{\rm ter},                    \label{eq:terminal-clock-column-factorization}
\end{equation}
then the full upper commutator is
\begin{equation}
 C_{\rm up}+L_{\rm up}A_\chi=Kc_{\rm ter}.      \label{eq:terminal-clock-full-commutator}
\end{equation}
The complete clock-bordered differential is conjugate, by the domain
lift \(v=w+A_\chi\beta\) and the range shear
\((f,b)\mapsto(f-Kb,b)\), to
\begin{equation}
 \begin{pmatrix}L_{\rm up}-K\ell&0\\ \ell&c_{\rm ter}\end{pmatrix}.
                                                        \label{eq:terminal-clock-triangular-border}
\end{equation}
Consequently its raw Schur scalar is
\begin{equation}
 -\ell L_{\rm up}^{-1}C_{\rm up}
 =c_{\rm ter}\{1-\ell L_{\rm up}^{-1}K\},    \label{eq:terminal-clock-raw-schur}
\end{equation}
whereas the event-aligned Schur coefficient in
\cref{eq:terminal-clock-triangular-border} is exactly \(c_{\rm ter}\).
On the terminal plateau the two displayed components of \(K\) are
\begin{equation}
 K_p=\frac{q_{0,e}p_*'(S)}{\delta q_{*,e}},\qquad
 (K_Z\beta)(\theta)
 =\frac{q_{0,e}Z_*'(S+\theta)}{\delta q_{*,e}}\,\beta,
 \quad q_{*,e}=\delta^{-1}r_*'(S).              \label{eq:terminal-clock-physical-shears}
\end{equation}

The composition map in \cref{eq:terminal-clock-pulled-rfde} is \(C^1\)
from a \(C^2\) swept terminal tube to a \(W^{1,\infty}\) terminal
residual, and from a \(C^1\) swept tube to a \(C^0\) residual.  Merely
placing the tube in \(W^{1,\infty}\) does not justify a Fr\'echet
derivative in the \(L^\infty\) operator topology.  Thus
\cref{eq:terminal-clock-triangular-border} is an operator theorem only on
the corresponding strong terminal restriction; it is not licensed by a
formal translation of a rough history.
\end{lemma}

\begin{corollary}[Uniform physical-event border on a reserved slow-clock buffer]
\label[corollary]{cor:fixed-envelope-terminal-clock-border}
Retain
\cref{thm:fixed-envelope-speed-row-zero,%
thm:fixed-envelope-terminal-slow-jet}
at order \(h\ge4\).  Choose the terminal-buffer constant
\(A>A_{\rm fl}\), fix
\begin{equation}
 0<a_\chi<A-A_{\rm fl},\qquad
 L_{\chi,\delta}=a_\chi S_\delta,               \label{eq:slow-clock-transition-length}
\end{equation}
and reserve this part of the post-flushing buffer for the clock.  Choose
the cutoff in \cref{lem:terminal-clock-pullback-border} so that
\begin{align}
 \chi_\delta&=0
   &&\text{on }[-\theta_m,S_\sigma-\theta_m-L_{\chi,\delta}],\notag\\
 \chi_\delta&=1
   &&\text{on }[S_\sigma-\theta_m,S_\sigma],\notag\\
 S_\delta\|\chi_\delta'\|_\infty
   +S_\delta^2\|\chi_\delta''\|_\infty&\le C_\chi .
                                                        \label{eq:slow-clock-cutoff-bounds}
\end{align}
Write \(\chi=\chi_\delta\) in the formulas below.
This choice uses the original admissible joint wedge; no additional
relation between \(S_\delta\) and \(\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}\) is
imposed.

Let \(Y_\sigma^{\rm fe}=(r_\sigma^{\rm fe},Z_\sigma^{\rm fe},
p_\sigma^{\rm fe})\) be the exact fixed-envelope segment, let
\(S_\sigma\) be its fixed fold-time duration, and put
\begin{equation}
 \widehat e_\sigma=r_\sigma^{\rm fe}(S_\sigma),\qquad
 e_\sigma=e_{\sigma,1},\qquad
 E_\sigma^{\rm fe}
 =\frac{\widehat e_\sigma-e_\sigma}{q_0(e_\sigma)}.
                                                        \label{eq:fixed-envelope-original-event-defect}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{align}
 \frac{|E_\sigma^{\rm fe}|}{\rho_\delta}
 &\le C\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},&
 \frac{|\tau_\sigma^{\rm fe}|}{\rho_\delta}
 +\frac{|\tau_\sigma^{\rm fe}|}{\delta S_\delta}
 &\le C\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}.         \label{eq:short-clock-base-defect}
\end{align}
The protected offset range in
\cref{eq:terminal-slow-jet-offset-range} contains the cutoff, its delayed
feet, and one fixed safety collar.  Throughout the normalized ball
\begin{equation}
 |\beta|\le C_\beta\rho_\delta
       \mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}.           \label{eq:short-clock-parameter-ball}
\end{equation}
the clock is a diffeomorphism after the usual ordered decrease of the
joint wedge.  Moreover, uniformly for \(0\le s\le S_\sigma\),
\begin{align}
 \|\sigma_{k,\beta}-(\,\cdot-\theta_k)\|_{C^0}
 &\le C\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},\notag\\
 \|\partial_s\sigma_{k,\beta}-1\|_{C^0}
 &\le C S_\delta^{-1}\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa},\notag\\
 \rho_\delta\|\partial_\beta\sigma_{k,\beta}\|_{C^0}
 +\rho_\delta^2\|\partial_\beta^2\sigma_{k,\beta}\|_{C^0}
 &\le C.                                         \label{eq:slow-clock-foot-bounds}
\end{align}

Let
\begin{equation}
 J_{\sigma,\chi}
 =[S_\sigma-L_{\chi,\delta}-2\theta_m-1,S_\sigma],         \label{eq:slow-clock-swept-window}
\end{equation}
intersected with the last branch interval if necessary.  Let
\(\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,0}(J_{\sigma,\chi})\) be the closed
subspace of
\(\mathsf E_{\sigma,\delta}^{T,\infty}\) whose two distributed source
components have continuous representatives on \(J_{\sigma,\chi}\), with
the inherited direct sup norm.  Define \(\mathcal Y_{\sigma,\chi}^0\) by
replacing the terminal-source summand in
\cref{eq:terminal-source-three-summand-range} by this subspace and retaining
the same continuous phase source and finite rows.

On the causal tangent space at the fixed-envelope zero require the
reconstructed terminal normal tangent to be \(C^1\) on
\(J_{\sigma,\chi}\), and define
\begin{equation}
 \|v\|_{\mathcal J_{\sigma,\chi}^1}
 =\frac1\varepsilon\sup_{J_{\sigma,\chi}}
   \left\{\|Z_v\|_N+\frac{|p_v|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}
 +\frac\delta\varepsilon\sup_{J_{\sigma,\chi}}
   \left\{\|Z_v'\|_N+\frac{|p_v'|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:short-clock-strong-terminal-norm}
\end{equation}
Write \(\mathcal X_{\sigma,\chi}^1\) for the intersection of the
source-split causal domain of the fixed-envelope differential with the
finite norm in \cref{eq:short-clock-strong-terminal-norm}, and give it the
sum of the old transported natural graph norm and this terminal norm.
These are explicit closed source and solution restrictions: the continuous
terminal-source Green formula maps \(\mathcal Y_{\sigma,\chi}^0\) onto
\(\mathcal X_{\sigma,\chi}^1\) with the uniform estimate in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-source-bound}.  In particular the range is not
defined as the image of the operator.  The restriction charges exactly
the one continuous derivative required by the clock composition and does
not strengthen the old history at the causal entry.

Let \(\mathscr A_{\chi,\sigma}^{\rm fe}\) be the coordinate lift of the
physical clock tangent: under the exact fixed-envelope reconstruction,
\begin{equation}
 D_u(r,Z,p)\big|_{u_\sigma^{\rm fe}}
       \mathscr A_{\chi,\sigma}^{\rm fe}=A_\chi,\qquad
 (\mathscr A_{\chi,\sigma}^{\rm fe})_a(e_{\sigma,0})=0,\qquad
 (\mathscr A_{\chi,\sigma}^{\rm fe})_\tau
 =-(\mathscr A_{\chi,\sigma}^{\rm fe})_a(e_{\sigma,1}).   \label{eq:slow-clock-coordinate-lift}
\end{equation}
The exact collective/normal chart determines its \((y,a)\)-components
uniquely; the last equality determines the auxiliary slack component and
makes the triangular phase--slack row vanish on the lift.

The clock lift is not uniformly bounded in the old hybrid phase norm.
Indeed, its phase component changes by order one across an
\(O(\rho_\delta)\) spatial interval, whereas that norm divides the phase
derivative by the vanishing terminal weight.  The correct moving-duration
space is therefore the clock-aligned pullback space.  If \(v\) is a
fixed-envelope coordinate tangent, \(h\in\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1\) is the free
terminal-history interface, and \(\beta\in\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}\), set
\begin{equation}
 \|(v,h,\beta)\|_{\mathcal X_{\sigma,\chi}^{\rm clk}}
 :=\|v-\mathscr A_{\chi,\sigma}^{\rm fe}\beta\|_
          {\mathcal X_{\sigma,\chi}^1}
   +\|h\|_{\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1}
   +\frac{|\beta|}{\rho_\delta}.                \label{eq:slow-clock-aligned-domain-norm}
\end{equation}
Write \(K_\sigma^{\rm fe}\) for the complete upper shear in
\cref{eq:terminal-clock-column-factorization}; it has the displayed
finite normal and terminal-history components and zero components in all
unchanged rows.  For an upper datum \(F\), interface datum \(g\), and
event datum \(b\), put
\begin{equation}
 \|(F,g,b)\|_{\mathcal Y_{\sigma,\chi}^{\rm clk}}
 :=\|(F,g)-K_\sigma^{\rm fe}b\|_
          {\mathcal Y_{\sigma,\chi}^0\times\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1}
   +\frac{|b|}{\rho_\delta}.                    \label{eq:slow-clock-aligned-range-norm}
\end{equation}
For each fixed \(\delta\) these norms describe the same algebraic domain
and range as the raw products.  Their point is uniformity: they quotient
the artificial phase gradient of a terminal time change before applying
the fixed-envelope estimates.  No uniform equivalence with the raw
product norms is asserted.

At \((u_\sigma^{\rm fe},0)\), the complete physical-event differential,
including the repelling speed-aligned finite row and a free terminal normal
history interface, is a dimension- and diagonal-uniform isomorphism from
the clock-aligned domain in
\cref{eq:slow-clock-aligned-domain-norm} onto the clock-aligned range in
\cref{eq:slow-clock-aligned-range-norm}.  More precisely, the two norms
are the pullbacks by the domain and range shears that give
\cref{eq:terminal-clock-triangular-border}, with the physical
\(A_\chi\) represented by \(\mathscr A_{\chi,\sigma}^{\rm fe}\), and with
\begin{equation}
 \frac12\le |c_{\sigma,\rm ter}^{\rm fe}|\le\frac32,
 \qquad
 \kappa_{p,\delta}^{\rm fe}=o(1),
 \qquad
 \|K_{Z,0}^{\rm fe}\|_
  {\mathcal L(\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta},\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1)}
 \le C.                                          \label{eq:short-clock-border-bounds}
\end{equation}
Here the repelling scalar shear is evaluated at the actual base endpoint
\(\widehat e_r\); explicitly,
\begin{equation}
 K_{p,r}^{\rm fe}
 =\frac{q_0(e_r)}{q_0(\widehat e_r)}
       R_{q,N}(\widehat e_r),                   \label{eq:short-clock-base-scalar-shear}
\end{equation}
because the fixed-envelope speed correction vanishes there.  The normal
history shear in \cref{eq:short-clock-border-bounds} is the actual clock
column on the fixed terminal history, not the formal column of
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-formal-history-column}.

This corollary closes the previously conditional moving-duration
\emph{differential} without a right-hand physical envelope or a
separate matching subwedge.  It does not
apply an implicit-function theorem to
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-original-event-defect}; therefore it does not yet
construct an original-endpoint nonlinear zero, a parameter family, a past
orbit, a connection, or a physical root.
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[Original-endpoint RFDE segment from the slow terminal clock]
\label[theorem]{thm:short-clock-original-endpoint-segment}
Retain
\cref{thm:fixed-envelope-speed-row-zero,%
thm:fixed-envelope-terminal-slow-jet,%
cor:fixed-envelope-terminal-clock-border}
and the strong terminal spaces in
\cref{eq:short-clock-strong-terminal-norm}.  Work pointwise at one
admissible parameter pair and let
\(\mathcal Y_\sigma(u)\) denote the full physical old-history/current
trajectory reconstructed from the fixed-envelope coordinates
\(u=(y,a,\tau)\).  Thus at \(\beta=0\) its exact RFDE residual, after the
normal/phase splitting, is \(\mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm fe}(u)\).
Write \(\mathfrak F_N\) for the full discrete-delay right-hand side of the
reconstructed fold-time system in
\cref{eq:fold-time-tracker-r,eq:fold-time-tracker-Z,%
eq:fold-time-tracker-p}.

For \(\beta\) in \cref{eq:short-clock-parameter-ball}, impose the exact
fixed-interval equation \cref{eq:terminal-clock-pulled-rfde} on the whole
interval \(0\le s\le S_\sigma\), using
\(Y=\mathcal Y_\sigma(u)\).  It agrees with the fixed-clock equation where
all relevant clock values are on the same plateau; its unsplit physical
row is
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr R_{\sigma,\rm RFDE}^{\rm clk}(Y,\beta)(s)
 =\delta Y_s(s)-\{1+d_\beta\chi'(s)\}
  \mathfrak F_N\bigl(Y(s),Y(\sigma_{1,\beta}(s)),\ldots,
                         Y(\sigma_{m,\beta}(s))\bigr).
                                                        \label{eq:short-clock-unsplit-rfde-row}
\end{equation}
Apply the same exact collective/normal decomposition and hard source split
as in \cref{eq:fixed-envelope-exact-residual}.  Keep the raw-compatible old-history row, the
causal-entry row, the phase gauge, the attracting entry row, and the
repelling speed-aligned terminal row, with every coefficient in that row
evaluated at the current terminal state \(r_Y(S_\sigma)\).  Evaluate the
terminal history on the fixed plateau in
\cref{eq:terminal-clock-history-fixed}, but do not prescribe it as boundary
data.  For the square augmented operator, adjoin a free interface unknown
\(h\in\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1\) and the row
\(\mathcal T_Z(Y)-h\), exactly as in
\cref{cor:conditional-event-aligned-source-split-inverse}.  Denote the
resulting square residual by
\(\mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm clk,aug}(u,h,\beta)\).  Retain the triangular
fixed-envelope phase-slack row and
append
\begin{equation}
 E_\sigma(u)=
 \frac{r_{\mathcal Y_\sigma(u)}(S_\sigma)-e_\sigma}
      {q_0(e_\sigma)}.                           \label{eq:short-clock-nonlinear-event}
\end{equation}
Let \(\operatorname{pr}_{\ne\mathrm{int}}\) delete the interface row and
define the reduced exact fixed-domain residual by the unique identity-row
elimination
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm clk}(u,\beta)
 :=\operatorname{pr}_{\ne\mathrm{int}}
 \mathbb F_\sigma^{\rm clk,aug}
  \bigl(u,\mathcal T_Z(\mathcal Y_\sigma(u)),\beta\bigr).
                                                        \label{eq:short-clock-exact-residual}
\end{equation}
Thus the augmented and reduced zeros correspond bijectively: the deleted
row uniquely recovers the actual terminal history.  The proof retains the
augmented variable through every inverse and contraction estimate and
projects only afterward.
No equation is cut off in this definition: the cutoff changes only the
clock chart, and zeros are conjugate to zeros of the unmodified physical
RFDE by \(\Xi_\beta\).

There is a joint constant \(C_{\rm oe}>0\) such that, after the ordered
decrease of the original joint wedge, the residual in
\cref{eq:short-clock-exact-residual} has a unique zero
\begin{equation}
 (u_\sigma^{\rm oe},\beta_\sigma^{\rm oe})
 \quad\hbox{with}\quad
 \|u_\sigma^{\rm oe}-u_\sigma^{\rm fe}
       -\mathscr A_{\chi,\sigma}^{\rm fe}
          \beta_\sigma^{\rm oe}\|_
       {\mathcal X_{\sigma,\chi}^1}
 +\frac{|\beta_\sigma^{\rm oe}|}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C_{\rm oe}\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}.  \label{eq:short-clock-zero-bound}
\end{equation}
The zero is unique in the displayed clock-aligned strong
\(C_{\rm oe}\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}\)-ball (after enlarging
\(C_{\rm oe}\) once in the proof), and its differential is a dimension-
and diagonal-uniform isomorphism between the corresponding translated
clock-aligned spaces at that zero.  No uniform raw-product estimate and no
uniqueness outside this local clock-aligned ball are asserted.

Define
\begin{equation}
 S_\sigma^{\rm oe}
 =S_\sigma+\frac{\beta_\sigma^{\rm oe}}{\delta},
 \qquad
 X_\sigma^{\rm oe}(t)
 =\mathcal Y_\sigma(u_\sigma^{\rm oe})
       \bigl(\Xi_{\beta_\sigma^{\rm oe}}^{-1}(t)\bigr).
                                                        \label{eq:short-clock-physical-segment}
\end{equation}
Then \(X_\sigma^{\rm oe}\) is a classical forward segment of the
unmodified RFDE on
\([-\theta_m,S_\sigma^{\rm oe}]\), has exactly the same raw-compatible
old history and causal entry as its fixed-clock representative, and
satisfies
\begin{equation}
 r_\sigma^{\rm oe}(S_\sigma^{\rm oe})=e_{\sigma,1}.
                                                        \label{eq:short-clock-original-endpoint}
\end{equation}
Its scalar speed is strictly negative on the whole segment.  The terminal
history
\[
 \theta\longmapsto
 X_\sigma^{\rm oe}(S_\sigma^{\rm oe}+\theta),
 \qquad-\theta_m\le\theta\le0,
\]
is exactly the fixed plateau history of
\(\mathcal Y_\sigma(u_\sigma^{\rm oe})\).

On the repelling branch the nonlinear speed row gives
\begin{equation}
 Q_r^{\rm oe}(e_{r,1})=0,\qquad
 c_{r,\rm ter}^{\rm oe}=1,\qquad
 K_{p,r}^{\rm oe}=R_{q,N}(e_{r,1}),\qquad
 \kappa_{p,\delta}^{\rm oe}=o(1).               \label{eq:short-clock-endpoint-speed-identities}
\end{equation}
On both branches the actual terminal normal history satisfies
\begin{align}
 \|\partial_s Z_\sigma^{\rm oe}\|_{C^0}
 &\le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta},&
 \|\partial_s^2 Z_\sigma^{\rm oe}\|_{C^0}
 &\le C\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta},          \label{eq:short-clock-endpoint-slow-jet}\\
 \|K_{Z,0}^{\rm oe}\|_
  {\mathcal L(\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta},\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1)}
 &\le C.                                         \label{eq:short-clock-actual-history-shear}
\end{align}
The derivative norms are taken on the fixed pulled-back terminal window;
by the terminal plateau this is the actual physical terminal-history
window.

This theorem closes a fixed-parameter, raw-compatible,
original-coordinate-endpoint RFDE segment and its actual moving-duration
speed/history columns.  Its raw old history is still the canonical local
assembler.  It does not construct a past-complete attracting orbit, a
repelling invariant history sheet, a branch-to-branch complete-history
connection, a parameter/gauge family, or a physical canard root.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Raw-gauge family of fixed-section outer zeros]
\label[theorem]{thm:raw-gauge-fixed-section-zero}
Retain
\cref{lem:second-jet-raw-history-assembler,%
thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual,%
cor:fixed-section-canonical-zero}
at order \(h\ge3\).  At a fixed admissible parameter pair define the
attracting and repelling raw-gauge spaces
\begin{align}
 \mathfrak B_{a,\delta}^h
 &=E_N\times\R\times\mathscr K_{N,\delta}^h,
 &g_a&=(\beta_a,b_a,\widehat\gamma_a),\notag\\
 \mathfrak B_{r,\delta}^h
 &=E_N\times\mathscr K_{N,\delta}^h\times\R,
 &g_r&=(\beta_r,\widehat\gamma_r,b_r),           \label{eq:raw-gauge-spaces}
\end{align}
with normalized norms
\begin{align}
 \|g_a\|_{\mathfrak B_{a,\delta}^h}
 &=\varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
    \|\beta_a\|_N+\frac{|b_a|}{r_{\rm out}}
       +\|\widehat\gamma_a\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,r_{\rm out}}
                       \right\},\notag\\
 \|g_r\|_{\mathfrak B_{r,\delta}^h}
 &=\varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
    \|\beta_r\|_N
       +\|\widehat\gamma_r\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
       +\frac{|b_r|}{r_{\rm out}}\right\}.      \label{eq:raw-gauge-norms}
\end{align}
Thus a bounded gauge has physical boundary amplitude \(O(\varepsilon)\)
in the displayed natural boundary norms.
The core histories \(\widehat\gamma_\sigma\) have zero endpoint jets
through order \(h\); they are genuine raw-history data, not extra boundary
rows.

For \(u=(x,a)\), replace the canonical history packages in
\cref{eq:canonical-nonlinear-history-packages} by
\begin{align}
 \mathscr H_{a,g_a}^{\rm raw}(u)
 &=\mathscr H_{r_{\rm out}}^{\rm rc}
      \bigl(c_a(a),(\beta_a,b_a,\widehat\gamma_a)\bigr),
                                                        \notag\\
 \mathscr H_{r,g_r}^{\rm raw}(u)
 &=\mathscr H_{-\rho_\delta}^{\rm rc}
      \bigl(c_r(a),(\beta_r,p_x(0),\widehat\gamma_r)\bigr),
                                                        \label{eq:raw-gauge-history-packages}
\end{align}
and put
\begin{align}
 B_{\gamma,a}^{g_a}(u)
 &=\mathscr H_{a,g_a}^{\rm raw}(u),\notag\\
 B_{\gamma,r}^{g_r}(u)
 &=\mathscr H_{r,g_r}^{\rm raw}(u)-\mathfrak C_ep_x(0),
                                                        \label{eq:raw-gauge-history-rows}\\
 \mathscr S_{\sigma,g_\sigma}^{\rm nl}(u)
 &=\bigl(B_{\gamma,\sigma}^{g_\sigma}(u),b_\sigma,
          S_{Z,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(u),
          S_{p,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(u)\bigr).       \label{eq:raw-gauge-normal-package}
\end{align}
Here \(b_a\) is the attracting resource-defect entry row and \(b_r\) is
the repelling future resource-defect row.  The repelling value \(p_x(0)\)
remains an output and enters the raw compatibility recursion exactly once.
Define
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_{\sigma}^{g_\sigma}(u)
 =\left(
   \mathscr N_\sigma x-
       \mathscr S_{\sigma,g_\sigma}^{\rm nl}(u),
   \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^{\rm nl}(a)
       -\bigl(Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}(x,a),0\bigr)
       \right).                                  \label{eq:raw-gauge-residual}
\end{equation}
At zero gauge this is exactly \(\mathcal F_\sigma\).

Let \(L_\sigma=D\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)\), and define the bounded gauge
injections into the residual data space by
\begin{align}
 \mathscr J_a g_a
 &=\left(
  D_U\mathscr H_{r_{\rm out}}^{\rm rc}(0,0)
       (\beta_a,b_a,\widehat\gamma_a),
       b_a,0,0;\,0,0\right),\notag\\
 \mathscr J_r g_r
 &=\left(
  D_U\mathscr H_{-\rho_\delta}^{\rm rc}(0,0)
       (\beta_r,0,\widehat\gamma_r),
       b_r,0,0;\,0,0\right).                     \label{eq:raw-gauge-linear-injections}
\end{align}
The semicolon separates the normal and phase data.  There are uniform
constants \(g_*,\lambda_g,C_g>0\) and
\(0<R_{\rm G}\le R_{\rm u}\) such that, whenever
\begin{equation}
 \lambda_\delta+\|g_\sigma\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}
 <\lambda_g,
 \qquad
 \|g_\sigma\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}<g_*,          \label{eq:raw-gauge-smallness}
\end{equation}
the residual \(\mathcal F_\sigma^{g_\sigma}\) has a unique zero
\begin{equation}
 u_{\sigma,\delta}^{g_\sigma}
 \in\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}
 \quad\hbox{in the open \(R_{\rm G}\)-ball}.     \label{eq:raw-gauge-zero}
\end{equation}
It satisfies
\begin{align}
 \|u_{\sigma,\delta}^{g_\sigma}\|_
       {\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}}
 &\le C_g\left(\lambda_\delta
       +\|g_\sigma\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}\right),
                                                        \label{eq:raw-gauge-zero-size}\\
 \left\|u_{\sigma,\delta}^{g_\sigma}
     +L_\sigma^{-1}\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)
     -L_\sigma^{-1}\mathscr J_\sigma g_\sigma\right\|
 &\le C_g\left(\lambda_\delta
       +\|g_\sigma\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}\right)^2,
                                                        \label{eq:raw-gauge-first-jet}\\
 \bigl\|[D_u\mathcal F_\sigma^{g_\sigma}
       (u_{\sigma,\delta}^{g_\sigma})]^{-1}\bigr\|
 &\le2M.                                           \label{eq:raw-gauge-zero-inverse}
\end{align}
On a smaller common gauge ball the zero is \(C^1\) in \(g_\sigma\), with
\begin{equation}
 D_g u_{\sigma,\delta}^{g}
 =-[D_u\mathcal F_\sigma^g(u_{\sigma,\delta}^g)]^{-1}
       D_g\mathcal F_\sigma^g(u_{\sigma,\delta}^g),
 \qquad
 \|D_g u_{\sigma,\delta}^g\|\le C_g,           \label{eq:raw-gauge-zero-derivative}
\end{equation}
and hence
\begin{equation}
 \|u_{\sigma,\delta}^{g_1}-u_{\sigma,\delta}^{g_2}\|
 \le C_g\|g_1-g_2\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.       \label{eq:raw-gauge-zero-lipschitz}
\end{equation}
The zero at \(g_\sigma=0\) is the canonical zero in
\cref{cor:fixed-section-canonical-zero}.

At a gauge zero, the exact boundary identities are
\begin{align}
 \gamma_x&=\mathscr H_{a,g_a}^{\rm raw}(u),&
 Z_x(0)&=\beta_a,&p_x(0)&=b_a &&(\sigma=a),\notag\\
 \gamma_x&=\mathscr H_{r,g_r}^{\rm raw}(u),&
 Z_x(0)&=\beta_r,&p_x(S_r)&=b_r &&(\sigma=r).     \label{eq:raw-gauge-exact-boundary-identities}
\end{align}
They therefore reconstruct exact monotone forward solution segments of the
unmodified RFDE, with the strict speed bound in
\cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-speed-sign}, uniformly on the gauge ball.
This proves a nonempty, infinite-dimensional gauge-indexed class of
raw-compatible finite-branch representatives.  It does not prove that a gauge history is
cut from a past orbit, compare two gauges after a physical endpoint recut,
construct a parameterized moving-event family, or produce a stable sheet,
connection, or root.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Generated-interior raw-gauge tracker representatives]
\label[corollary]{cor:generated-interior-canonical-tracker}
Retain \cref{thm:raw-gauge-fixed-section-zero}.  At any one of its
gauge-indexed zeros,
write its phase component as \(a_{\sigma,\delta}\), and
write the reconstructed forward solution as
\[
 v_\sigma(s),\quad -\theta_m\le s\le S_\sigma,
 \qquad
 w_\sigma(s),\quad 0\le s\le S_\sigma,
\]
in fold time, and put
\[
 r_\sigma(s)=\pi_N^T\{v_\sigma(s)-\mathbf1\}
 =\Theta_{a_{\sigma,\delta}}(\bar r_\sigma(s)).
\]
After decreasing \(\delta_0(r_{\rm out})\) once more,
\(S_\sigma>2\theta_m\).  Choose
\begin{equation}
 2\theta_m<s_0<s_1\le S_\sigma,\qquad
 J_\sigma^{\rm gen}=r_\sigma([s_0-\theta_m,s_1]),
 \qquad I_\sigma^{\rm gen}=r_\sigma([s_0,s_1]).   \label{eq:generated-interior-intervals}
\end{equation}
Then \(r_\sigma\) is a \(C^1\) diffeomorphism from
\([s_0-\theta_m,s_1]\) onto \(J_\sigma^{\rm gen}\).  If
\(s_\sigma=(r_\sigma|_{[s_0-\theta_m,s_1]})^{-1}\), define
\begin{equation}
 V_\sigma(r)=v_\sigma(s_\sigma(r)),\qquad
 W_\sigma(r)=w_\sigma(s_\sigma(r)),\qquad
 q_\sigma(r)=\delta^{-1}r_\sigma'(s_\sigma(r)).   \label{eq:generated-interior-tracker}
\end{equation}
There are uniform constants \(c_q,C_q,c_w,C_w,C_V>0\) such that
\begin{align}
 -C_q\le q_\sigma(r)&\le-c_q<0,
       &&r\in J_\sigma^{\rm gen},                 \label{eq:generated-interior-q-bounds}\\
 c_w|r|\le|W_\sigma'(r)|&\le C_w|r|,
       &&r\in J_\sigma^{\rm gen},                \label{eq:generated-interior-wprime-bounds}\\
 \|V_\sigma'(r)\|_N&\le C_V,
       &&r\in J_\sigma^{\rm gen}.                \label{eq:generated-interior-Vprime-bound}
\end{align}
For every \(r\in I_\sigma^{\rm gen}\) and every delay atom,
\begin{equation}
 r_{\sigma,k}(r)
 :=r_\sigma(s_\sigma(r)-\theta_k)
 =\Phi_{q_\sigma}^{-\delta\theta_k}(r)
 \in J_\sigma^{\rm gen}.                         \label{eq:generated-interior-backtracks}
\end{equation}
The triple \((V_\sigma,W_\sigma,q_\sigma)\) exactly satisfies
\cref{eq:exact-outer-voltage-invariance,eq:exact-outer-resource-invariance}
on \(I_\sigma^{\rm gen}\), with the backtracks in
\cref{eq:generated-interior-backtracks}.  Its complete-history embedding is
not a formal collar: it is exactly the generated forward window
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal H_{V_\sigma,W_\sigma,q_\sigma}(r)
 =\left(
   \theta\longmapsto v_\sigma(s_\sigma(r)+\theta),\;
   w_\sigma(s_\sigma(r))
   \right).                                      \label{eq:generated-interior-history-window}
\end{equation}
Consequently the stationary-coordinate quotient in
\cref{prop:physical-history-phase-quotient} and the exact resource-gauge
normal equation in \cref{prop:exact-resource-gauge-quotient} apply on this
generated interior.  In particular, the latter has its exact Volterra
realization and
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal K_s\|\le \frac{C}{S_\delta},\qquad
 \|(\Id-\mathcal K_s)^{-1}\|\le2.                \label{eq:generated-interior-volterra-bound}
\end{equation}

This corollary constructs an exact generated-interior tracker
representative carried by every zero in the raw-gauge ball.  The preceding
theorem proves Lipschitz dependence of the fixed-section zeros on the gauge;
this corollary makes no common-chart comparison of the reparameterized
trackers.  It proves existence, not flat forgetting or raw-gauge independence.
The result does not prescribe both physical branch endpoints,
construct a moving-event or parameter family, extend the initial collar to
a past-complete orbit, produce a repelling stable sheet, or prove a physical
connection or root.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[Common generated buffer and fixed physical-section hit]
\label[proposition]{prop:raw-gauge-physical-hit}
Retain
\cref{thm:raw-gauge-fixed-section-zero,%
cor:generated-interior-canonical-tracker}
at order \(h\ge3\), pointwise at one fixed admissible parameter pair.  Put
\begin{equation}
 r_h=\frac12r_{\rm out},\qquad
 e_{a,h}=r_h,\qquad e_{r,h}=-r_h,                 \label{eq:physical-hit-sections}
\end{equation}
and fix \(0<\kappa_h<1/8\).  After decreasing \(\delta_0\), assume
\(\rho_\delta<r_{\rm out}/4\).  Define the reduced bracket times by
\begin{equation}
 \bar r_\sigma(T_{\sigma,-})=e_{\sigma,h}+\kappa_hr_h,
 \qquad
 \bar r_\sigma(T_{\sigma,+})=e_{\sigma,h}-\kappa_hr_h.
                                                        \label{eq:physical-hit-reference-bracket}
\end{equation}
Then \(T_{\sigma,-}<T_{\sigma,+}\).  After one further decrease of
\(\delta_0\),
\begin{equation}
 3\theta_m<T_{\sigma,-}<T_{\sigma,+}<S_\sigma,
 \qquad
 [T_{\sigma,-}-\theta_m,T_{\sigma,+}]
 \quad\hbox{is generated interior}.              \label{eq:physical-hit-common-buffer}
\end{equation}

There are uniform \(g_h,\lambda_h,C_h>0\) such that, whenever
\begin{equation}
 \lambda_\delta<\lambda_h,
 \qquad
 \|g_\sigma\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}<g_h,          \label{eq:physical-hit-gauge-ball}
\end{equation}
the raw-gauge zero gives the complete physical history
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)
 =\left(\theta\longmapsto v_{\sigma,g}(s+\theta),
                         w_{\sigma,g}(s)\right)\in\mathscr Y_N,
 \qquad s\in[T_{\sigma,-},T_{\sigma,+}].          \label{eq:physical-hit-history-family}
\end{equation}
The map \((s,g)\mapsto\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)\) is jointly \(C^1\)
into \(\mathscr Y_N\), and
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s,g}\left\{
  \|D_g\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)\|+
  \|\tau_{\sigma,g}(s)\|_{\mathscr Y_N}\right\}\le C_h, \label{eq:physical-hit-uniform-history-jets}
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
 \tau_{\sigma,g}(s)
 =\frac1{r_{\sigma,g}'(s)}
   \left(\theta\longmapsto v_{\sigma,g}'(s+\theta),
                         w_{\sigma,g}'(s)\right),
 \qquad \varrho_N\tau_{\sigma,g}(s)=1.            \label{eq:physical-hit-normalized-tangent}
\end{equation}
The first norm in \cref{eq:physical-hit-uniform-history-jets} is the
operator norm from the normalized gauge space to the ordinary product norm
on \(\mathscr Y_N\).  Thus this is a dimension-uniform weak complete-history
buffer estimate, not a \(C^2\) strong-buffer estimate.

For every gauge in \cref{eq:physical-hit-gauge-ball} there is a unique
selected hit
\begin{equation}
 S_{\sigma,h}(g)\in(T_{\sigma,-},T_{\sigma,+}),
 \qquad r_{\sigma,g}(S_{\sigma,h}(g))=e_{\sigma,h}.           \label{eq:physical-hit-time}
\end{equation}
It is the only hit of this section on the monotone forward branch segment.
The event time, its natural scaled displacement, and the complete-history
hit
\begin{align}
 \widehat\tau_{\sigma,h}(g)
 &=\delta\{S_{\sigma,h}(g)-S_{\sigma,h}(0)\},\notag\\
 \mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)
 &=\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(S_{\sigma,h}(g))
   \in\Sigma_{N,e_{\sigma,h}}.                    \label{eq:physical-hit-map}
\end{align}
are \(C^1\) in \(g\).  Here
\begin{equation}
 \Sigma_{N,e}=\{Y\in\mathscr Y_N:\varrho_NY=1+e\}.          \label{eq:physical-hit-section}
\end{equation}
For a gauge direction \(k\), all fixed-time
derivatives below are evaluated at \(s=S_{\sigma,h}(g)\), and
\begin{align}
 DS_{\sigma,h}(g)k
 &=-\frac{\varrho_ND_g\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)k}
          {r_{\sigma,g}'(s)},\notag\\
 D\widehat\tau_{\sigma,h}(g)k
 &=-\frac{\varrho_ND_g\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)k}
          {q_{\sigma,g}(e_{\sigma,h})},           \label{eq:physical-hit-time-derivative}\\
 D\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)k
 &=\bigl(\Id-\tau_{\sigma,g}(s)\varrho_N\bigr)
       D_g\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)k.              \label{eq:physical-hit-history-derivative}
\end{align}
Consequently
\begin{equation}
 \|D\widehat\tau_{\sigma,h}(g)\|
 +\|D\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)\|\le C_h,          \label{eq:physical-hit-map-uniform-bound}
\end{equation}
whereas the unscaled derivative \(DS_{\sigma,h}\) may lose the sharp
factor \(\delta^{-1}\).  The projection in
\cref{eq:physical-hit-history-derivative} is exactly the
stationary-coordinate phase quotient in
\cref{eq:physical-quotient-process} at the hit.

This proposition constructs a common weak generated buffer and a fixed
interior physical-section hit map for the fixed-parameter gauge family.  It
does not construct the \(C^2\) strong terminal buffer required by
\cref{lem:terminal-relative-trace-scale-chart}, recut either outer branch
endpoint, prove gauge flatness, produce parameter jets or a repelling sheet,
or construct a physical connection or root.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Common strong generated buffer at the physical hit]
\label[proposition]{prop:raw-gauge-strong-hit-buffer}
Retain \cref{prop:raw-gauge-physical-hit} at order \(h\ge3\), still
pointwise at one fixed admissible parameter pair, and put
\begin{equation}
 B_{\sigma,h}
 =[T_{\sigma,-}-\theta_m,T_{\sigma,+}],\qquad
 Y_{\sigma,g}=(r_{\sigma,g},Z_{\sigma,g},p_{\sigma,g}).
                                                        \label{eq:strong-hit-fixed-buffer}
\end{equation}
For \(Y=(r,Z,p)\in C^3(B_{\sigma,h})\), define
\begin{equation}
 \|Y\|_{\mathfrak S_{\sigma,h}^3}
 =\max_{0\le j\le3}\delta^j
   \sup_{s\in B_{\sigma,h}}
   \left\{
    \frac{|\partial_s^jr(s)|}{r_h}
   +\frac{\|\partial_s^jZ(s)\|_N}{\varepsilon}
   +\frac{|\partial_s^jp(s)|}{\varepsilon r_h}
   \right\}.                                    \label{eq:strong-hit-buffer-norm}
\end{equation}
After decreasing the common gauge ball and
\(\delta_0(r_{\rm out})\), there are uniform constants
\(c_{\rm buf},C_{\rm buf}>0\) such that
\begin{equation}
 \min\{S_{\sigma,h}(g)-(T_{\sigma,-}-\theta_m),
          T_{\sigma,+}-S_{\sigma,h}(g)\}
 \ge \frac{c_{\rm buf}}{\delta}                 \label{eq:strong-hit-time-margin}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}
 g\longmapsto Y_{\sigma,g}|_{B_{\sigma,h}}
 \quad\hbox{is \(C^1\) from \(\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h\)
 into \(\mathfrak S_{\sigma,h}^3\)},             \label{eq:strong-hit-C1-map}
\end{equation}
with
\begin{equation}
 \sup_g\left\{
  \|Y_{\sigma,g}\|_{\mathfrak S_{\sigma,h}^3}
  +\|D_gY_{\sigma,g}\|_{\mathcal L(
       \mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h,
       \mathfrak S_{\sigma,h}^3)}
       \right\}\le C_{\rm buf}.                \label{eq:strong-hit-C1-bound}
\end{equation}
The constants are independent of \(N\), \(\delta\), the branch, and the
fixed parameter point in the retained structural ball.

More explicitly, let \(L_\delta,\ell_\delta\ge0\) satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \delta\{L_\delta+\theta_m+\ell_\delta\}
 \le\frac12c_{\rm buf},                         \label{eq:strong-hit-window-budget}
\end{equation}
and set
\begin{equation}
 K_\delta=[-L_\delta-\theta_m,0],\qquad
 K_\delta^{\ell}
 =[-L_\delta-\theta_m-\ell_\delta,\ell_\delta].
                                                        \label{eq:strong-hit-moving-windows}
\end{equation}
If \(S_{\sigma,h}^0=S_{\sigma,h}(0)\), then the fixed-reference buffer
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal Y_{\sigma,h}(g)(\varsigma)
 =Y_{\sigma,g}(S_{\sigma,h}^0+\varsigma),\qquad
 \varsigma\in K_\delta^{\ell},                 \label{eq:strong-hit-reference-buffer}
\end{equation}
is \(C^1\) in \(g\) into the scaled \(C^3\) space.  If, in addition,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_g|S_{\sigma,h}(g)-S_{\sigma,h}^0|
 <\frac12\ell_\delta,                            \label{eq:strong-hit-chart-shift-budget}
\end{equation}
then every translated window sampled by
\(\widehat\tau_{\sigma,h}(g)\) stays in the same generated buffer.
Consequently \cref{lem:terminal-relative-trace-scale-chart}, with
\(d=1\), applies to
\((\mathcal Y_{\sigma,h}(g),\widehat\tau_{\sigma,h}(g))\).
It produces a \(C^1\) family of event-aligned \(C^1\) retained traces in
the fixed physical section.  For a product trace \(X=(x,z,p)\) on
\(K_\delta\), write
\begin{equation*}
 \|X\|_{C^1_{\delta,\mathrm{ord}}(K_\delta)}
 =\max_{0\le j\le1}\delta^j
   \sup_{\varsigma\in K_\delta}
       \bigl\{|\partial_\varsigma^jx|
              +\|\partial_\varsigma^jz\|_N
              +|\partial_\varsigma^jp|\bigr\}.
\end{equation*}
Thus, in this ordinary unnormalized product space, its first gauge
differential has the safe operator bound
\begin{equation}
 \|D_g(r^\#,N_Z^\#,N_p^\#)\|_{\mathcal L(
       \mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h,
       C^1_{\delta,\mathrm{ord}}(K_\delta))}
 \le C_{\rm buf}\delta^{-2}.                    \label{eq:strong-hit-recut-loss}
\end{equation}
This is a supplied strong input for the trace chart, not a claim that raw
translation is uniformly bounded on one same strong space.

The two window conditions are nonempty on one fixed gauge ball: after
choosing \(L_\delta=O(S_\delta)\), one may take
\(\ell_\delta=c_{\rm buf}/(4\delta)\) and decrease the gauge radius so
that \cref{eq:strong-hit-chart-shift-budget} holds.

The proposition concerns time regularity and first gauge dependence.  It
does not make the gauge map \(C^2\), estimate an
\(\varepsilon\)-normalized terminal phase-to-normal Schur column, prove
gauge forgetting, construct a hybrid source inverse, take parameter
derivatives, or produce a repelling sheet, physical connection, or root.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Repelling component-kernel finite-generation flushing]
\label[proposition]{prop:repelling-component-flushing}
Retain \cref{lem:repelling-component-generations}.  Fix \(d\ge1\), take
\(h\ge\max\{3,d\}\), and choose \(M=M_d\) so that
\begin{equation}
 M(1-2\vartheta)>d.                                \label{eq:repelling-flush-generation-count}
\end{equation}
There is \(A_d^*>0\) such that, for fixed \(A_d>A_d^*\),
\begin{equation}
 L_0=A_dS_\delta,\qquad
 L_b=(M+1)\theta_m+L_0+1,                         \label{eq:repelling-flush-buffer-length}
\end{equation}
and sufficiently small \(\delta\), one has
\(2L_b+\theta_m<S_r\).  Set
\begin{align}
 J_{r,\rm in}
 &=\bar r_r([L_b-\theta_m,L_b]),\notag\\
 J_{r,\rm u}
 &=\bar r_r([S_r-L_b-\theta_m,S_r-L_b]).          \label{eq:repelling-flush-windows}
\end{align}

Split the solution linearly as
\((Z,p,Q)=(Z_{\rm in},p_{\rm in},Q_{\rm in})
 +(Z_{\rm u},p_{\rm u},Q_{\rm u})\), where the inner component has data
\((\gamma,b_u)=(\gamma,0)\) and the terminal component has data
\((\gamma,b_u)=(0,b_u)\).  On the two windows use
\(r=\bar r_r(s)\).  Then
\begin{align}
 &\|Z_{\rm in}\|_{\mathsf Z_\varepsilon^d(J_{r,\rm in})}
 +\|p_{\rm in}\|_{\mathsf P_\varepsilon^d(J_{r,\rm in})}
 +\|Q_{\rm in}\|_{\mathsf Q_\varepsilon^{d-1}(J_{r,\rm in})}
 \notag\\
 &\quad\le C_{d,A_d}\mathcal E_{r,\rm in}(\delta)
             \varepsilon^{-1}
             \|\gamma\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta},      \label{eq:repelling-inner-flush}\\
 &\|Z_{\rm u}\|_{\mathsf Z_\varepsilon^d(J_{r,\rm u})}
 +\|p_{\rm u}\|_{\mathsf P_\varepsilon^d(J_{r,\rm u})}
 +\|Q_{\rm u}\|_{\mathsf Q_\varepsilon^{d-1}(J_{r,\rm u})}
 \notag\\
 &\quad\le C_{d,A_d}\mathcal E_{r,\rm u}(\delta)
             \varepsilon^{-1}\frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}},      \label{eq:repelling-terminal-flush}
\end{align}
where
\begin{align}
 \mathcal E_{r,\rm in}(\delta)
 &=\left(\frac{S_\delta}{\delta}\right)^d
       \left\{e^{-cL_0/\delta}+e^{-cS_\delta L_0}\right\}
   +\delta^{M(1-2\vartheta)-d}S_\delta^{d-M},\notag\\
 \mathcal E_{r,\rm u}(\delta)
 &=\delta^{-2d}e^{-cL_0/\delta}
   +\delta^{M(2-2\vartheta)-2d}.                  \label{eq:repelling-flush-errors}
\end{align}
The number \(A_d^*\) is chosen so that the second inner action term tends to
zero; this is possible because \(S_\delta^2=2p\log(1/\delta)\).  Hence both
errors tend to zero.  These are normalized component trace estimates, not
support-vanishing statements.

The result is fixed-parameter and fixed-phase.  It uses no backward RFDE
evolution and does not include the compatible \(p(0)\)-to-collar feedback.
It proves neither the coupled phase--normal inverse, a nonlinear tracker,
the speed sign, a repelling invariant history sheet, nor a physical root.
\end{proposition}

The omitted feedback is not another infinite-dimensional boundary
condition.  It is a scalar feedback through the already constructed
compatible collar chart.  The next result closes that feedback in the
strong history class and records the extra cross-column estimate needed at
the terminal handoff.

\begin{proposition}[High-order compatible repelling-collar closure]
\label[proposition]{prop:compatible-repelling-collar-closure}
Retain \cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer,%
prop:compatible-endpoint-jet-collar,%
prop:repelling-component-flushing}.  Fix \(d\ge1\), take
\(h\ge\max\{3,d\}\), and now choose the generation number so that
\begin{equation}
 M(1-2\vartheta)>2d.                              \label{eq:compatible-repelling-generation-count}
\end{equation}
Use the same \(L_0,L_b,J_{r,\rm in},J_{r,\rm u}\) as in
\cref{eq:repelling-flush-buffer-length,eq:repelling-flush-windows}.  Let
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak C_e:\R\longrightarrow
 C^h([ -\theta_m,0];\R^N)                         \label{eq:strong-compatible-feedback-map}
\end{equation}
be a bounded linear map satisfying, uniformly on the retained chart ball,
\begin{align}
 (\mathfrak C_ec)(0)&=0,\notag\\
 \|\mathfrak C_ec\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
 &\le C_\Gamma\frac{|c|}{\rho_\delta}.           \label{eq:strong-compatible-feedback-bound}
\end{align}
For every
\(\gamma_0\in C^h([ -\theta_m,0];\R^N)\) with
\(\gamma_0(0)\in E_N\), and every \(b_u\in\R\), the mixed problem
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} with \(F=H=0\),
\begin{equation}
 Z(0)=\gamma(0),\qquad p(S_r)=b_u,\qquad
 \gamma=\gamma_0+\mathfrak C_ep(0),               \label{eq:strong-compatible-feedback-problem}
\end{equation}
has a unique solution with \((Z,p)\in\mathfrak S_{r,d}\).  It splits
linearly as
\begin{equation}
 (Z,p,Q)=(Z_{\rm c},p_{\rm c},Q_{\rm c})
          +(Z_{\rm u},p_{\rm u},Q_{\rm u}),       \label{eq:compatible-repelling-column-split}
\end{equation}
where the first closed-loop column has data \((\gamma_0,b_u)=(\gamma_0,0)\)
and the second has data \((0,b_u)\).  Their actual old histories are
\begin{equation*}
 \gamma_{\rm c}=\gamma_0+\mathfrak C_ep_{\rm c}(0),
 \qquad
 \gamma_{\rm u}=\mathfrak C_ep_{\rm u}(0),
\end{equation*}
and obey
\begin{align}
 \|\gamma_{\rm c}\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
