 &\le C\|\gamma_0\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta},\notag\\
 \|\gamma_{\rm u}\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
 &\le C\frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}}.                 \label{eq:compatible-repelling-history-columns}
\end{align}

The collar column retains the inner handoff estimate
\begin{align}
 &\|Z_{\rm c}\|_{\mathsf Z_\varepsilon^d(J_{r,\rm in})}
 +\|p_{\rm c}\|_{\mathsf P_\varepsilon^d(J_{r,\rm in})}
 +\|Q_{\rm c}\|_{\mathsf Q_\varepsilon^{d-1}(J_{r,\rm in})}\notag\\
 &\qquad\le C_{d,A_d}\mathcal E_{r,\rm in}(\delta)
       \varepsilon^{-1}
       \|\gamma_0\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}.          \label{eq:compatible-repelling-inner-column}
\end{align}
For the terminal column put
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal E_{r,\rm cross}(\delta)
 =\delta^{-2d}e^{-cL_0/\delta}
   +\delta^{M(1-2\vartheta)-2d}S_\delta^{-M}.      \label{eq:compatible-repelling-cross-error}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{align}
 &\|Z_{\rm u}\|_{\mathsf Z_\varepsilon^d(J_{r,\rm u})}
 +\|p_{\rm u}\|_{\mathsf P_\varepsilon^d(J_{r,\rm u})}
 +\|Q_{\rm u}\|_{\mathsf Q_\varepsilon^{d-1}(J_{r,\rm u})}\notag\\
 &\qquad\le C_{d,A_d}
   \{\mathcal E_{r,\rm u}(\delta)
                  +\mathcal E_{r,\rm cross}(\delta)\}
       \varepsilon^{-1}\frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}}.            \label{eq:compatible-repelling-terminal-column}
\end{align}
Both error factors on the last line tend to zero, uniformly in \(N\).

At every base point of the compatible collar chart,
\(\mathfrak C_e=D_{p_e}(\operatorname{pr}_\phi\Gamma_e^h)\), with
\(Z_e,\widehat\gamma,\nu,\eta\) fixed, satisfies
\cref{eq:strong-compatible-feedback-bound}.  Thus the proposition applies
to the actual linearized raw-compatibility feedback, not merely to an
abstract collar map.  It remains a fixed-parameter, fixed-phase result: the
coupled phase--normal border, nonlinear tracker, speed sign, and physical
root are not asserted.
\end{proposition}

There is nevertheless an intrinsic quotient once an actual physical history
has been constructed.  The point is to quotient by the transported tangent
line, rather than by an eigenvector of a frozen voltage--resource matrix; a
fixed complement is only a coordinate gauge for that quotient.

\begin{proposition}[Stationary-coordinate quotient along a physical history]
\label[proposition]{prop:physical-history-phase-quotient}
Fix \(N,\delta,\nu,\eta\), and in the raw equation replace
\(B_{k,N}(\zeta)\) by the structural-ball layer \(L_{k,N}(\eta)\).
Let \((\bar v(t),\bar w(t))\) be a classical solution of this physical
equation and rescale it to fold time by
\[
 v_*(s)=\bar v(s/\delta),\qquad w_*(s)=\bar w(s/\delta).
\]
Thus the fold-time delays are \(\theta_k\).  Let
\(Z_*(s)=(v_{*,s},w_*(s))\in\mathscr Y_N\) be compatible and \(C^2\) on an
interval enlarged by one delay, and let
\[
 r_*(s)=\pi_N^T\{v_*(s)-\mathbf1\}.
\]
Suppose \(r_*'(s)\ne0\) throughout that interval, and denote the variational
solution process of the rescaled RFDE along \(Z_*\) by
\(\mathcal U_*(s,u)\).  Then
\begin{equation}
 \tau_*(s)=\frac1{r_*'(s)}
 \left(\theta\longmapsto v_*'(s+\theta),\;w_*'(s)\right)
 \in\mathscr Y_N                                      \label{eq:physical-normalized-tangent}
\end{equation}
is the unique vector in the time-tangent line satisfying
\(\varrho_N\tau_*(s)=1\).  Its line is transported exactly:
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal U_*(s,u)\tau_*(u)
 =\frac{r_*'(s)}{r_*'(u)}\tau_*(s).                  \label{eq:physical-tangent-transport}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\begin{equation}
 Q_*(s)=\Id-\tau_*(s)\varrho_N,
 \qquad
 \widehat{\mathcal U}_*(s,u)
 =Q_*(s)\mathcal U_*(s,u)|_{\ker\varrho_N}          \label{eq:physical-quotient-process}
\end{equation}
defines an exact linear process on the fixed stationary-coordinate gauge
section
\(\ker\varrho_N\): for \(u\le t\le s\),
\begin{equation}
 \widehat{\mathcal U}_*(s,t)
 \widehat{\mathcal U}_*(t,u)
 =\widehat{\mathcal U}_*(s,u).                      \label{eq:physical-quotient-cocycle}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, the intrinsic object is the variational process induced on
\(\mathscr Y_N/\operatorname{span}\{\tau_*(s)\}\), written in the physical
stationary-coordinate gauge.  Another bounded transversal gives a conjugate
representation of the same quotient process provided it is uniformly
transverse to the tangent line and its projections, quotient
identifications, and inverse identifications have common bounds.  In every
history norm for which
\(\varrho_N\) is bounded,
\begin{equation}
 \|Q_*(s)\|\le1+\|\tau_*(s)\|\,\|\varrho_N\|.       \label{eq:physical-quotient-bound}
\end{equation}
The construction is unchanged if the time tangent is multiplied by a
nonzero scalar before imposing \(\varrho_N\tau=1\); no eigenvector or
spectral projection of a frozen full system enters it.
\end{proposition}

The row \(\varrho_N\) need not be dual-covariant, and
\(\ker\varrho_N\) need not be invariant; the projection at every step in
\cref{eq:physical-quotient-process} is essential.  A dual-covariant phase
functional annihilating the stable and strong-unstable bundles could be
defined only after the missing normal dichotomy has been proved.  Thus the
proposition constructs neither such a phase functional nor an invariant
normal bundle.

The monotonicity in \cref{eq:outer-speed-sign} is therefore the correct
leading input for phase removal, but it is not yet the hypothesis of
\cref{prop:physical-history-phase-quotient}: one must first construct a true
positive-\(\delta\) history with \(r_*'\ne0\), and then prove dimension-uniform
bounds and the required parameter jets for
\cref{eq:physical-normalized-tangent,eq:physical-quotient-process}.  In
particular, the fixed row in
\cref{prop:backward-asymptotic-nonselection} can normalize the tangent of
each already selected member, but the row alone selects neither that physical
history nor its transported tangent line.  The present proposition is not a
global anchor or history-selection theorem.

\begin{proposition}[Exact resource-gauge normal equation]
\label[proposition]{prop:exact-resource-gauge-quotient}
Retain \cref{prop:exact-outer-history-equation} for the shared-resource law,
and let \(r_s\) be a generated orbit in fold time:
\[
 r_s'=\delta q(r_s),\qquad
 Z_*(s)=\mathcal H_{V,w,q}(r_s).
\]
Assume \(w'(r_s)\ne0\) on a delay-enlarged orbit interval, and denote the
variational process along \(Z_*\) by \(\mathcal U_*(s,\sigma)\).  The
automatic \(C^2\) regularity in
\cref{prop:exact-outer-history-equation} applies.  Write
\begin{align}
 r_{s+\theta}&=\Phi_q^{\delta\theta}(r_s),&
 \chi_s(\theta)&=\frac{q(r_{s+\theta})}{q(r_s)},\notag\\
 e_s(\theta)&=\chi_s(\theta)V'(r_{s+\theta}),&
 E_s^c&=(e_s,w'(r_s)).                              \label{eq:resource-gauge-center-history}
\end{align}
Then \(E_s^c=\partial_r\mathcal H_{V,w,q}(r_s)\), and
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal U_*(s,\sigma)E_\sigma^c
 =\frac{q(r_s)}{q(r_\sigma)}E_s^c.                 \label{eq:resource-gauge-center-cocycle}
\end{equation}
In particular, the genuine time-translation tangent is
\(\delta q(r_s)E_s^c\), not a frozen slow eigenvector.

Let \((\xi_s,\omega(s))\) solve the full variational equation along \(Z_*\).
Define
\begin{equation}
 a(s)=\frac{\omega(s)}{w'(r_s)},\qquad
 \psi_s(\theta)=\xi(s+\theta)-e_s(\theta)a(s),       \label{eq:resource-gauge-coordinates}
\end{equation}
and put
\begin{equation}
 h_s(\theta)=\frac{e_s(\theta)}{w'(r_s)},\qquad
 H_s=h_s(0)=\frac{V'(r_s)}{w'(r_s)}.                \label{eq:resource-gauge-h}
\end{equation}
At each fixed \(s\), the history \(\psi_s\) is unchanged when any multiple of
\(E_s^c\) is added to the full variation.  With \(u(s)=\psi_s(0)\), the exact triangular
quotient equations are
\begin{align}
 a'(s)&=\delta q'(r_s)a(s)
       +\frac{\delta}{w'(r_s)}\pi_N^Tu(s),           \label{eq:resource-gauge-a}\\
 (\partial_s-\partial_\theta)\psi_s(\theta)
 &=-\delta h_s(\theta)\pi_N^Tu(s),                  \label{eq:resource-gauge-transport}\\
 u'(s)&=\delta^{-1}\mathcal J_*(s)u(s)
 +\delta K\sum_{k=0}^mL_{k,N}(\eta)
       \{u(s)-\psi_s(-\theta_k)\}
 -\delta H_s\pi_N^Tu(s),                            \label{eq:resource-gauge-boundary}
\end{align}
where the transport identity is for
\(-\theta_m\le\theta<0\), in the mild characteristic sense for continuous
histories; \(\theta=0\) is governed by
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-boundary}.  Here
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal J_*(s)=\mathcal A_N-\diag\!\left(
 2c_N\circ\{V(r_s)-\mathbf1\}
 +\beta\{V(r_s)-\mathbf1\}^{\circ2}\right).         \label{eq:resource-gauge-current-matrix}
\end{equation}

This transport--boundary system has an exact realization on an ordinary
voltage history.  For \(u_s(\sigma)=u(s+\sigma)\), define the Volterra
operator
\begin{equation}
 (\mathcal K_s\phi)(\theta)
 =\delta\int_\theta^0
 h_{s+\sigma}(\theta-\sigma)\pi_N^T\phi(\sigma)\,\dd\sigma.
                                                               \label{eq:resource-gauge-volterra}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \psi_s=(\Id-\mathcal K_s)u_s,                       \label{eq:resource-gauge-history-conjugacy}
\end{equation}
and \(u\) satisfies the standard nonautonomous RFDE
\begin{align}
 u'(s)={}&\delta^{-1}\mathcal J_*(s)u(s)
 +\delta K\sum_{k=0}^mL_{k,N}(\eta)
 \bigl[u(s)-u(s-\theta_k)
       +(\mathcal K_su_s)(-\theta_k)\bigr]\notag\\
 &-\delta H_s\pi_N^Tu(s).                           \label{eq:resource-gauge-standard-rfde}
\end{align}

Suppose, on the whole delay collar, that \(q\), its reciprocal, and \(V'\)
have common bounds, and that
\begin{equation}
 |w'(r)|\ge c_w|r|,\qquad |r|\ge c_\rho\rho_\delta. \label{eq:resource-gauge-collar-bounds}
\end{equation}
For every \(\kappa_*\ge0\), use
\[
 \|\phi\|^\sharp_{\kappa_*}
 =\sup_{-\theta_m\le\theta\le0}
 e^{2\kappa_*\theta}\|\phi(\theta)\|_N.
\]
Then, uniformly in \(N\) and the orbit point,
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal K_s\|
 \le C\frac{\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 \le\frac{C}{S_\delta}.                             \label{eq:resource-gauge-volterra-bound}
\end{equation}
There is \(\delta_0>0\) such that, for \(0<\delta<\delta_0\),
\begin{equation}
 \|(\Id-\mathcal K_s)^{-1}\|\le2,\qquad
 \|(\Id-\mathcal K_s)^{-1}-\Id\|\le\frac{C}{S_\delta}.
                                                               \label{eq:resource-gauge-volterra-inverse}
\end{equation}
Conversely, on this range
\begin{equation}
 u_s=(\Id-\mathcal K_s)^{-1}\psi_s,                 \label{eq:resource-gauge-history-inverse}
\end{equation}
so \cref{eq:resource-gauge-history-conjugacy} is a two-sided coordinate
equivalence between the quotient transport--boundary system and
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-standard-rfde}; the scalar \(a\) is then recovered
from \cref{eq:resource-gauge-a}.
The full moving norm
\begin{equation}
 \|(\xi_s,\omega)\|_{*,s}
 =\|\xi_s\|^\sharp_{\kappa_*}
   +\frac{|\omega|}{|w'(r_s)|}                      \label{eq:resource-gauge-anisotropic-norm}
\end{equation}
is uniformly equivalent to
\(\|\psi_s\|^\sharp_{\kappa_*}+|a(s)|\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Local two-sided quotient flushing at a physical hit]
\label[proposition]{prop:physical-hit-quotient-flushing}
Retain
\cref{prop:raw-gauge-strong-hit-buffer,%
prop:exact-resource-gauge-quotient}
on the shared-resource law, still pointwise at one fixed admissible
parameter pair.  Fix \(A>0\), put
\begin{equation}
 L_\delta=AS_\delta,\qquad
 \kappa_{\sigma,h}
 =\kappa_{N,\delta}(e_{\sigma,h}),                \label{eq:hit-flushing-length-rate}
\end{equation}
and equip \(\mathscr X_N\) with the fixed hit norm
\begin{equation}
 \|\phi\|_{\sigma,h}^{\sharp}
 =\sup_{-\theta_m\le\theta\le0}
   e^{2\kappa_{\sigma,h}\theta}\|\phi(\theta)\|_N.
                                                               \label{eq:hit-fixed-fading-norm}
\end{equation}
For every raw gauge in the common ball, write
\[
 s_h=S_{\sigma,h}(g),\qquad
 I_{\sigma,h}(g)=[s_h-L_\delta,s_h+L_\delta].
\]
After decreasing \(\delta_0(A,r_{\rm out})\), this interval and its
one-delay enlargement lie in the common generated buffer.  Every solution
\(u\) of the exact standard quotient RFDE
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-standard-rfde} along that already constructed
tracker obeys
\begin{align}
 \|u_{s_h}\|_{a,h}^{\sharp}
 &\le C e^{-c\kappa_{a,h}L_\delta}
       \|u_{s_h-L_\delta}\|_{a,h}^{\sharp},
                                                        \label{eq:hit-attracting-interior-flush}\\
 \|u_{s_h}\|_{r,h}^{\sharp}
 &\le C e^{-c\kappa_{r,h}L_\delta}
   \left\{\|u_{s_h-L_\delta}\|_{r,h}^{\sharp}
          +\|u_{s_h+L_\delta}\|_{r,h}^{\sharp}\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:hit-repelling-interior-flush}
\end{align}
Uniformly in \(N\) and the retained indices,
\begin{equation}
 \kappa_{\sigma,h}\ge c_\kappa\log(1/\delta),
 \qquad
 e^{2\kappa_{\sigma,h}\theta_m}\le\delta^{-2\vartheta}.
                                                        \label{eq:hit-fading-rate-bounds}
\end{equation}
Thus the right-hand sides of
\cref{eq:hit-attracting-interior-flush,%
eq:hit-repelling-interior-flush}
are superalgebraically small relative to uniformly bounded endpoint
histories.

The repelling estimate uses a stable-left/one-dimensional-unstable-right
current Green representation; its backward factor is only the inverse on
the transported finite-dimensional unstable line.  This is a local,
fixed-parameter interior estimate for already constructed physical branch
segments, not a global invariant normal bundle or a new boundary-value
inverse.  It does not extend either segment through the fold, identify a
repelling invariant history sheet, take parameter derivatives, relate the
two branches, or construct a physical connection or root.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[First-order raw-gauge forgetting at fixed physical hits]
\label[theorem]{thm:physical-hit-first-order-flatness}
Retain \cref{prop:physical-hit-quotient-flushing} and use the
\(\varepsilon\)-normalized event norm
\begin{equation}
 \|(\phi,\omega)\|_{\mathrm{ev},h}
 =\varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
       \|\phi\|_{\infty,N}+\frac{|\omega|}{r_h}\right\},
 \qquad
 \|\phi\|_{\infty,N}
 =\sup_{-\theta_m\le\theta\le0}\|\phi(\theta)\|_N,
 \qquad \varepsilon=\delta^2.                    \label{eq:physical-hit-event-normal-norm}
\end{equation}
For every \(P>0\) there are \(C_P,\delta_P>0\), independent of \(N\),
the branch, the fixed admissible parameter point, and the gauges in the
common convex ball, such that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\sigma\in\{a,r\}}\sup_g
 \|D_g\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)\|_
   {\mathcal L(\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h,
               (\mathscr Y_N,\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{ev},h}))}
 \le C_P\delta^P,
 \qquad 0<\delta<\delta_P.                       \label{eq:physical-hit-gauge-flat-derivative}
\end{equation}
In particular, for any two gauges in that ball,
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g_1)
       -\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g_2)\|_{\mathrm{ev},h}
 \le C_P\delta^P
       \|g_1-g_2\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.          \label{eq:physical-hit-gauge-flat-pair}
\end{equation}
Hence each physical section carries a well-defined raw-gauge-independent
flat class
\begin{equation}
 [\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}]_{\mathrm{flat}}
 :=
 [\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)]_{\mathcal O(\delta^\infty)}
 \quad\hbox{in }\Sigma_{N,e_{\sigma,h}}.           \label{eq:physical-hit-flat-class}
\end{equation}
Here \(g=g(\delta)\) may be any uniformly bounded gauge family in the
common ball, and the equivalence relation is the all-orders estimate
\cref{eq:physical-hit-gauge-flat-pair}.

The class in \cref{eq:physical-hit-flat-class} is asymptotic: it does not
assert exact gauge independence at a fixed positive \(\delta\).
The event quotient removes the time tangent, so the theorem does not make
the hit time \(S_{\sigma,h}(g)\) or its scaled displacement
\(\widehat\tau_{\sigma,h}(g)\) flat.  It constructs two separate
raw-gauge-independent hit classes, not a branch-to-branch relation.  In
particular, it
does not prove fold passage, membership in a repelling stable sheet,
parameter-jet flatness, a signed connection gap, transversality, or a
physical root.
\end{theorem}

The fixed-parameter qualifier in the preceding theorem cannot be removed by
compactness alone: superalgebraic closeness of histories does not control
their parameter derivatives.  We next construct the required common
parameter chart and then differentiate the quotient argument itself.  Put
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal I_{\rm phys}
 =\{(i,j):0\le i\le1,\ 0\le j\le2\}
   \cup\{(i,j):0\le i\le2,\ 0\le j\le1\},       \label{eq:physical-rectangular-index-set}
\end{equation}
and, for a Banach-valued map $F(\nu,\eta)$, write
\begin{equation}
 \|F\|_{\mathcal J_{\rm phys};X}
 =\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal I_{\rm phys}}
   \sup_{(\nu,\eta)}
   \|\partial_\nu^iD_\eta^jF(\nu,\eta)\|_{
      \mathcal L^j(\mathfrak T_N^{\rm red};X)}.   \label{eq:physical-rectangular-jet-norm}
\end{equation}
Thus

\[
 \mathcal J_{\rm phys}
 =(C_\nu^1C_\eta^2)\cap(C_\nu^2C_\eta^1),
\]

including the mixed third derivatives
$\partial_\nu D_{\eta\eta}^2$ and
$\partial_{\nu\nu}^2D_\eta$, but not a fourth-order
$\partial_{\nu\nu}^2D_{\eta\eta}^2$ slot.

For the gauge variable we retain exactly the staircase needed to compose
with a parameter-dependent gauge field:
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal K_{\rm phys}
 =\{(\ell,i,j):0\le\ell\le1,
                  \ (i,j)\in\mathcal I_{\rm phys}\}
  \cup\{(\ell,i,j):2\le\ell\le3,
                  \ \ell+i+j\le3\}.             \label{eq:physical-gauge-staircase}
\end{equation}
Thus one gauge derivative may be followed by the complete rectangular
parameter jet, while the higher gauge derivatives needed by the
third-order chain rule are retained through total order three.  This is
not a claim of full $C_g^3\mathcal J_{\rm phys}$ product regularity.

The rough current graph cannot be trivialized by translating or rescaling
its time interval: translation is not Fr\'{e}chet differentiable on the
piecewise $W^{1,\infty}$ graph space.  We instead freeze the reference
fold-time equation itself.

\begin{lemma}[Fixed-reference rough residual chart]
\label[lemma]{lem:fixed-reference-parameter-residual}
Retain
\cref{prop:fold-time-tracker-normal-form,%
thm:raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse,%
thm:raw-gauge-fixed-section-zero}
at order $h\ge8$, and fix an admissible reference parameter
$\lambda^\circ=(\nu^\circ,\eta^\circ)$.  Let
$\bar r_\sigma^\circ:[0,S_\sigma^\circ]\to I_\sigma$ be its reduced
fold-time path.  Use this one interval, its fixed-delay enlargement, and
the graph and range norms at $\lambda^\circ$ for every nearby parameter.
Here $\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga,\circ}$,
$\mathcal Y_{\sigma,\delta}^{\circ}$, and
$\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^{h,\circ}$ denote the resulting fixed copies
of, respectively, the domain, the product target of
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-residual}, and the normalized raw-gauge space.
There are common neighborhoods and an exact residual
\begin{equation}
 \widehat{\mathcal F}_{\sigma,\lambda^\circ}:
 \mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga,\circ}
 \times\mathcal P_N^\circ
 \times\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^{h,\circ}
 \longrightarrow\mathcal Y_{\sigma,\delta}^{\circ}          \label{eq:fixed-reference-parameter-residual}
\end{equation}
with the following properties.

For $u=(x,a)$, reconstruct on the fixed interval
\begin{equation}
 r_u(s)=\Theta_a^\circ(\bar r_\sigma^\circ(s)),\qquad
 v_u(s)=\mathbf1+r_u(s)\mathbf1+z_{0,N}(r_u(s))+Z_x(s),
 \qquad
 w_u(s)=w_{\rm seed,N}(r_u(s))-p_x(s),            \label{eq:fixed-reference-physical-fields}
\end{equation}
At a zero, the inner phase row fixes exactly one end of each phase path:
\begin{equation}
 r_{a,u}(S_a^\circ)=\rho_\delta,
 \qquad
 r_{r,u}(0)=-\rho_\delta.                       \label{eq:fixed-reference-inner-endpoints}
\end{equation}
The opposite, outer endpoint is free.  Thus the fixed duration does not
impose two endpoint conditions on either branch.

For clarity, the fixed row operation used below is part of the definition.
Let $H_{\sigma,\lambda,g}(u)$ be the actual-parameter compatible old
collar returned by the raw history package, let $\gamma_x$ be the trial
old-history coordinate, and put
\begin{equation}
 R_\gamma=\gamma_x-H_{\sigma,\lambda,g}(u).       \label{eq:fixed-reference-collar-mismatch}
\end{equation}
Write $R_{\rm bulk}^{H}$ for the two unmodified raw fold-time residuals
when $H_{\sigma,\lambda,g}(u)$ is inserted in every negative-time delayed
slot.  If the raw voltage residual is written as left side minus right
side, the fixed-reference old-history injection has unprojected voltage
and resource components
\begin{equation}
 (\mathbf J_{\lambda^\circ}^{\rm old}\rho)(s)
 =\delta K\!\sum_{k:\,s-\theta_k<0}
       L_{k,N}(\eta^\circ)\rho(s-\theta_k),
 \qquad
 (\mathbf J_{\lambda^\circ}^{\rm old,w}\rho)(s)=0. \label{eq:fixed-reference-old-history-injection}
\end{equation}
Let $\mathcal J_{\lambda^\circ}^{\rm old}$ denote
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-old-history-injection} after the fixed
collective--transverse row reduction at $\lambda^\circ$.  On the collar
and bulk rows apply the triangular isomorphism
\begin{align}
 \mathscr U_{\lambda^\circ}(R_\gamma,R_{\rm bulk}^{H})
 &=\bigl(R_\gamma,
       R_{\rm bulk}^{H}+\mathcal J_{\lambda^\circ}^{\rm old}R_\gamma\bigr),
                                                        \notag\\
 \mathscr U_{\lambda^\circ}^{-1}(R_\gamma,\widetilde R_{\rm bulk})
 &=\bigl(R_\gamma,
       \widetilde R_{\rm bulk}
          -\mathcal J_{\lambda^\circ}^{\rm old}R_\gamma\bigr).
                                                        \label{eq:fixed-reference-row-isomorphism}
\end{align}
The seam, finite boundary, and inner phase rows are left unchanged.
Both maps in \cref{eq:fixed-reference-row-isomorphism} are bounded in the
normalized fixed-reference product norms, uniformly in $N$ and $\delta$.

Use the raw-compatible collar at the actual parameter and gauge on the
old fixed-delay interval.  The residual in
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-parameter-residual} is obtained by substituting
these fields into the two unmodified raw fold-time equations
\cref{eq:raw-fold-time-fields}, together with the same seam, finite
boundary, and inner phase rows as in
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-residual}, and then applying the fixed bounded row
operations at $\lambda^\circ$.  Thus its zero is equivalent to the
unmodified forward RFDE on the fixed interval, compatibility of its
initial history, and the displayed finite boundary and phase rows; it does
not assert a past orbit.  Indeed, at a zero $R_\gamma=0$, so
$R_{\rm bulk}^{H}=0$ by
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-row-isomorphism}.  No moving trial path or
parameter-dependent terminal evaluation enters the definition.  At the
reference parameter the identity
$R_{\rm bulk}^{\gamma}=R_{\rm bulk}^{H}
 +\mathcal J_{\lambda^\circ}^{\rm old}R_\gamma$
makes it exactly
$\mathcal F_\sigma^g$ in the fixed graph coordinates, and
\begin{equation}
 \left\|\bigl[D_u\widehat{\mathcal F}_{\sigma,\lambda^\circ}
       (u;\lambda,g)\bigr]^{-1}\right\|\le4M       \label{eq:fixed-reference-parameter-inverse}
\end{equation}
on a smaller common product ball.
At a zero, the physical collective speed is represented kinematically by
\begin{equation}
 q_{\sigma,\lambda,g}(r_u(s))
 =q_0^\circ(\bar r_\sigma^\circ(s))
       (\Theta_a^\circ)'(\bar r_\sigma^\circ(s)). \label{eq:fixed-reference-speed-identity}
\end{equation}
Consequently the common sign follows from the reference sign and the
orientation margin of the phase chart; it is not inferred by identifying
the reference speed with the changed physical parameter.

The map is four times Fr\'{e}chet differentiable on that rough product
ball, with derivatives uniform in $N$, $\delta$, and the branch in the
normalized parameter and gauge norms.  More precisely, it has every derivative required by
\cref{eq:physical-gauge-staircase}.  This regularity is strong-to-weak only
where a time derivative is spent: the fixed translations
$s\mapsto s-\theta_k$ are bounded linear maps, while the finite
compatibility recursion and the nonlinear phase Nemytskii maps supply the
remaining derivatives.  The lemma makes no same-space differentiability
claim for a moving translation on piecewise $W^{1,\infty}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proposition}[Parameter-coherent physical-hit families]
\label[proposition]{prop:parameter-coherent-physical-hit-family}
Retain
\cref{thm:raw-gauge-fixed-section-zero,%
prop:raw-gauge-strong-hit-buffer,%
lem:fixed-reference-parameter-residual,%
lem:terminal-relative-trace-scale-chart}
at one fixed order $h\ge8$.  After decreasing the common structural ball
and the raw-gauge ball, the following conclusions hold on one common open
parameter box
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal P_N=(-\nu_*,\nu_*)
   \times B_{\eta_*}^{\mathfrak T_N^{\rm red}}(0)\subset
       \R\times\mathfrak T_N^{\rm red}.          \label{eq:physical-hit-parameter-box}
\end{equation}

First, use the single fixed-reference residual chart in
\cref{lem:fixed-reference-parameter-residual}, centered at the middle of
the displayed box.  This freezes the interval and the fixed-delay graph
before the parameter is varied; it is not a translation or rescaling of a
rough trial path.  Its unique zero
\begin{equation}
 (\nu,\eta,g)\longmapsto
 u_{\sigma,\delta}(\nu,\eta,g)
 \in\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}          \label{eq:parameter-coherent-raw-zero}
\end{equation}
has every mixed derivative
\begin{equation}
 D_g^\ell\partial_\nu^iD_\eta^j
       u_{\sigma,\delta},\qquad
 (\ell,i,j)\in\mathcal K_{\rm phys},             \label{eq:physical-hit-mixed-derivative-class}
\end{equation}
and these derivatives are dimension uniformly bounded as multilinear maps
from the normalized gauge and parameter spaces.  The inverse at every zero
satisfies \cref{eq:fixed-reference-parameter-inverse}.  In particular, the exact reconstructed
RFDE segments have the rectangular parameter jets, the fixed-parameter
$C_g^3$ jets, and precisely the mixed staircase in
\cref{eq:physical-gauge-staircase}.  They retain, uniformly on the box,
\begin{equation}
 q_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}\le-c_q<0,
 \qquad
 c_w|r|\le|W_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}'(r)|\le C_w|r|,
 \qquad
 \|V_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}'(r)\|_N\le C_V          \label{eq:parameter-coherent-tracker-signs}
\end{equation}
on the common generated collar containing the physical hit.
Moreover, with
\(\mathbf y=(r/r_h,Z/\varepsilon,p/(\varepsilon r_h))\), the zero family
has the triangular strong-to-weak ledger
\begin{equation}
 \sup_s\delta^d
 \left\|\partial_s^d
 D_g^\ell\partial_\nu^iD_\eta^j\mathbf y(s)\right\|
 \le C_{\ell,i,j,d},
 \qquad
 (\ell,i,j)\in\mathcal K_{\rm phys},\quad
 0\le d\le h-(\ell+i+j).                         \label{eq:parameter-coherent-time-jet-ledger}
\end{equation}
The supremum is on the common generated subbuffer used below, including
the delay collar.  In particular, since $h\ge8$, every mixed slot retained
here has the extra time derivatives needed to translate it into a scaled
$C^3$ event window.

Second, fix $A>0$, put $L_\delta=AS_\delta$, and use the event-relative
window
\begin{equation}
 K_{\delta,h}=[-L_\delta-\theta_m,L_\delta].       \label{eq:parameter-coherent-event-window}
\end{equation}
The fixed-chart endpoints straddle the physical section with a uniform
coordinate margin: for some $c_{\rm end}>0$,
\begin{equation}
 r_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}(0)-e_{\sigma,h}
       \ge c_{\rm end}r_{\rm out},
 \qquad
 e_{\sigma,h}-r_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}(S_\sigma^\circ)
       \ge c_{\rm end}r_{\rm out}.              \label{eq:parameter-coherent-endpoint-gap}
\end{equation}
Consequently there is a unique hit $S_{\sigma,h}$ and, for a uniform
$c_{\rm hit}>0$,
\begin{equation}
 \min\{S_{\sigma,h}-\theta_m,
            S_\sigma^\circ-S_{\sigma,h}\}
 \ge \frac{c_{\rm hit}}{\delta}.                \label{eq:parameter-coherent-hit-margin}
\end{equation}
For the exact tracker triple $Y=(r,Z,p)$, define
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal Y_{\sigma,h}^{\#}(\nu,\eta,g)(\varsigma)
 =Y_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}
    (S_{\sigma,h}(\nu,\eta,g)+\varsigma),
 \qquad \varsigma\in K_{\delta,h}.              \label{eq:parameter-coherent-event-buffer}
\end{equation}
After decreasing \(\delta_0(A,r_{\rm out})\), this is a common generated
buffer for all retained arguments.  If
\(b=\ell+i+j\) and the derivative belongs to
\cref{eq:physical-hit-mixed-derivative-class}, then
\begin{equation}
 \bigl\|D_g^\ell\partial_\nu^iD_\eta^j
     \mathcal Y_{\sigma,h}^{\#}\bigr\|_{
     \mathcal L^{\ell+j}(\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h,
       \mathfrak T_N^{\rm red};\mathfrak S^3(K_{\delta,h}))}
 \le C_b\delta^{-2b}.                            \label{eq:parameter-coherent-event-buffer-jets}
\end{equation}
Here the notation on the left means the corresponding mixed multilinear
operator, with the scalar \(\nu\)-directions suppressed, and
$\mathfrak S^3(K_{\delta,h})$ is the norm in
\cref{eq:strong-hit-buffer-norm} with $B_{\sigma,h}$ replaced by
$K_{\delta,h}$.  The exponent is a safe strong-to-weak translation loss,
not an assertion that every slot actually attains it.  Writing
\begin{equation}
 v(s)=\mathbf1+r(s)\mathbf1+z_{0,N}(r(s))+Z(s),
 \qquad w(s)=w_{\rm seed,N}(r(s))-p(s),           \label{eq:parameter-coherent-physical-reconstruction}
\end{equation}
restriction of the event-relative buffer to $[-\theta_m,0]$ gives the
complete-history hit map with the same staircase regularity,
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(\nu,\eta,g)
 =\left(\theta\longmapsto
       v_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}
          (S_{\sigma,h}(\nu,\eta,g)+\theta),
       \ w_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}
          (S_{\sigma,h}(\nu,\eta,g))\right)
 \in\Sigma_{N,e_{\sigma,h}}.                     \label{eq:parameter-coherent-hit-map}
\end{equation}
All constants are independent of $N$, $\delta$, the two branches, and the
point of the retained parameter box.

This proposition constructs a parameter-coherent family on the fixed
physical interior sections relative to the chosen reference chart
$\lambda^\circ$.  It proves no covariance between two different
fixed-reference charts.  It does not yet estimate the differentiated
quotient, identify the original outer endpoints, or construct a terminal
Schur inverse, repelling history sheet, branch-to-branch relation, or root.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Rectangular-jet raw-gauge forgetting at physical hits]
\label[theorem]{thm:physical-hit-rectangular-flatness}
Retain
\cref{prop:parameter-coherent-physical-hit-family,%
prop:physical-hit-quotient-flushing,%
thm:physical-hit-first-order-flatness}.
For every $P>0$ and every
$(\ell,i,j)\in\mathcal K_{\rm phys}$ with
\begin{equation}
 \ell\ge1,                                      \label{eq:physical-hit-flat-mixed-index}
\end{equation}
there are $C_{P,\ell,i,j},\delta_{P,\ell,i,j}>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}
 \bigl\|D_g^\ell\partial_\nu^iD_\eta^j
       \mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(\nu,\eta,g)\bigr\|_{
  \mathcal L^{\ell+j}(\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h,
       \mathfrak T_N^{\rm red};
       (\mathscr Y_N,\|\cdot\|_{\rm ev,h}))}
 \le C_{P,\ell,i,j}\delta^P.                   \label{eq:physical-hit-mixed-flatness}
\end{equation}
The supremum is over the common gauge and parameter boxes, and the constants
are independent of $N$.

More generally, for $a=1,2$ let
$(g_{a,N,\delta,\sigma})_{N,\delta,\sigma}$ be a family of maps from
$\mathcal P_N$ to $\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h$ taking values in the
common convex gauge ball and having tame rectangular jets.  Assume that
the following constants are independent of $N$, $\delta$, and the branch;
we suppress those three indices below:
\begin{equation}
 \|g_a\|_{\mathcal J_{\rm phys};
             \mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}
 \le C_{12}\delta^{-M_{12}}e^{c_{12}S_\delta},
 \qquad a=1,2.                                   \label{eq:physical-hit-tame-gauge-fields}
\end{equation}
Then for every $P>0$,
\begin{equation}
 \bigl\|\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(\cdot,g_1(\cdot))
       -\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(\cdot,g_2(\cdot))
       \bigr\|_{\mathcal J_{\rm phys};
             (\mathscr Y_N,\|\cdot\|_{\rm ev,h})}
 \le C_{P,12}\delta^P.                          \label{eq:physical-hit-rectangular-pair-flatness}
\end{equation}
Thus each of the two physical sections carries a well-defined
\(\mathcal J_{\rm phys}\)-flat complete-history class relative to the
chosen fixed-reference chart, independent of the raw gauge within the
stated tame class.  The constant $C_{P,12}$ is likewise independent of
$N$, $\delta$, and the branch.

The conclusion concerns differences between physical representatives.
Parameter derivatives of one representative are generally nonzero and are
not flushed away; those derivatives contain the response that a later
connection conormal must retain.  The theorem still gives two separate hit
classes.  It does not make the hit times flat, supply an
\(\varepsilon\)-normalized full phase--normal RFDE terminal Schur inverse,
pass through the fold,
construct a repelling sheet or signed membership gap, or prove a physical
root.  Nor does it identify the classes produced from two different
reference charts.
\end{theorem}

\begin{definition}[Endpoint-admissible raw gauges]
\label[definition]{def:endpoint-admissible-raw-gauges}
Retain the normalized raw-gauge spaces in
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-spaces,eq:raw-gauge-norms}.  For the moving-duration
original-endpoint problem put
\begin{align}
 \mathfrak G_{a,\delta}^{\rm oe,h}
 &=E_N\times\R\times\mathscr K_{N,\delta}^h
   =\mathfrak B_{a,\delta}^h,\notag\\
 \mathfrak G_{r,\delta}^{\rm oe,h}
 &=E_N\times\mathscr K_{N,\delta}^h,
 &g_r^{\rm oe}&=(\beta_r,\widehat\gamma_r),     \label{eq:endpoint-admissible-gauge-spaces}
\end{align}
with the norms inherited from \cref{eq:raw-gauge-norms} after deleting
the last scalar summand on the repelling branch.  Thus an attracting
endpoint gauge still contains its physical entry resource datum \(b_a\).
On the repelling branch the future scalar datum \(b_r\) is not a gauge:
the unique future scalar freedom is used instead by the physical speed
row
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_r^{\rm sp,nl}=\varepsilon Q_e=0.     \label{eq:endpoint-repelling-speed-allocation}
\end{equation}
The terminal voltage history remains a free triangular interface unknown;
it is not an additional Fredholm boundary condition.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[The repelling terminal scalar is a row, not a gauge]
\label[proposition]{prop:endpoint-repelling-row-allocation}
Retain \cref{prop:repelling-speed-aligned-terminal-row}, including its
receding joint-diagonal hypotheses and uniform source-split restrictions.
At the repelling original endpoint, the speed row in
\cref{eq:endpoint-repelling-speed-allocation} and the old future resource
row occupy the same one-dimensional terminal Fredholm slot.  More
precisely, on the closed terminal scalar column
\(\mathfrak u_r^{\rm ter}b=G_r(0,b,0,0)\),
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}\mathfrak u_r^{\rm ter}b
 =\chi_{r,\delta}b,
 \qquad
 |\chi_{r,\delta}-1|
 \le C\omega_{r,\delta}^{\rm sp}=o(1).          \label{eq:endpoint-row-allocation-isomorphism}
\end{equation}
Consequently, in the linear bordered problem, after all other data have
been fixed, the speed row determines the terminal scalar uniquely.  If a
later nonlinear construction supplies a classical moving-endpoint zero,
this row is its transverse differential for the constraint \(Q_e=0\).
The proposition itself does not construct that zero.  Prescribing an
independent \(b_r\) as well
would overdetermine the bordered problem;
deleting the speed row would instead produce a transversal family and
would lose the endpoint identities in
\cref{eq:speed-aligned-endpoint-consequences,eq:speed-aligned-endpoint-shear}.
The allocation in
\cref{def:endpoint-admissible-raw-gauges} is therefore forced by the
linear Fredholm geometry rather than chosen as a normalization.
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[Higher slow-clock feet in a fixed swept bundle]
\label[lemma]{lem:higher-slow-clock-feet}
Retain \cref{lem:terminal-clock-pullback-border,cor:fixed-envelope-terminal-clock-border},
but choose the fixed terminal cutoff in \(C^5\) so that
\begin{equation}
 S_\delta^j\|\chi_\delta^{(j)}\|_\infty\le C_j,
 \qquad 1\le j\le5.                            \label{eq:higher-clock-cutoff-bounds}
\end{equation}
Freeze the interval, cutoff, swept window, and hard source interface
before varying parameters.  On every fixed normalized clock ball
\(|\beta|\le c_\beta\rho_\delta\) for which both the fixed swept-window
containment and \cref{eq:terminal-clock-diffeomorphism-margin} hold, the
implicit feet
in \cref{eq:terminal-clock-foot-identities} have the uniform jets
\begin{equation}
 \max_{1\le j\le4}
 \rho_\delta^j
 \|\partial_\beta^j\sigma_{k,\beta}\|_{C^1}
 \le C,
 \qquad 0\le k\le m.                            \label{eq:higher-clock-foot-jets}
\end{equation}
The same conclusion holds after any mixed parameter or gauge derivative
which reaches a foot only through a Banach-space clock field
\(\beta=\beta(\nu,\eta,g)\), with the ordinary Banach-space chain-rule
products of the displayed \(\beta\)-derivatives and the jets of the clock
field on the right.  No moving characteristic function
or moving rough graph occurs in this statement.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Outer-annulus terminal clock at the \(R_\delta\) scale]
\label[lemma]{lem:outer-annulus-terminal-clock}
Retain \cref{thm:short-clock-original-endpoint-segment} on the repelling
branch, write \(S_*=S_r^{\rm oe}\), and let
\(r_*:[-\theta_m,S_*]\to\mathbb R\) be its physical collective path.
There is a fixed \(a_R>0\), independent of \(N\) and the diagonal wedge,
such that, with
\begin{equation}
 L_{R,\delta}=a_R\frac{R_\delta}{\delta},        \label{eq:outer-clock-transition-length}
\end{equation}
the delay-enlarged terminal interval
\begin{equation}
 J_{R,\delta}=[S_*-L_{R,\delta}-2\theta_m,S_*]  \label{eq:outer-clock-terminal-window}
\end{equation}
lies in the generated outer annulus and satisfies
\begin{equation}
 -R_\delta\le r_*(s)\le-\frac12R_\delta,
 \qquad s\in J_{R,\delta}.                      \label{eq:outer-clock-terminal-annulus}
\end{equation}
Choose a fixed-shape cutoff \(\chi_{R,\delta}\in C^5\) with
\begin{align}
 \chi_{R,\delta}&=0
 &&\text{on }[-\theta_m,S_*-\theta_m-L_{R,\delta}],\notag\\
 \chi_{R,\delta}&=1
 &&\text{on }[S_*-\theta_m,S_*],\notag\\
 \left(\frac{R_\delta}{\delta}\right)^j
   \|\chi_{R,\delta}^{(j)}\|_\infty&\le C_j,
 &&1\le j\le5.                                  \label{eq:outer-clock-cutoff-bounds}
\end{align}
For \(|\beta|\le c_RR_\delta\), where \(c_R>0\) is fixed sufficiently
small, put
\begin{equation}
 \Xi_\beta(s)=s+\frac\beta\delta\chi_{R,\delta}(s),
 \qquad
 \sigma_{k,\beta}(s)
 =\Xi_\beta^{-1}\{\Xi_\beta(s)-\theta_k\}.       \label{eq:outer-clock-map-and-feet}
\end{equation}
Then \(\Xi_\beta\) is an increasing diffeomorphism, all its delay feet
remain in the fixed old-history/current interval, and
\begin{align}
 \|\sigma_{k,\beta}-(\,\cdot-\theta_k)\|_{C^0}
 +\|\partial_s\sigma_{k,\beta}-1\|_{C^0}
 &\le C\frac{|\beta|}{R_\delta},                \label{eq:outer-clock-foot-displacement}\\
 \max_{1\le j\le4}R_\delta^j
 \|\partial_\beta^j\sigma_{k,\beta}\|_{C^1}
 &\le C_j.                                      \label{eq:outer-clock-foot-jets}
\end{align}
The conclusions persist after the mixed Banach-space chain rule for a
clock field \(\beta=\beta(\nu,\eta,g)\), provided its normalized jets are
measured with \(|\beta|/R_\delta\).  The cutoff, interval, and every hard
source interface are frozen before those derivatives are taken.

For the physical event row and its terminal clock column use
\begin{equation}
 E_R(Y)=\frac{r_Y(S_*)+R_\delta}{q_0(-R_\delta)},
 \qquad \|b_E\|_{\mathbb R_{R_\delta}}=\frac{|b_E|}{R_\delta}.
                                                        \label{eq:outer-clock-event-row}
\end{equation}
At the exact endpoint zero,
\begin{equation}
 c_{R,\rm ter}
 =\frac{r_*'(S_*)}{\delta q_0(-R_\delta)}=1,
 \qquad
 K_{p,R}=R_{q,N}(-R_\delta),                    \label{eq:outer-clock-scalar-column}
\end{equation}
and the scalar column is uniformly bounded from
\(\mathbb R_{R_\delta}\) into the speed-row range normalized by
\(|b_{\rm sp}|/(\varepsilon R_\delta)\).
If, on the fixed terminal history window,
\begin{equation}
 \|Z_*'\|_{C^0}\le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{R_\delta},
 \qquad
 \|Z_*''\|_{C^0}\le C\frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta},          \label{eq:outer-clock-history-jet-gate}
\end{equation}
then the actual history shear
\begin{equation}
 (K_{Z,R}\beta)(\theta)
 =\frac{Z_*'(S_*+\theta)}\delta\,\beta,
 \qquad-\theta_m\le\theta\le0,                 \label{eq:outer-clock-history-column}
\end{equation}
is uniformly bounded from \(\mathbb R_{R_\delta}\) to
\(\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1\).

This is the endpoint clock scale required by a fixed-size parameter and
raw-gauge family.  It does not assert
\cref{eq:outer-clock-history-jet-gate}; that action-weighted terminal jet
and the corresponding strong Green restriction are separate obligations.
Nor does the lemma attach an event-after tail to the repelling segment.
It also does not construct the clock-pulled strong nonlinear residual:
freezing the hard interfaces is necessary but does not by itself provide
rough-space composition differentiability.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proposition}[True-end repelling second jet at the outer scale]
\label[proposition]{prop:repelling-outer-endpoint-slow-jet}
Retain
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green,%
thm:short-clock-original-endpoint-segment}
at order \(h\ge4\), with \(M\ge2\), on the repelling branch.  Write
\[
 S_*=S_r^{\rm oe},\qquad
 Z_*=Z_r^{\rm oe},\qquad p_*=p_r^{\rm oe},
\]
for the exact physical fold-time segment ending at
\(r_*(S_*)=-R_\delta\), and put
\begin{align}
 \widetilde J_{R,\delta}^{\rm ter}
 &=[S_*-2\theta_m,S_*],\notag\\
 J_{R,\delta}^{\rm ter}
 &=[S_*-\theta_m,S_*].                          \label{eq:outer-endpoint-jet-windows}
\end{align}
After the ordered decrease of the same joint wedge,
\begin{align}
 &\sup_{\widetilde J_{R,\delta}^{\rm ter}}
   \left\{\|Z_*'(s)\|_N+\frac{|p_*'(s)|}{R_\delta}\right\}
 +\delta\sup_{J_{R,\delta}^{\rm ter}}
   \left\{\|Z_*''(s)\|_N+\frac{|p_*''(s)|}{R_\delta}\right\}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{R_\delta}.       \label{eq:outer-endpoint-sharp-second-jet}
\end{align}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \|Z_*'\|_{C^0(J_{R,\delta}^{\rm ter})}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{R_\delta},
 \qquad
 \|Z_*''\|_{C^0(J_{R,\delta}^{\rm ter})}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta}.             \label{eq:outer-endpoint-history-jet}
\end{equation}
Thus \cref{eq:outer-clock-history-jet-gate} holds for the exact base
segment, and the history column in
\cref{eq:outer-clock-history-column} is a uniform map
\(\mathbb R_{R_\delta}\to\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1\).

This is a sharper terminal estimate for the already constructed
fixed-parameter base trajectory.  It does not construct a parameterized
clock-pulled residual, a nonautonomous RFDE dichotomy, or a full
moving-endpoint inverse.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Long-window strong restriction at the exact repelling endpoint]
\label[theorem]{thm:repelling-long-window-strong-restriction}
Retain
\cref{thm:speed-bordered-affine-endpoint-core,%
cor:conditional-event-aligned-source-split-inverse,%
cor:fixed-envelope-terminal-clock-border,%
thm:short-clock-original-endpoint-segment,%
lem:outer-annulus-terminal-clock,%
prop:repelling-outer-endpoint-slow-jet}
on the repelling branch.  Conjugate the fixed-envelope coordinates by the
already selected base clock so that the exact physical endpoint segment is
written on \([ -\theta_m,S_*]\), and freeze the image of the old hard
bulk--terminal interface.  This is a fixed base change, not a new clock
unknown.  All source and domain spaces below are the push-forwards by this
fixed base diffeomorphism; its derivative and inverse are uniformly bounded,
so their transported norms are uniformly equivalent to the physical-time
norms.  We retain the old symbols for these transported spaces.  Retain the
unique source decomposition
\[
 f=f_{\rm B}+f_{\rm bd}+f_T
 \in\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{r,\mathrm{split}+T}
\]
in these transported coordinates.  Write
\(f_{\rm B}=(0,0,F_{\rm B},H_{\rm B})\) and
\(f_T=(0,0,F_T,H_T)\), using zero extensions across the frozen
interface, and set
\begin{equation}
 \Sigma_Rf=(F_{\rm B}+F_T,H_{\rm B}+H_T)|_{J_{R,\delta}}.
                                                        \label{eq:long-window-total-source}
\end{equation}
Define the closed source restriction
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{r,R}^{\mathrm{tot},0}
 =\left\{f\in\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{r,\mathrm{split}+T}:
 \Sigma_Rf\ \hbox{has one representative in }
 C(J_{R,\delta};E_N\times\mathbb R)\right\},              \label{eq:long-window-continuous-total-source}
\end{equation}
with the inherited three-summand norm.  In particular, the two one-sided
traces of the \emph{total} distributed source agree at the artificial
interface.  Neither split summand is required to be continuous there.

Let \(v=(y,a,\tau,h)\) denote the transported fixed-clock upper unknown,
where
\begin{equation}
 y\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}+T}(\mathscr N_r^{\rm sp}),
 \quad a\in\mathsf A_{r,\mathrm{hyb}},
 \quad \tau\in\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta},
 \quad h\in\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1,                           \label{eq:long-window-upper-unknown}
\end{equation}
and let \((Z_v,p_v)\) be its reconstructed exact-base normal tangent.
More precisely, if \(\mathcal Y_*\) denotes the exact physical
reconstruction at the original-endpoint zero, then
\begin{equation}
 (Z_v,p_v)=\operatorname{pr}_{Z,p}
       D\mathcal Y_*(y,a,\tau).                         \label{eq:long-window-reconstructed-tangent}
\end{equation}
Put
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal J_{R}^1(v)
 =\frac1\varepsilon\sup_{J_{R,\delta}}
    \left\{\|Z_v\|_N+\frac{|p_v|}{R_\delta}\right\}
 +\frac\delta\varepsilon\sup_{J_{R,\delta}}
    \left\{\|Z_v'\|_N+\frac{|p_v'|}{R_\delta}\right\},   \label{eq:long-window-strong-normal-norm}
\end{equation}
and let \(\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}\) be the subspace of
\cref{eq:long-window-upper-unknown} for which
\((Z_v,p_v)|_{J_{R,\delta}}\in C^1\), with norm
\begin{equation}
 \|v\|_{\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}}
 =\|y\|_{\mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}+T}(\mathscr N_r^{\rm sp})}
  +\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{r,\mathrm{hyb}}}
  +\frac{|\tau|}{\rho_\delta}
  +\|h\|_{\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1}
  +\mathcal J_R^1(v).                           \label{eq:long-window-upper-domain-norm}
\end{equation}
Set
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}
 =\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{r,R}^{\mathrm{tot},0}
  \times\mathsf H_{r,\mathrm{hyb}}
  \times\mathbb R_{\rho_\delta}^2
  \times\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1,                   \label{eq:long-window-upper-range}
\end{equation}
with the inherited product norm.

Let \(L_{*,\rm up}\) be the exact-base differential of all fixed-clock
upper rows: the raw-compatible history row, the hard-split distributed
normal rows, the collective phase row, the causal-entry and phase-slack
rows, the physical terminal speed row, and the free terminal-history row
\(\mathcal T_Zv-h\).  Let
\begin{equation}
 \ell_*v=DE_R(Y_*)v                                      \label{eq:long-window-event-functional}
\end{equation}
be the physical event differential.  Extend the terminal shear
\(K_R\) by zero in every unchanged row and use its two nonzero components
\begin{equation}
 K_{p,R}=R_{q,N}(-R_\delta),\qquad
 (K_{Z,R}b)(\theta)=\frac{Z_*'(S_*+\theta)}\delta b.
                                                        \label{eq:long-window-upper-shear}
\end{equation}
Then the event-aligned upper operator
\begin{equation}
 L_{*,R}^{\rm ea}=L_{*,\rm up}-K_R\ell_*:
 \mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}
 \longrightarrow\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}              \label{eq:long-window-event-aligned-isomorphism}
\end{equation}
is a dimension- and diagonal-uniform isomorphism.  In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \|v\|_{\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}}
 \le C\|L_{*,R}^{\rm ea}v\|_{\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}}.
                                                        \label{eq:long-window-strong-inverse-bound}
\end{equation}

The theorem is a strong linear restriction at one exact endpoint base.
It does not yet define the \(R_\delta\)-clock nonlinear residual, prove a
two-point composition estimate, differentiate a moving \(r\)-chart, or
construct a parameter/gauge family.
\end{theorem}

The geometric clock in \cref{lem:outer-annulus-terminal-clock} exists on
the larger \(R_\delta\)-ball.  The fixed strong source graph, however,
has a smaller nonlinear action scale.  Put
\begin{equation}
 \Lambda_{R,\delta}=\lambda_\delta R_\delta.     \label{eq:outer-clock-action-scale}
\end{equation}
On the retained joint wedge,
\begin{align}
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{\Lambda_{R,\delta}}&=o(1),&
 \frac{\lambda_\delta R_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}
 &=2\alpha\varkappa^2
   +2\alpha\varkappa^3\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta^{7/2}=o_{\rm ord}(1),
                                                        \notag\\
 \frac{\delta}{\Lambda_{R,\delta}}
 &\le\frac{\delta}{R_\delta^2}
   =\mathfrak w_{\delta,\varkappa}.              \label{eq:outer-clock-action-scale-ledger}
\end{align}
Here \(o_{\rm ord}(1)\) means small after the theorem's ordered choice of
\(\varkappa_0\), followed by the joint wedge decrease.  The first line
follows from \(\lambda_\delta\ge S_\delta^{-2}\) and
\(\mathfrak w_{\delta,\varkappa}\le\mathfrak w_*\).
The distinction between \(R_\delta\) and \(\Lambda_{R,\delta}\) is
substantive: on the full \(R_\delta\)-ball, a mixed clock--normal term in
the bulk source can lose \(1/\lambda_\delta\), while a phase prefix can
lose \(R_\delta^2/\rho_\delta\).  The scale
\(\Lambda_{R,\delta}\) closes both losses without placing an unproved
kinetic restriction on an arbitrary strong-space direction.

Write \(u_*=u_r^{\rm oe}\), \(Y_*=\mathcal Y_*(u_*)\), and
\(h_*=\mathcal T_ZY_*\).  For an algebraic coordinate displacement \(v\)
for which the relative phase reconstruction is defined, let
\(Y(v)=\mathcal Y_*(u_*+\operatorname{pr}_{\ne h}v)\).  In every delayed term below use the same
pulled feet \(\sigma_{k,\beta}\) from
\cref{eq:outer-clock-map-and-feet}.  Define first, before any hard split,
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr R_R^{\rm clk}(v,\beta)(s)
 =\delta\partial_sY(v)(s)
 -\left\{1+\frac{\beta}{\delta}\chi_{R,\delta}'(s)\right\}
 \mathfrak F_N\bigl(Y(v)(s),Y(v)(\sigma_{1,\beta}(s)),\ldots,
                         Y(v)(\sigma_{m,\beta}(s))\bigr).
                                                        \label{eq:outer-clock-unsplit-row}
\end{equation}
Apply the exact collective/normal decomposition used in
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-exact-residual}.  If its two distributed normal
components are \((F_R,H_R)\), define their bulk and terminal summands by
multiplication with the two fixed indicators at the transported old hard
interface.  The boundary summand retains the exact raw-compatible history
and the current-terminal-state physical speed row.  Retain also the exact
collective phase, causal-entry, phase-slack, and free interface row
\(\mathcal T_ZY(v)-(h_*+v_h)\), and append
\[
 E_R(v)=\frac{r_{Y(v)}(S_*)+R_\delta}{q_0(-R_\delta)}.
\]
This defines the augmented residual
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb F_{r,R}^{\rm clk,aug}(v,\beta)
 \in\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}
       \times\mathbb R_{\Lambda_{R,\delta}}
                                                        \label{eq:outer-clock-raw-augmented-residual}
\end{equation}
whenever the displayed membership holds.

For a raw upper residual/event pair \((F,b)\), put
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr S_R(F,b)=(F-K_Rb,b).                  \label{eq:outer-clock-range-shear}
\end{equation}
Equip the \emph{sheared} product
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm clk}
 =\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}
       \times\mathbb R_{\Lambda_{R,\delta}},
 \qquad
 \|(G,b)\|_{\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm clk}}
 =\|G\|_{\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}}
       +\frac{|b|}{\Lambda_{R,\delta}}.         \label{eq:outer-clock-sheared-range-norm}
\end{equation}
Thus the corresponding pullback norm of the raw pair is
\(\|F-K_Rb\|_{\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}}
+|b|/\Lambda_{R,\delta}\).
The shear is applied exactly once.

Let
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf A_{r,R}^{\rm rel,0}
 =\{c\in\mathsf A_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}^1(I_r):
          c(e_{r,0})=0\}                         \label{eq:outer-clock-relative-zero-entry-space}
\end{equation}
carry the inherited relative norm.  Denote by \(\iota_\phi c\) the
coordinate vector which adds \(c\) in the phase slot, adds
\(-c(e_{r,1})\) in the triangular phase-slack slot, and is zero in the
normal and free-history slots.

\begin{proposition}[Relative clock seed and total-source range]
\label[proposition]{prop:outer-clock-relative-seed}
Retain \cref{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart,%
lem:nonlinear-reduced-flow-phase-delay,%
lem:outer-annulus-terminal-clock,%
thm:repelling-long-window-strong-restriction}.  There are uniform
\(c_{\rm seed},C_{\rm seed}>0\) and a \(C^1\) map
\begin{equation}
 c_R^0:(-c_{\rm seed}\Lambda_{R,\delta},
          c_{\rm seed}\Lambda_{R,\delta})
       \longrightarrow\mathsf A_{r,R}^{\rm rel,0}          \label{eq:outer-clock-relative-seed-map}
\end{equation}
such that the exact collective phase row of
\(\mathbb F_{r,R}^{\rm clk,aug}(\iota_\phi c_R^0(\beta),\beta)\)
vanishes.  It is causally anchored by
\(c_R^0(\beta)(e_{r,0})=0\); its endpoint is absorbed by the phase-slack
component in \(\iota_\phi\).  Its value is unique in the fixed small
relative-phase ball used in the proof.  Put
\[
 B=\frac{|\beta|}{\Lambda_{R,\delta}},
 \qquad \widetilde B
   =\frac{|\widetilde\beta|}{\Lambda_{R,\delta}}.
\]
Then
\begin{align}
 \|c_R^0(\beta)\|_{\phi,\rm rel}
 &\le C_{\rm seed}\lambda_\delta B,\notag\\
 \left\|c_R^0(\beta)-\beta a_{R,*}^{\rm seed}\right\|_
       {\phi,\rm rel}
 &\le C_{\rm seed}\lambda_\delta^2B^2,           \label{eq:outer-clock-seed-quadratic}\\
 \left\|\{c_R^0(\beta)-\beta a_{R,*}^{\rm seed}\}
       -\{c_R^0(\widetilde\beta)
               -\widetilde\beta a_{R,*}^{\rm seed}\}\right\|_
       {\phi,\rm rel}
 &\le C_{\rm seed}\lambda_\delta^2(B+\widetilde B)
       \frac{|\beta-\widetilde\beta|}
            {\Lambda_{R,\delta}},               \label{eq:outer-clock-seed-two-point}
\end{align}
where
\begin{equation}
 a_{R,*}^{\rm seed}=Dc_R^0(0)1,\qquad
 \|a_{R,*}^{\rm seed}\|_{\phi,\rm rel}
 \le\frac{C_{\rm seed}}{R_\delta}.               \label{eq:outer-clock-relative-linear-seed}
\end{equation}
This is the phase component of the causal seed with the normal coordinate
held at its base value.  It need not equal the phase component of the
full physical clock tangent; the strong correction in the next lemma is
required before that identification is made.

Let
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb G_R^0(\beta)
 =\operatorname{pr}_{\rm up}\mathscr S_R
    \mathbb F_{r,R}^{\rm clk,aug}
       (\iota_\phi c_R^0(\beta),\beta).          \label{eq:outer-clock-seed-upper-forcing}
\end{equation}
Then \(\mathbb G_R^0(\beta)\in\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}\), and
\begin{align}
 \|\mathbb G_R^0(\beta)\|_{\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}}
 &\le C_{\rm seed}\lambda_\delta B,\notag\\
 \|[\mathbb G_R^0(\beta)-D\mathbb G_R^0(0)\beta]
   -[\mathbb G_R^0(\widetilde\beta)
        -D\mathbb G_R^0(0)\widetilde\beta]\|_
       {\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm up,0}}
 &\le C_{\rm seed}\lambda_\delta^2(B+\widetilde B)
       \frac{|\beta-\widetilde\beta|}
            {\Lambda_{R,\delta}}.               \label{eq:outer-clock-seed-forcing-two-point}
\end{align}
The distributed source in each expression is obtained by first forming
the one continuous total row in \cref{eq:outer-clock-unsplit-row} and
only then applying the fixed split.  Thus its two one-sided total traces
agree even though the hard interface lies inside the long clock
transition.  No individual split summand is asserted to be continuous.

The exact phase seed is essential.  Replacing it by only its linear part
would generically leave a pure-clock phase primitive of size
\(\beta^2/\rho_\delta\).  Since
\(R_\delta^2/\rho_\delta=2\alpha\varkappa^2S_\delta^2\), it diverges
for every fixed \(\varkappa>0\); hence a linear-only construction would
not justify the larger geometric clock ball.  On the action scale used
here the exact seed additionally provides the strong two-point statement
needed below; it is not replaced by a merely formal first-order lift.
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[Exact nonlinear event-sheared outer-clock collar]
\label[lemma]{lem:exact-outer-clock-phase-collar}
Retain \cref{prop:outer-clock-relative-seed}.  After decreasing
\(c_{\rm seed}\), there is a \(C^1\) map
\begin{equation}
 w_R^0:(-c_{\rm seed}\Lambda_{R,\delta},
          c_{\rm seed}\Lambda_{R,\delta})
       \longrightarrow\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1},
 \qquad w_R^0(0)=0,                              \label{eq:outer-clock-strong-collar-correction}
\end{equation}
such that, with
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak C_R(\beta)
 =\iota_\phi c_R^0(\beta)+w_R^0(\beta),          \label{eq:outer-clock-complete-collar}
\end{equation}
one has the exact identity
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{pr}_{\rm up}\mathscr S_R
 \mathbb F_{r,R}^{\rm clk,aug}
       (\mathfrak C_R(\beta),\beta)=0.           \label{eq:outer-clock-collar-upper-zero}
\end{equation}
Its value is unique in the fixed small strong-space ball used in the
contraction.
Moreover,
\begin{align}
 \|w_R^0(\beta)\|_{\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}}
 &\le C_{\rm col}\lambda_\delta B,\notag\\
 \|[w_R^0(\beta)-Dw_R^0(0)\beta]
   -[w_R^0(\widetilde\beta)
        -Dw_R^0(0)\widetilde\beta]\|_
       {\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}}
 &\le C_{\rm col}\lambda_\delta(B+\widetilde B)
       \frac{|\beta-\widetilde\beta|}
            {\Lambda_{R,\delta}}.               \label{eq:outer-clock-strong-collar-two-point}
\end{align}
All causal-entry, phase-slack, speed, and free-interface rows in
\cref{eq:outer-clock-collar-upper-zero} are literal rows of the same
boundary-value problem.  In particular the relative seed does not move
the causal phase anchor, while the normal old history remains governed
by the same raw-compatible row; no new entry gauge is inserted.

If
\begin{equation}
 e_R^{\rm col}(\beta)=E_R(\mathfrak C_R(\beta)), \label{eq:outer-clock-collar-event}
\end{equation}
then
\begin{align}
 e_R^{\rm col}(0)&=0,&D e_R^{\rm col}(0)&=1,\notag\\
 \frac{|e_R^{\rm col}(\beta)-\beta|}
      {\Lambda_{R,\delta}}
 &\le C_{\rm col}\lambda_\delta B^2,\notag\\
 \frac{|[e_R^{\rm col}(\beta)-\beta]
       -[e_R^{\rm col}(\widetilde\beta)-\widetilde\beta]|}
       {\Lambda_{R,\delta}}
 &\le C_{\rm col}\lambda_\delta(B+\widetilde B)
       \frac{|\beta-\widetilde\beta|}
            {\Lambda_{R,\delta}}.               \label{eq:outer-clock-collar-event-two-point}
\end{align}
The derivative identity is the exact clock commutator with
\(c_{R,\rm ter}=1\), now represented as a relative phase seed plus a
strong correction.  It does not assert that
\(\mathfrak C_R(\beta)\) is a physical RFDE zero when \(\beta\ne0\):
\cref{eq:outer-clock-collar-upper-zero} is event-sheared, and its
remaining event is precisely \(e_R^{\rm col}(\beta)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Exact nonlinear outer-clock residual and two-point inverse]
\label[theorem]{thm:exact-nonlinear-outer-clock-residual}
Retain \cref{thm:repelling-long-window-strong-restriction,%
prop:outer-clock-relative-seed,%
lem:exact-outer-clock-phase-collar}.  For
\(w\in\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}\) and
\(|\beta|<c_{\rm seed}\Lambda_{R,\delta}\), reconstruct the exact state from the
base coordinate plus
\begin{equation}
 v=\mathfrak C_R(\beta)+w .                     \label{eq:outer-clock-aligned-coordinate}
\end{equation}
Use the already defined total-source-first augmented residual in
\cref{eq:outer-clock-raw-augmented-residual}, and define
\begin{equation}
 \widehat{\mathbb F}_{r,R}^{\rm clk}(w,\beta)
 =\mathscr S_R\mathbb F_{r,R}^{\rm clk,aug}
       (\mathfrak C_R(\beta)+w,\beta).           \label{eq:outer-clock-aligned-nonlinear-residual}
\end{equation}

There is a dimension- and diagonal-uniform \(r_{R,0}>0\) such that the
map in \cref{eq:outer-clock-aligned-nonlinear-residual} is well defined
and \(C^1\) on
\begin{equation}
 \|w\|_{\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}}
       +\frac{|\beta|}{\Lambda_{R,\delta}}<r_{R,0},
                                                        \label{eq:outer-clock-aligned-ball}
\end{equation}
with values in \(\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm clk}\).  It satisfies
\begin{align}
 \widehat{\mathbb F}_{r,R}^{\rm clk}(0,0)&=0,\notag\\
 D\widehat{\mathbb F}_{r,R}^{\rm clk}(0,0)
 &=\begin{pmatrix}
     L_{*,R}^{\rm ea}&0\\
     \ell_*&1
   \end{pmatrix},                               \label{eq:outer-clock-nonlinear-linearization}
\end{align}
where the last scalar domain and range factors carry the
\(\Lambda_{R,\delta}\)-normalized norm.  In particular this differential and its
inverse are uniform.

Put \(z=(w,\beta)\),
\begin{equation}
 \|z\|_{R,\rm clk}
 =\|w\|_{\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}}
       +\frac{|\beta|}{\Lambda_{R,\delta}},\qquad
 \mathbb Q_{r,R}^{\rm clk}(z)
 =\widehat{\mathbb F}_{r,R}^{\rm clk}(z)
  -D\widehat{\mathbb F}_{r,R}^{\rm clk}(0)z .  \label{eq:outer-clock-nonlinear-remainder}
\end{equation}
Then, after one uniform decrease of \(r_{R,0}\),
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathbb Q_{r,R}^{\rm clk}(z)
       -\mathbb Q_{r,R}^{\rm clk}(\widetilde z)\|_{
          \mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm clk}}
 \le C\{\|z\|_{R,\rm clk}+\|\widetilde z\|_{R,\rm clk}\}
       \|z-\widetilde z\|_{R,\rm clk}.          \label{eq:outer-clock-nonlinear-two-point}
\end{equation}
Consequently there are uniform \(r_{R,1},C_{\rm inv}>0\) and a unique local
right-hand-side solution map
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak Z_R:
 B_{\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm clk}}(0,r_{R,1})
 \longrightarrow
 B_{\mathcal X_{r,R}^{\rm up,1}
       \times\mathbb R_{\Lambda_{R,\delta}}}
       (0,r_{R,0})                               \label{eq:outer-clock-local-solution-map}
\end{equation}
such that
\begin{equation}
 \widehat{\mathbb F}_{r,R}^{\rm clk}
       (\mathfrak Z_R(d))=d,\qquad
 \|\mathfrak Z_R(d)\|_{R,\rm clk}
       \le C_{\rm inv}\|d\|_{\mathcal Y_{r,R}^{\rm clk}}. \label{eq:outer-clock-local-solution-bound}
\end{equation}
It is \(C^1\), and the zero at \(d=0\) is unique in the displayed
clock-aligned ball.

No \(p^\sharp\) variable depending on \(\beta\) is used in the Banach
chart.  Such a shift is used only as a fixed-\(\beta\) algebraic identity
inside the proof, with exactly the same feet \(\sigma_{k,\beta}\), and is
converted back to the physical \(p\) before applying the strong inverse.
The theorem supplies the nonlinear outer-clock chart needed for later
forcing by parameters and gauges.  It does not yet differentiate those
external variables, construct a moving-\(r\) family, or produce a
past-complete orbit or physical root.
It is an action-compatible nonlinear chart inside the larger geometric
\(R_\delta\)-clock domain.  A full \(R_\delta\)-ball strong inverse would
require an additional clock-covariant source-graph trivialization and is
not claimed here.
\end{theorem}

For the same-end repelling boundary problem, let
\begin{equation}
 J_{r,\delta}^{\rm ev}
 =[-3R_\delta/2,-R_\delta/2]                   \label{eq:moving-r-event-tube}
\end{equation}
and equip \(C^4(J_{r,\delta}^{\rm ev})\) with the scaled relative norm
\begin{equation}
 \|a\|_{\phi,\rm rel}^{(4)}
 =\sup_{J_{r,\delta}^{\rm ev}}\frac{|a(r)|}{|r|}
   +\sum_{j=1}^4R_\delta^{j-1}
       \|a^{(j)}\|_{C^0(J_{r,\delta}^{\rm ev})}.             \label{eq:moving-r-event-phase-norm}
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform physical-event preimage in the relative phase chart]
\label[lemma]{lem:moving-r-event-preimage}
Retain the reduced-flow phase map
\begin{equation}
 \Theta_a(r)=\Phi_{q_0}^{a(r)}(r)               \label{eq:moving-r-event-phase-map}
\end{equation}
on the tube \cref{eq:moving-r-event-tube}, where
\begin{equation}
 -q_+\le q_0(r)\le-q_-<0,
 \qquad
 \max_{0\le j\le5}R_\delta^j\|q_0^{(j)}\|_{C^0}
 \le C_q.                                        \label{eq:moving-r-event-speed-bounds}
\end{equation}
There are uniform
\(\chi_{\rm ev},C_{\rm ev}>0\) such that, whenever
\(\|a\|_{\phi,\rm rel}^{(4)}<\chi_{\rm ev}\), the equation
\begin{equation}
 \Theta_a(\zeta_r(a))=-R_\delta                 \label{eq:moving-r-event-preimage-equation}
\end{equation}
has a unique solution in
\([-5R_\delta/4,-3R_\delta/4]\).  The map
\begin{equation}
 a\longmapsto\zeta_r(a)                         \label{eq:moving-r-event-preimage-map}
\end{equation}
is four times Fr\'{e}chet differentiable from the displayed scaled phase
ball to \(\mathbb R\), and
\begin{align}
 \frac{|\zeta_r(a)+R_\delta|}{R_\delta}
 &\le C_{\rm ev}\|a\|_{\phi,\rm rel}^{(4)},\notag\\
 \frac{|D^j\zeta_r(a)[h_1,\ldots,h_j]|}{R_\delta}
 &\le C_{{\rm ev},j}
       \prod_{k=1}^j\|h_k\|_{\phi,\rm rel}^{(4)},
 \qquad1\le j\le4.                             \label{eq:moving-r-event-preimage-jets}
\end{align}
At the unshifted chart,
\begin{equation}
 \zeta_r(0)=-R_\delta,
 \qquad
 D\zeta_r(0)h=-q_0(-R_\delta)h(-R_\delta).      \label{eq:moving-r-event-preimage-first-jet}
\end{equation}

Thus the literal section \(r=-R_\delta\) can be used as the future
boundary of a fixed-tube graph problem without a rough time translation.
The lemma only constructs its phase preimage.  It neither supplies the
truncated phase--normal inverse nor permits a terminal scalar placed at a
different, more remote endpoint to be used in the speed row.
\end{lemma}

For a Banach space \(X\), an interval \(J\) of size \(O(R_\delta)\), and
an integer \(k\ge0\), write
\begin{equation}
 \|f\|_{C^k_{R_\delta}(J;X)}
 =\max_{0\le j\le k}R_\delta^j
       \|\partial_r^jf\|_{C^0(J;X)}.            \label{eq:moving-r-scaled-strong-norm}
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[Generated terminal trivialization of the moving \(r\)-endpoint]
\label[lemma]{lem:moving-r-terminal-trivialization}
Choose a fixed \(\chi_{\rm tr}\in C^8([0,\infty);[0,1])\) such that
\begin{equation}
 \chi_{\rm tr}(0)=1,
 \qquad
 \chi_{\rm tr}(x)=0\quad(x\ge1/4).              \label{eq:moving-r-terminal-cutoff}
\end{equation}
After decreasing the phase radius in
\cref{lem:moving-r-event-preimage}, define, on
\(I_{r,\delta}^{\rm ref}=[-R_\delta,-\rho_\delta]\),
\begin{equation}
 \Psi_a(r)
 =r+\chi_{\rm tr}\left(\frac{r+R_\delta}{R_\delta}\right)
       \{\zeta_r(a)+R_\delta\}.                \label{eq:moving-r-terminal-trivialization}
\end{equation}
Then \(\Psi_a\) is an orientation-preserving \(C^4\) diffeomorphism from
\(I_{r,\delta}^{\rm ref}\) onto
\([\zeta_r(a),-\rho_\delta]\), satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \Psi_a(-R_\delta)=\zeta_r(a),
 \qquad
 \Psi_a(r)=r\quad
 \left(r\ge-\frac34R_\delta\right),
 \qquad
 \frac12\le\Psi_a'(r)\le\frac32,               \label{eq:moving-r-terminal-trivialization-properties}
\end{equation}
and has, for \(1\le j\le4\), the uniform parameter bounds
\begin{equation}
 \|D^j(\Psi_a-\Id)[h_1,\ldots,h_j]\|_
      {C^4_{R_\delta}(I_{r,\delta}^{\rm ref})}
 \le C_jR_\delta
       \prod_{k=1}^j\|h_k\|_{\phi,\rm rel}^{(4)}.          \label{eq:moving-r-terminal-trivialization-jets}
\end{equation}

For every Banach space \(X\), the pullback
\begin{equation}
 (a,f)\longmapsto f\circ\Psi_a                  \label{eq:moving-r-terminal-composition-map}
\end{equation}
is four times Fr\'{e}chet differentiable from the product of the phase
ball and the scaled \(C^8\) ball on the fixed enlargement
\begin{equation}
 J_{r,\delta}^{\rm tr}=[-3R_\delta/2,-\rho_\delta]          \label{eq:moving-r-terminal-fixed-enlargement}
\end{equation}
into \(C^4_{R_\delta}(I_{r,\delta}^{\rm ref};X)\).  Its
derivatives satisfy the ordinary scaled Fa\`a di Bruno bounds, uniformly
in \(N\), \(\delta\), and the diagonal wedge.

The trivialization is the identity on the entry side of the outer graph.
Thus it moves neither the raw old-history collar nor any fixed finite
method-of-steps seam.  It is a strong-to-weak terminal chart; no
same-space differentiability of translation on a rough history space is
asserted.
\end{lemma}

The preceding endpoint statements deliberately stop before a parameterized
original-endpoint theorem.  A direct replacement of the raw compatible
collar by a parameter-dependent boundary lift would be circular: the
compatible collar also depends on the unknown segment, and the star range
charges its boundary row by \(\lambda_\delta^{-1}\).  Any subsequent
construction must therefore separate two operations.  At a fixed outer
radius, recutting an already existing raw-gauge zero can at most regard the
speed correction at an interior physical section as an observable; it does
not supply the joint-diagonal endpoint family.  The diagonal condition
\(Q_e=0\) must instead be imposed in a moving-(r) boundary-value problem
whose future scalar row is located at that same endpoint; only there does
\cref{prop:endpoint-repelling-row-allocation} apply.  In particular, the
proposition does not justify shooting from a remote raw future row to a
moving interior event.  This order keeps the hard interfaces fixed and
never differentiates a moving characteristic function.

\begin{lemma}[Generated repelling core and logarithmic-square action]
\label[lemma]{lem:repelling-generated-core-action}
Retain
\cref{prop:unprepared-outer-skeleton,thm:short-clock-original-endpoint-segment}
on the repelling branch, fix an integer \(M\ge1\), and put
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr L_\delta=\log(1/\delta)=\frac{S_\delta^2}{2p}.   \label{eq:repelling-core-large-log}
\end{equation}
Denote its exact physical fold-time path by
\(r_*:[0,S_r^{\rm oe}]\to[-R_\delta,-\rho_\delta]\), oriented from
\(-\rho_\delta\) to \(-R_\delta\).  Choose fixed constants
\begin{equation}
 1<c_b,
 \qquad c_c>0,                                   \label{eq:repelling-core-section-constants}
\end{equation}
and put
\begin{equation}
 \rho_{b,\delta}=c_b\rho_\delta,
 \qquad
 \rho_{c,\delta}=c_c\delta\mathscr L_\delta.    \label{eq:repelling-core-section-radii}
\end{equation}
After decreasing \(\delta_*\), the exact repelling endpoint segment has
unique hit times
\begin{equation}
 r_*(s_b)=-\rho_{b,\delta},
 \qquad
 r_*(s_c)=-\rho_{c,\delta},
 \qquad
 (M+2)\theta_m<s_b<s_c<S_r^{\rm oe}-\theta_m.   \label{eq:repelling-generated-core-hits}
\end{equation}
In particular, every delay window based at
\(s\in[s_b,S_r^{\rm oe}]\) is generated by the same forward RFDE segment; no raw
old-history value enters it.

Fix \(0<\vartheta_*<\vartheta\).  On
\([s_b-\theta_m,S_r^{\rm oe}]\) define
\begin{align}
 \kappa_*(s)
 &=\vartheta_*
   \left\{
    \frac{\delta}{\mathfrak a_N(r_*(s))}
       +\frac{\theta_m}{\mathscr L_\delta}
   \right\}^{-1},\notag\\
 \mathscr A_*(s,t)
 &=\int_t^s\kappa_*(u)\,\dd u,
 \qquad s\ge t,                                 \label{eq:repelling-core-moving-action}\\
 \|\phi\|_{s,\sharp}
 &=\sup_{-\theta_m\le\theta\le0}
    e^{-2\mathscr A_*(s,s+\theta)}
       \|\phi(\theta)\|_N.                     \label{eq:repelling-core-moving-history-norm}
\end{align}
If \(\theta_m=0\), the second term in braces and the history weights are
deleted.  Otherwise, uniformly in \(N\) and the retained diagonal wedge,
\begin{align}
 \|\phi\|_{s,\sharp}
 &\le\|\phi\|_{\infty,N}
 \le\delta^{-2\vartheta_*}\|\phi\|_{s,\sharp},
 &&s_b\le s\le S_r^{\rm oe},                    \label{eq:repelling-core-history-norm-comparison}\\
 \mathscr A_*(s_c,s_b)
 &\ge c_{bc}\mathscr L_\delta^2,
 &\mathscr A_*(S_r^{\rm oe},s_b)&\ge c_{bc}\mathscr L_\delta^2.  \label{eq:repelling-core-action-lower-bound}
\end{align}
Consequently, for every \(P>0\),
\begin{equation}
 \delta^{-M_0}e^{c_0S_\delta^3}
 e^{-c_1\mathscr A_*(S_r^{\rm oe},s_b)}
 \le C_{P,M_0,c_0,c_1}\delta^P                 \label{eq:repelling-core-action-flat-envelope}
\end{equation}
for arbitrary fixed \(M_0,c_0,c_1>0\).

This lemma supplies geometry and a moving norm, not a dichotomy.  It does
not invert the RFDE semiflow, construct a nonautonomous stable bundle, or
turn the repelling reference segment into a past-complete orbit.  Those
steps require the exact resource/phase quotient and a forward entry seam.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Generated-core quotient coefficient ledger]
\label[lemma]{lem:repelling-generated-core-coefficient-ledger}
Retain
\cref{prop:unprepared-outer-skeleton,%
prop:exact-resource-gauge-quotient,%
thm:short-clock-original-endpoint-segment,%
lem:repelling-generated-core-action}
for the shared-resource law.  Put
\begin{equation}
 I_*=[s_b,S_*],\qquad
 I_*^-=[s_b-\theta_m,S_*],\qquad
 a_*(s)=\mathfrak a_N(r_*(s)),\qquad
 b=\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}.              \label{eq:generated-core-ledger-intervals}
\end{equation}
The monotone exact segment, restricted to \(I_*^-\), has an
\(r\)-graph representation
\[
 \mathcal H_*(r)=(V_*(r)_\bullet,w_*(r)),
 \qquad q_*(r)=\delta^{-1}r_*'(s_*(r)),
\]
which satisfies the exact outer graph equations.  The nonvanishing
resource derivative proved below then makes
\cref{prop:exact-resource-gauge-quotient} applicable.  Uniformly on the
retained joint wedge,
\begin{align}
 c_a|r_*(s)|\le a_*(s)&\le C_a|r_*(s)|,\notag\\
 -q_+\le q_*(r_*(s))&\le-q_-<0,\notag\\
 \frac12|r_*(s)|\le |r_*(s+\theta)|
 &\le2|r_*(s)|,
 &&\substack{s\in I_*,\ -\theta_m\le\theta\le0,\\
             s+\theta\in I_*^-},               \label{eq:generated-core-local-geometry}\\
 \sup_{I_*^-}\|V_*'(r_*(s))\|_N&\le C,\notag\\
 c_w|r_*(s)|\le |w_*'(r_*(s))|
 &\le C_w|r_*(s)|,
 &&s\in I_*^-.                                  \label{eq:generated-core-graph-derivatives}
\end{align}
The \(q_*\)-line in
\cref{eq:generated-core-local-geometry} holds on all of \(I_*^-\);
the \(a_*\)-comparison is used on \(I_*\).

Let \(\mathcal J_*(s)\), \(H_s\), \(h_s\), and \(\mathcal K_s\) be the
exact quotient coefficients in
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-current-matrix,%
eq:resource-gauge-h,eq:resource-gauge-volterra}.
Then, for \(s\in I_*\),
\begin{align}
 \|\mathcal J_*(s)
       -\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(r_*(s))\|
 &\le C\varepsilon b,                            \label{eq:generated-core-current-difference}\\
 |r_*(s)|\left\{\|H_s\|_N
       +\sup_{-\theta_m\le\theta\le0}
          \|h_s(\theta)\|_N\right\}
 &\le C,                                         \label{eq:generated-core-resource-quotient-coefficients}\\
 \|\mathcal K_s\|_
  {\mathcal L((\mathscr X_N,\|\cdot\|_{s,\sharp}),
              (\mathscr X_N,\|\cdot\|_{s,\sharp}))}
 &\le C\frac{\delta}{|r_*(s)|}
 \le C\frac{\delta}{a_*(s)}.                    \label{eq:generated-core-moving-volterra}
\end{align}

There is a dimension-uniform \(C^2\) current spectral frame
\begin{equation}
 T_N(r):E_N\times\mathbb R\longrightarrow\mathbb R^N,
 \qquad
 T_N(r)^{-1}\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(r)T_N(r)
 =\begin{pmatrix}S_N^s(r)&0\\0&\lambda_{c,N}(r)\end{pmatrix}, \label{eq:generated-core-current-frame}
\end{equation}
such that \(T_N,T_N^{-1},\partial_rT_N\) are uniformly bounded,
\[
 \|e^{S_N^s(r)t}\|\le Ce^{-D\gamma t/2},
 \qquad
 c_\lambda|r|\le\lambda_{c,N}(r)\le C_\lambda|r|
 \quad(r<0),
                                                               \label{eq:generated-core-current-block-rates}
\]
and, along the generated path,
\begin{equation}
 \eta_{\rm fr}(\delta)
 :=\sup_{s\in I_*}
   \frac{\delta}{a_*(s)}
   \|T_N(r_*(s))^{-1}\partial_sT_N(r_*(s))\|
 \le C\frac{\delta}{S_\delta}=o(1).             \label{eq:generated-core-frame-number}
\end{equation}

Finally, set
\begin{align}
 \eta_{\rm core}(\delta)
 :=\sup_{s\in I_*}\biggl\{
 &\frac{\varepsilon b}{a_*(s)}
 +\frac{\delta^2}{a_*(s)}
       (1+\delta^{-2\vartheta_*})
 +\frac{\delta^3}{a_*(s)^2}\delta^{-2\vartheta_*}
 +\frac{\delta^2}{a_*(s)^2}\biggr\}.             \label{eq:generated-core-contraction-number}
\end{align}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \eta_{\rm core}(\delta)+\eta_{\rm fr}(\delta)
 \le C\left\{
    \frac{\delta b}{S_\delta}
   +\frac{\delta^{1-2\vartheta_*}}{S_\delta}
   +\frac{\delta^{1-2\vartheta_*}}{S_\delta^2}
   +\frac1{S_\delta^2}
   +\frac{\delta}{S_\delta}\right\}
 =o(1).                                          \label{eq:generated-core-contraction-closes}
\end{equation}
The lemma collects the exact coefficients and the smallness budget.  It
does not itself construct a stable or unstable RFDE bundle, a mixed Green
inverse, or a physical speed-row replacement.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Action-weighted normal dichotomy on the generated repelling core]
\label[theorem]{thm:repelling-generated-core-normal-dichotomy}
Retain
\cref{prop:frozen-voltage-history-splitting,%
prop:exact-resource-gauge-quotient,%
lem:repelling-generated-core-action,%
lem:repelling-generated-core-coefficient-ledger}.
Write the exact standard quotient RFDE on \(I_*=[s_b,S_*]\) as
\begin{equation}
 u'(s)=\delta^{-1}\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(r_*(s))u(s)
       +\mathcal R_*(s)u_s+f(s),                \label{eq:generated-core-quotient-forced}
\end{equation}
where, exactly,
\begin{align}
 \mathcal R_*(s)\phi
={}&\delta^{-1}
  \{\mathcal J_*(s)-\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(r_*(s))\}\phi(0)\notag\\
 &+\delta K\sum_{k=0}^mL_{k,N}(\eta)
 \left[\phi(0)-\phi(-\theta_k)
       +(\mathcal K_s\phi)(-\theta_k)\right]
 -\delta H_s\pi_N^T\phi(0).                    \label{eq:generated-core-quotient-remainder}
\end{align}

At \(s_b\), put
\[
 e_b^u=e_{c,N}(r_*(s_b)),\qquad
 \ell_b^u=\ell_{c,N}(r_*(s_b)),
\]
and define the bounded history embedding and graph coordinate
\begin{align}
 (\jmath_b^ua)(\theta)&=e_b^ua,
 &&-\theta_m\le\theta\le0,\notag\\
 \mathscr K_b
 &=\{\psi\in\mathscr X_N:\ell_b^u\psi(0)=0\},\notag\\
 \Gamma_b\phi
 &=\phi-\jmath_b^u\ell_b^u\phi(0)\in\mathscr K_b.           \label{eq:generated-core-entry-graph-coordinate}
\end{align}
Here \(\jmath_b^u\) is only a bounded right inverse for the current
boundary coordinate; it is not asserted to be an invariant unstable RFDE
history.  Thus a full entry history is prescribed modulo exactly one current
unstable coordinate, not together with an additional copy of that
coordinate.  At the true terminal point put
\[
 \ell_S^u=\ell_{c,N}(-R_\delta).
\]

Let \(\mathcal F_*\) be the space of measurable sources with
\begin{equation}
 \|f\|_{\mathcal F_*}
 =\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{s\in I_*}
       \frac{\delta}{a_*(s)}\|f(s)\|_N<\infty,   \label{eq:generated-core-source-norm}
\end{equation}
and let \(\mathscr D_*\) consist of
\(u\in C([s_b-\theta_m,S_*];\mathbb R^N)\), locally
\(W^{1,\infty}\) on \(I_*\), for which
\[
 u'-\delta^{-1}\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(r_*)u
       -\mathcal R_*u_\bullet\in\mathcal F_*.
\]
Put
\begin{equation}
 \|u\|_{\mathcal X_*}
 =\sup_{s_b\le s\le S_*}\|u_s\|_{s,\sharp}.     \label{eq:generated-core-solution-norm}
\end{equation}
Equip \(\mathscr D_*\) with the graph norm
\[
 \|u\|_{\mathscr D_*}
 =\|u\|_{\mathcal X_*}
 +\left\|u'-\delta^{-1}\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(r_*)u
       -\mathcal R_*u_\bullet\right\|_{\mathcal F_*}.
\]
Then, after decreasing the same joint wedge, the mixed operator
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb L_*u
 =\left(
 u'-\delta^{-1}\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(r_*)u
       -\mathcal R_*u_\bullet,\,
 \Gamma_bu_{s_b},\,
 \ell_S^uu(S_*)\right)                           \label{eq:generated-core-mixed-operator}
\end{equation}
is a dimension- and diagonal-uniform isomorphism
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb L_*:\mathscr D_*
 \longrightarrow\mathcal F_*\times\mathscr K_b\times\mathbb R,
                                                               \label{eq:generated-core-mixed-isomorphism}
\end{equation}
and its inverse satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \|u\|_{\mathcal X_*}
 \le C\left\{\|f\|_{\mathcal F_*}
       +\|\psi_b\|_{s_b,\sharp}+|b_u|\right\}.    \label{eq:generated-core-mixed-green-bound}
\end{equation}
The same constant works on every subinterval
\([t_0,t_1]\subset I_*\).  More precisely, with
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 e_{t_0}^u&=e_{c,N}(r_*(t_0)),&
 \ell_{t_0}^u&=\ell_{c,N}(r_*(t_0)),\\
 (\jmath_{t_0}^ua)(\theta)&=e_{t_0}^ua,
 &&-\theta_m\le\theta\le0,\\
 \mathscr K_{t_0}
 &=\{\psi\in\mathscr X_N:\ell_{t_0}^u\psi(0)=0\},\\
 \Gamma_{t_0}\phi
 &=\phi-\jmath_{t_0}^u\ell_{t_0}^u\phi(0)
 \in\mathscr K_{t_0}.
\end{aligned}                                    \label{eq:generated-core-subinterval-entry-coordinate}
\end{equation}
the same estimate holds with entry coordinate \(\Gamma_{t_0}\) and
terminal current covector \(\ell_{c,N}(r_*(t_1))\).
